# Database Schema — Cavnar AI

**Two copies of a week (schedule audit 10/10/26 ARCH-1, BUGSLIFE-3).** `schedule_csv` is the WORKING copy — the Studio's draft; every save lands here, and on a week staff already have it carries the manager's unsent changes until Send. `published_csv` (boot column; `schedule_versions.backfill_published_copies` filled it at boot for every week published before, from what each person was last told) is the PUBLISHED copy — exactly what staff were sent (`schedule_publish` stamps it with `published_at` in one transaction) plus the covers and swaps the requests flow told them about (`schedule_versions.write_on` with `LIVE_REASON`, which carries the change into the working copy shift by shift). Every staff and operational reader reads only the published copy: the portal and the app (`staff_schedule`, `models.get_published_week_csv`), the `/s/` link (`get_schedule_share`), drops, swaps and claims (`shift_requests`), time-off conflicts, intraday no-show / coverage / pulse, attendance, task sheets, reminders and every learner's "week that ran". **The week in force has one definition** (ARCH-11): a week's LIVE copy is its newest published copy (by `COALESCE(republished_at, published_at)`, then id) — `models.live_where` / `live_csv_sql` in SQL, `live_week_row` / `live_weeks` / `live_week_for` / `live_week_rows` in Python; a published copy is replaced only by a newer published one, so a generation's supersede of a copy mid-Send can no longer hide the week staff have (BUGSLIFE-2). A week's WORKING copy is its newest row nothing superseded (`models.week_draft`, `newest_draft`; `schedule_versions.draft_rows`). A generation and the quality gate's restore never supersede a copy a Send holds (a `publishing_at` claim younger than `PUBLISH_CLAIM_MINUTES`); a Send that ends without publishing lets the newer draft supersede it.

**Rows (ARCH-6, ARCH-31, SCALE-31, contract #4).** Both copies and every `schedule_versions` row are one format, `schedule_rows`: the eight columns (`date, day, employee, role, shift_start, shift_end, scheduled_hours, notes`) then `shift_id` (uuid4 hex; a shift keeps it through edits, versions, swaps, covers and a regeneration of the week — `schedule_rows.carry_ids`: its own id, else the prior row with its key, its slot, or its person and date; a new shift gets a new one), `person_id` (`people.id` of the one live person the name means, `""` when none or two — `schedule_versions.stamp_people`) and `locked` (`"1"`: the owner pinned it — a row the manager changed in a save locks by default, `schedule_versions.apply_locks`; every pass, the solver and a regenerate keep it exactly). Written by `csv.writer` (quoted: a comma or quote in a note survives; a line break becomes a space). Text written before 10/10/26 is the eight columns, read the same way; its ids arrive on the next write.

### `schedule_notices` (the publish outbox)
One row per person a Send, a changes send or a resend tells (`schedule_publish`, schedule audit 10/10/26 ARCH-19, ARCH-20, BUGSLIFE-7, BUGSLIFE-9): `restaurant_id`, `history_id`, `week_start`, `batch` (one event; UNIQUE `batch, employee_name`), `employee_name`, `person_id`, `kind` (`posted` — new to the week; `changed`; `removed` — taken off a week they had), `status` (`pending` → `sending` → `sent` | `unreachable` | `failed`; `cancelled` when a newer notice about the same week replaced it before it went), `channel` / `sent_to` / `delivery` / `shifts` (what reached them), `attempts`, `last_error`, `next_attempt_at` (a failure is retried after 5, 30 and 120 minutes, then left failed for the owner's Send again), `claimed_at` (a delivery claim older than 10 minutes is taken over), `requested_by`, `created_at`, `sent_at`. Written in the stamp's own transaction (one statement for the cancels and one for the inserts — scale audit 10/19/26 A5-F14); delivered after it — inline for 8 seconds, the rest from a bulk `schedule_notices` job_queue row per batch (`schedule_publish.queue_delivery` / `run_queued_notices`, 120 s a round, queued again while some remain) and the minute duty `schedule_notices` (`run_schedule_notices`, which also takes the retries); claimed 10 at a time in one write, each claim with its own token, a chunk the bound cut short handed back as it was (`_claim_notices` / `_unclaim`). A person counts as told only on `sent` — and a push only once Apple answered (schedule re-audit 10/11/26 BUGSLIFE-3): a queued push is `sent` with `delivery` `pending`; the chain's final word is written onto the row (`schedule_publish._notice_outcome`) — `delivery` `delivered`, the text or email the fallback sent instead, or `failed` (retried on the backoff) when it reached nobody. Who has a week is read across every copy of it staff were sent (`week_copy_ids`, BUGSLIFE-8). Created at boot (`schedule_publish.init_schedule_notices`, from `init_db`); indexed on `(status, next_attempt_at)`, `(restaurant_id, history_id)` and `created_at`; retention 180 days (floor 60).

Single SQLite database (`reviews.db`), WAL mode, on a Railway persistent volume in production. Schema lives across `models.py` (`init_db()` + `ensure_columns()` + a dozen `init_*()` helpers for tables that didn't exist from day one), `auth.py` (`init_auth()`), `push.py`, `webhooks.py`, `guest_marketing.py`, `sales_audits.py`, `ops.py` (`init_ops`), `ai_utils.py` (`init_ai_ops`), `billing_jobs.py` (`init_billing`), `admin_events.py` (`init_admin_events`), `admin_ops.py` (`init_admin_ops`), `offboarding.py` (`init_offboarding`) — all called from `init_db` — plus `platform_monitor.init_platform_tables` and `provider_health.init_provider_health`, which `hosted_dashboard.py`'s boot block calls, and a few single-table owners (`milestones`, `home_brief`). All of it runs at boot. There is no schema version table — schema migrations are additive `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` statements guarded by `try/except`. One-time DATA migrations are recorded by name in `data_migrations` so they never run twice (the first, 9/29/26: `email_log.sent_at` from Chicago wall time to UTC). **257 table names** after the memory round (9/29/26; 212 before it); the count is the `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` names across non-test Python (`python3 scripts/repo_inventory.py`), and this file lists them by area rather than re-deriving each one. The tables the admin-console fix round added (9/29/26) are in *Operations, audit and billing ledgers* below; the memory round's 45 are in the area each belongs to (*People and shifts*, *Ask Cavnar*, *Recommendations*, *AI reads*, *Events and public history*, *The change log and what outlives a prune*, *Intelligence engine* and the rest); *Retention* says how long each ledger is kept, and which are kept forever.

`restaurant_id` is the tenant boundary on essentially every table below it — every read must filter by it from the authenticated session, never from client-supplied input.

## Core identity

### `restaurants` (models.py)
The tenant row (243 columns on 9/29/26). Everything else hangs off `restaurants.id`. The list below is by purpose, not exhaustive; the authoritative column set is the four touch points — the `Restaurant` dataclass, the migration lists (`init_db()`'s ALTERs and `ensure_columns()`), `update_restaurant().allowed`, and `get_restaurant()`'s hydration — and a column missing from any one of them is a bug, not a documentation gap. Not itemised here: the per-type alert channel matrix (`alert_*`, `al_<type>_email|sms|push`, `alert_quiet_*`, `alert_max_per_day`), every POS/OAuth credential column (`toast_*`, `square_*`, `clover_*`, `rpower_*`, `gmb_*`, `ig_*`/`fb_*` — encrypted at rest by `credentials.py`), the 2FA columns, the automation switches (`auto_*`, `send_delay_minutes`, `weekly_plan_enabled`), `category` (the intelligence cohort), `organization_id`, `row_version`, `paused_until`, `deletion_requested_at`.
- **Identity/contact**: `name`, `owner_email`, `owner_name`, `owner_phone`, `google_place_id`, `yelp_business_id`
- **Brand voice**: `voice_notes`, `neighborhood`, `vibe`, `known_for`, `sign_off_name`, `never_say`
- **Revenue target**: `monthly_revenue_target` is the one stored figure (0 = none). A weekly figure (`weekly_revenue_target`, accepted by `models.update_restaurant` and every settings route) is stored as weekly × `metrics.WEEKS_PER_MONTH` (52/12) and read back with `models.weekly_revenue_target(r)`, to the cent — there is no weekly column (9/28/26).
- **Labor settings**: `hourly_rate`, `labor_target_pct`, `week_start_day` (0=Mon, for FLSA overtime on the employer's own workweek — and since 10/10/26 the schedule week too: a draft covers the seven days from it, the Studio's header, the Crew grid, week-only note rules, Ask's week and the auto-draft all read it (`schedule_engine._week_monday(start_day=)`, `week_day_order`); Simple EJ's is 2, Wednesday to Tuesday; the auto-draft day may be any day but the week's eve, `models.auto_draft_days`), scheduling JSON blobs (`role_rates_json`, `close_times_json`, `role_close_buffer_json`, `role_minimums_json`, `role_strength_json`, `shift_leader_rules_json`, `quality_weights_json`), `quality_tuning_json` (schedule audit 10/3/26 SQ-22, `models.get_quality_tuning`): `{profile key: {min_quality, floors: {dimension: floor}}}` — the floors and bar an applied calibration set on a BUILT-IN shift profile (`schedule_learning.apply_profile_calibration`; a profile the restaurant configured takes them in its own `shift_profiles` config instead), laid over the built-ins by `shift_quality.profiles_from_config(tuning=)` and kept when a built-in's demand level moves; part of `models.capability_version`. `sched_notes`; `kitchen_stations_json` (9/30/26, `kitchen_stations.normalise`): `{roles, stations, needs: [{station, daypart: morning|night|all, days, count}], skills: {person: [stations]}}` — the kitchen's stations, which each daypart needs and who is trained on each; edited one change at a time (`kitchen_stations.apply_edit`, `POST /labor/rules {station_edit}`), a scheduling setting in `change_log`. `person_rates_json` (`{"Kailey Gordon": 17.5}`, 9/30/26 — `models.person_rates`): one person's own hourly rate where it differs from their role's; a shift costs the POS's pay for the punch first, then this, then the person's pay on their other punches (`labor.rate_book`: RPOWER sends $0 on many PM and bartender punches), then the role's rate, then the median of the role's people, then the blended rate (`labor._shift_rate`; a drafted week is priced the same way through `labor.person_rate_book` → `schedule_economics.priced_cost`); a pay field (`PAY_FIELDS`), so a change re-costs the history. `salaried_staff_json` (`[{name, annual}]`, owner, 9/28/26 — `models.salaried_staff`): salaried people are costed by salary, never by the hour — `labor._analyse_for_restaurant` drops their punches from hourly labor (and so from `labor_daily_history`), and `labor.salaried_summary` / `dsr.access._live_salaries` put annual ÷ 52 ÷ trading days a week (`models.open_days_per_week`, from `open_times_json`/`close_times_json`, 7 when unset) on each day with sales, **inside** the owner's labor % since 9/30/26 (the target judges the all-in figure; `labor_daily_history` stays hourly and the share is added on read), owner-only (`dsr.access.OWNER_ONLY_PREFIXES` carries `salaried`; `models.viewer_sees_salaries`). The schedule's hours budget judges hourly labor only — against the all-in target LESS the salaried staff's share of the drafted week (`models.salaried_week_share`: the share on each of the week's trading days; schedule audit 10/3/26 D-1), bought at the measured wage (`labor.labor_rate_basis`: the chain above, hours-weighted; D-2). `labor_standards_json` (10/3/26, D-25 — `labor_standards.overrides`): `{family: {"all"|"morning"|"night": work per hour}}`, the owner's own labor standard for a role family (guests per server-hour, bar tickets per bartender-hour, tickets per cook-hour), standing over the measured one; saved one family at a time (`POST labor/labor-standards`); an owner's standard resizes that role's SHIFT REQUIREMENTS number, with its reason. `daypart_boundary` (TEXT, 10/10/26, schedule audit ARCH-12): where lunch ends and dinner starts for a shift covering neither core service window ("4:00pm"; NULL is 3:00pm) — `schedule_time.daypart_scope`, set on Labor → Scheduling → Advanced or the admin settings.
- **Salaried entries are linked to people** (schedule audit 10/3/26 D-7): a `salaried_staff_json` entry saved through Account → Targets is stored under the spelling of the one person its name means (an alias counts) with that person's `person_id`; `models.salaried_keys` gives every spelling of every salaried person (the hourly analysis, the rules and pay rates match through it), and `models.salaried_matches` names an entry that matches nobody on the roster, with a similar roster name to check (`salaried_warnings` on the targets payload). `person_rates_json` entries are matched through the same identity (`models.person_rates`).
- **Location grouping**: `location_group`, `location_name` (multi-location clients)
- **Food cost**: `inventory_frequency`, `delivery_days`, `inventory_notes`, `food_cost_target`, `plate_margin_target_pct` (10/16/26: the margin % of its price at or above which a dish's margin reads green on Plate costs; NULL = 100 minus the food cost target `cogs.dish_reference` judges dishes by — `backoffice_recipes.margin_target`; Account → Targets, 30–95, clearable), `bar_cost_target` / `beer_cost_target` / `liquor_cost_target` / `wine_cost_target` (10/14/26: the owner's own alcohol cost targets, a percent, NULL = not set — the Bar page judges against the industry range then; Account → Targets, clearable. Since 10/15/26 `models.update_restaurant` refuses anything but NULL or 5–60 ('' clears), records who set a changed one in `target_setters_json` as for labor and food, and every reader goes through `thresholds.bar_target_for`), `comp_void_target_pct` (10/15/26: the most comps and voided lines may be, % of gross item sales — discounts not counted; NULL = not set, 0.1–25 on Account → Targets, clearable; judged on the Reports night's Given away card, `dsr.block_service.comp_void`), `inventory_updated_at` (never written by anything — every freshness reading uses `MAX(ingredients.last_recount_at)` instead, as `ordering.count_freshness` does. Candidate for future cleanup after additional verification; do not delete.)
- **Billing/contract**: `stripe_customer_id`, `docusign_envelope_id`, `contract_status` (pending / sent / delivered / signed / declined / voided — the last three from DocuSign Connect, written only for the restaurant's CURRENT envelope and never over `signed`, `webhook_routes._docusign_state`), `contract_signed_at` (UTC, set once when the envelope completes), `billing_status` (trial / active / past_due / paused / churned / canceled / internal — `models.in_service` is false for paused, churned and canceled; `models.is_paying` is true for active and past_due only), `pause_reason` (self / dispute / refund / admin, `models.PAUSE_REASONS`: a dispute holds the whole group, a full refund only the location that paid; `models.pause_lock` and `billing_hold` read it — only a `self` pause is the owner's to resume, the rest only an admin's lift-hold clears, and a hold survives churn; a NULL reason on a paused row with no `paused_until` reads as an admin lock), `paused_until`, `converted_at` (UTC, set once at the first paid checkout, else the first paid invoice with money in it). `contract_signed_at` and `converted_at` were backfilled at boot from the event ledger (`billing_jobs._backfill_lifecycle_stamps`, NULLs only). Every change to `billing_status`, `pause_reason`, `paused_until`, `contract_status`, `stripe_customer_id`, `service_tier` or a `module_*` flag writes a `billing_status_history` row inside `update_restaurant`'s own transaction (`models.BILLING_HISTORY_FIELDS`), attributed by `models.billing_context` (else the request's endpoint and signed-in user, else `system`)
- **Access control**: `service_tier` (trial/starter_reviews/starter_labor/starter_inventory/starter_marketing/full) auto-sets `module_reviews`/`module_labor`/`module_inventory`/`module_marketing` booleans, which can be overridden per client
- **Integrations**: `gmb_revoked_at` (UTC, stamped by `gmb.get_valid_token` when Google revokes the grant; the console raises it as a failing integration)
- **Ops**: `is_demo` (seed data may be reset — never true for a real client; also the only thing that lets "demo" Toast credentials load the synthetic feed, `toast.demo_allowed`), `demo_cleared_at` (UTC, stamped when admin turns `is_demo` off, with `learning_since`: the seeded rows stay — never hard-deleted, the seed's daily labor rows marked `source='seed'` — and nothing recorded before `learning_since` teaches any learner, its own or the platform's — `models.learning_eligible(r, since=)` / `learning_rows_sql`), `internal_notes` (Will-only), `pos_system`, `digest_day`/`digest_enabled`, `last_fetched_at`, `last_active_tab` (a short lowercase tab name or "other", never the ping's raw text — SEC-04, 10/16/26), `last_activity`
- **Misc computed/cached**: `latitude`/`longitude`/`weather_cache_json`/`weather_cached_at` (geocoded once, cached forecast), `weather_hourly_json` (the NWS hourly forecast fetched with the periods — `{startTime, temperature, probabilityOfPrecipitation, shortForecast}` per hour, aged by `weather_cached_at`, NULL when the last refresh brought none; each meal's own weather for the schedule — `weather.meal_weather`, scheduler audit 10/10/26 LEARNING-21), `aivis_city_json` (the city Google has for the Place ID, as the AI-visibility check asks about it — `{"place_id", "city", "at"}`, re-read when the Place ID changes or after 90 days; AI cost audit 10/7/26 #46), `email_theme`, `marketing_emails_opt_out`, `timezone`

### `users` / `sessions` / `login_history` (auth.py)
- `users`: one row per login, `restaurant_id` FK, `username`/`email` unique, `password_hash`, `is_admin`, `is_active`, `role` ∈ `permissions.py`'s roles — `owner` (multi-location), `client` (the primary login and DB default; shown as Co-owner), `manager`, `member` (Teammate), `employee` (PIN identity, staff portal only), `support` (Cavnar staff, read-only admin). `two_fa_enabled` / `two_fa_method` (email | sms; fix round 9/29/26 — | app, an authenticator app, R10 9/29/26: its secret is in `user_totp`, never on this row) are an INTERNAL login's own second factor (admin and support): the restaurant-wide 2FA switch never applies to one, and it never uses the restaurant's backup codes (`user_backup_codes` below). `create_user` enforces the password policy (8 or more characters, not in a known breach) unless the secret is generated (`generated=True`).
- `sessions`: `token` PK (the SHA-256 of the bearer), `expires_at` and `last_active` in SQLite's `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` UTC form and compared through `datetime()` — rows written in ISO form (`…T…+00:00`) outlived their expiry to the end of that UTC day as text, and `auth.normalize_session_rows` rewrites them at boot; the nightly prune compares `expires_at` as text too, so it must stay in this form. `ip_address`, `user_agent`, `device_type` (`web`, `ios`, `staff_pin`, `admin-view-as`), `active_restaurant_id`, and since 9/29/26 `two_factor_at` (this session passed a second factor), `reauth_at` (the last password step-up, read by `auth.recent_auth_required` — 15 minutes), `acting_admin_id` (on a view-as session, the admin behind it) and `read_only` (a view-as a support login opened; on the row, so it fails closed). Lifetimes: 30 days absolute with an 8-hour inactivity rule on the web (iOS and staff PIN sessions are exempt from the inactivity rule); staff PIN sessions 14 hours; an internal login's session 12 hours absolute (`auth.ADMIN_SESSION_HOURS`, old rows capped at boot); a view-as session 2 hours absolute from when it was opened, never extended (`auth.VIEW_AS_HOURS`). Hard-deleted on revoke and device-dedup; an expired row goes a day after `expires_at` (`ops._RETENTION_DAYS`).
- `login_history`: append-only (a row is never edited) — independent of `sessions`, this is what the Account "sign-in activity" view reads, so a login stays visible regardless of what later happens to the session it produced. Kept 90 days, pruned nightly by the retention registry (`login_history` on `created_at`, floor 60) and at every boot by `auth.prune_login_history`, which reads the SAME window from the registry (`ops.retention_days`) — never a number of its own — and prunes nothing when that window is off or refused. Before rows go, each login's month is summarised into `engagement_monthly` (`history_rollups.roll_engagement`). `idx_login_history_created` (`auth.AUTH_INDEXES`, made at boot with the table) serves both deletes — `idx_login_history_user` leads with `user_id` and serves the Account view.
- `trusted_devices`: "remember this device 30 days" for 2FA, one row per device (superseded a single-column approach that only held the last device).
- `two_fa_backup_codes`: hashed, single-use fallback codes generated at 2FA setup.
- `admin_request_hits` (models.py, used by `security.admin_request_allowed`): the /admin request ceiling's counts — per session key (a prefix of the session token's hash) and minute (`win`, epoch minutes): `requests`, `writes`. A sliding-window counter (this minute plus the last, weighted), shared by every process (blind re-audit 10/11/26 ARCH-26); rows older than two minutes are deleted by each count.
- `login_attempts` (models.py, used by `security.py`): durable login throttling. `kind` is `account` (a failure against a username), `ip`, `anon` (an attempt naming no account), `account_ip` (an account and address pair — internal logins are locked per address, so a stranger's guesses cannot lock the admin out from everywhere) and `reauth` (a wrong step-up password: five end the session). Rows older than two days are pruned daily (`scheduler.run_prune_login_attempts`).
- `user_backup_codes` (auth.py, 9/29/26): an internal login's own hashed, single-use backup codes (`user_id`, `code_hash`, `used_at`). A restaurant's `two_fa_backup_codes` never pass an admin and his never pass them.
- `user_passkeys` (auth.py `AUTH_SCHEMA`, owner, 9/30/26 — `passkeys.py`): one row per passkey a login added in Account → Security — `user_id`, `credential_id` (the authenticator's id, base64url, unique), `public_key` (the COSE public key it signs with — public, so kept in off-site backups: `KEEP_COLUMNS`), `sign_count` (only ever rises; a lower one is a cloned key and refused), `transports`, `aaguid`, `backed_up`, `name` (the device kind it was added on), `created_at`, `last_used_at`. Removed one at a time by its login. Bounded at `passkeys.MAX_PASSKEYS_PER_LOGIN` (10) per login, so no retention entry.
- `passkey_challenges` (auth.py `AUTH_SCHEMA`, 9/30/26): one WebAuthn challenge per ceremony — `challenge` (PK, base64url), `purpose` (`register` | `login`), `user_id` (a registration's login; NULL for a sign-in, where the passkey names the login), `created_at`. Single use (deleted when answered), and every row older than `passkeys.PASSKEY_CHALLENGE_MINUTES` (5) is deleted whenever a new one is issued.
- `user_totp` (auth.py `AUTH_SCHEMA`, R10 9/29/26): an internal login's authenticator-app (TOTP, RFC 6238) state, one row per login at most — `user_id` (PK), `secret` (the active secret), `pending_secret` + `pending_expires_at` (the secret a QR code was just shown for; dead after 15 minutes, active only once a code from the app confirms it), `last_step` (the last 30-second step a code was accepted for — each step passes once), `activated_at`, `updated_at`. Both secrets are Fernet ciphertext under `CREDENTIAL_KEY` (`enc:v1:`; enrolment is refused without a working key, and a value that is not ciphertext is never trusted). Its own table so the secret never rides on the `users` row every session lookup loads. Emptied by `auth.clear_user_two_factor` (disable, admin reset, break-glass) and `auth.clear_user_totp` (a switch back to email or SMS); emptied from every off-site backup (`SCRUB_TABLES`). Bounded by the number of internal logins, so no retention entry.
- `users.policy_notice_dismissed` (TEXT, 9/29/26): the Privacy Policy / Terms change (`policy_notice.POLICY_UPDATED_ON`, ISO) this login dismissed the 30-day dashboard notice for — per login, so a later change (a new date) shows again. One value per row; kept with the login.
- `organizations` + `restaurants.organization_id`: the owner-level grouping above a location group (backfilled from `location_group` + `owner_email`).
- `preferences` (`preferences.py`, memory audit 9/29/26) — who a setting belongs to. PK (`scope`, `scope_id`, `key`), `value` JSON, `set_by`, `set_at`. `scope` `org` (`scope_id` = the organisation key): a group owner's default for every location, written by `preferences.apply_to_all_locations` (only `preferences.ORG_KEYS` — voice, never-say, briefing, quiet hours, the daily cap and the alert matrix; never auto-approve), which also writes the value onto every location's own `restaurants` column, so a location's setting stays where every reader already reads it; `scope` `login` (`scope_id` = `"<user_id>:<restaurant_id>"`): a login's own notification choices (`preferences.LOGIN_KEYS` — push on/off, muted alert types, their own quiet hours), BOUNDED by the location's — they can only take alerts off their own phone, never turn on a type the owner turned off; `preferences.UNMUTABLE_TYPES` (health, issue, issue_escalated, coverage, critical_low) cannot be muted. `push.fire_push` applies them. Resolution: login → location → organisation → product default. Bounded by its keys, kept until changed. It has no `restaurant_id` column (the scope id carries it), so `delete_restaurant` does not reach its rows.

### Staff portal (auth.py `AUTH_SCHEMA`)
- `memberships`: an employee's identity at a restaurant (name, job role, PIN hash, `pos_id`), the row the staff portal signs in against. `schedule_texts_at` (TEXT, NULL = off) is the employee's own "text me", set only from their unchecked-by-default box in the staff app; the signup consent covers the one-time code alone. What it covers is the login preference `staff_sms_scope` (`preferences`, no DDL): `["schedule","request"]` for a consent given on the current wording (`people.STAFF_SMS_CONSENT_TEXT`, version 2 — a posted or changed week, swaps, open shifts and time off), `["schedule"]` for one given on the old wording. A text goes only when the purpose is in scope and `TWILIO_STAFF_MESSAGING_SERVICE_SID` is configured (`people.reach(purpose=)`, Friction #17, employee audit H14). Employee-app fix round (10/2/26): `unlinked_at` (an owner unlinked this login) and `deleted_at` (the employee deleted their account — `auth.delete_own_staff_account`) record how a login ended, so the roster switch (`staff_settings._sync_portal_access`) never restores it; an owner's explicit reactivation clears both. **One active employee login per name per restaurant**: enforced in `upsert_membership`, `update_membership_details` and `set_membership_active` (`NameTakenError`, 409 `name_taken` on the owner routes) and by the partial unique index `uq_memberships_active_employee_name` on `(restaurant_id, lower(trim(employee_name))) WHERE is_active=1 AND role='employee'`, made at boot (`auth.ensure_active_name_index`) — when existing rows already break it the index is skipped and `ops.capture` names them (`job="memberships_name_index"`); console logins are outside the rule.
- Staff account ledgers (auth.py `AUTH_SCHEMA`, 10/2/26): `staff_pin_resets` — forgot PIN by text, one live reset per phone (`phone` PK, the membership it was found on, `code_hash`, `attempts`, `sends`, `token_hash` once the code is typed back — 15 minutes, single use; rows older than that are deleted as the next reset starts, the used one at once); `staff_otp_sends` — every verification text the staff surface sends, kind, time and `restaurant_id` (the join code's restaurant; NULL for a send that named none — 10/19/26 SEC-04) only, never the number, for the hourly ceilings (`STAFF_OTP_HOURLY_CAP`, each restaurant's `STAFF_OTP_RESTAURANT_HOURLY_CAP`, the anonymous `STAFF_OTP_ANON_HOURLY_CAP`), rows older than a day deleted as new ones are written; `pin_change_attempts` — wrong current PINs on Change PIN, per membership, its own counter (3 misses lock it 15 minutes and end the session) so the next person at a shared tablet cannot lock the owner of the session out of sign-in. `login_history` gains the staff events `pin_reset_by_text` and `account_deleted`; `activity_log` gains `staff_account_deleted` (the owner's Account activity).
- Deleting one's own staff account (`auth.delete_own_staff_account`): the membership is stamped `deleted_at` and deactivated, the PIN cleared, the text consent and claimed phone cleared, the PIN counters deleted, this restaurant's staff sessions ended and the person's `/s/` and calendar links expired (`staff_insights.expire_links_for`); with no other active login the identity closes too (`users.is_active=0`, phone cleared, every session deleted, every staff-tier phone unregistered). The restaurant's own records about the person (shifts, roster, the contacts the owner typed) are not touched. Deactivating or unlinking a login expires those links the same way.
- `membership_pin_attempts`, `portal_attempts`, `portal_nonces`: PIN lockout, portal throttling, one-time nonces. A lockout lasts 15 min × 2^(lockouts in the last day), capped at 24 h (`lockout_count`, `last_locked_at`); `portal_attempts.ok=1` marks a hit that ended well, which counts toward the 300-per-5-min ceiling but not the 30 failures (SEC-18/19).
- `two_fa_challenges`: one row per 2FA sign-in attempt (`purpose='login'`) or per login's "Send test code" (`'setup'`); the code and the pending secret are keyed hashes only, single-use, 10 minutes. `restaurants.two_fa_code/two_fa_pending/two_fa_expires` are no longer read and are blanked at boot (SEC-20/39).
- `view_as_sessions`: who opened each admin view-as session (by session-token hash) and whether it is read-only (opened by support; SEC-12). Since 9/29/26 the session row itself carries `acting_admin_id` and `read_only`, so this table is history only (candidate for future cleanup after additional verification); `ai_utils`' attribution reads `flask.g`'s acting admin first, then the session row's `acting_admin_id`, and `opened_by` only when that is NULL. Its rows older than 2 days are deleted whenever a view-as opens (`auth.record_view_as_session`) — deliberately not in the retention registry (one pruner per table).
- `staff_portal_tokens`: the per-restaurant portal links; `staff_signups`: the claim-your-name flow.
- `permission_grants`, `login_prefs`: see *Strategic foundations* below.

## Reviews

### `reviews` (models.py)
`restaurant_id` + `platform` (google/yelp/csv/manual) + `external_id` — **unique on `(restaurant_id, platform, external_id)`**, deliberately including `restaurant_id` in the key (a global-unique key let two tenants sharing a `google_place_id` silently steal each other's reviews). Carries the raw review (`author`, `rating` 1–5, `text`, `review_date`, `fetched_at`), Claude's analysis (`sentiment`, `categories` JSON, `summary`, `urgency`), and the response workflow (`draft_response`, `response_status`: pending/drafted/approved/posted/skipped, `approved_at`, `posted_at`, `draft_edited`, `regenerate_count`, `deleted_at`). `response_action` is approved_as_is / edited / regenerated / **auto_approved** / **bulk_approved** — `auto_approved` is the auto-approve rule's own approval and `bulk_approved` a publish-many (approve-all, Home's "Publish N replies") of an unedited, unregenerated draft; neither is counted by `auto_approve_trust` or used as a style example, and a regenerated draft counts against trust like an edit. A bulk publish only ever posts `models.BULK_PUBLISHABLE_SQL`: recent (`REPLY_OWED_MAX_AGE_DAYS`), not urgent, not `draft_needs_review`; `update_draft` never overwrites an approved or posted reply. `original_draft` is the model's text as it stood before the owner's first edit (kept, never overwritten; reset by a regenerate). `draft_run_id` (re-audit 10/7/26 #5) is the `ai_runs` id of the `draft_response` run that wrote the stored draft (`update_draft(run_id=)`): the owner's answer and the auto-approve gate's verdict are filed on it, not on the subject's newest run. At a person's approval (never the auto-approve rule's or a bulk publish's) `models.record_reply_edit` compares it with the approved reply (`reply_edits.compare`) and stores `edit_distance` (word edit distance, 0–1), `edit_category` (unchanged / light / heavy / rewrite) and `edit_signals` (JSON, a closed vocabulary: shortened, lengthened, removed/added exclamations, apology, invitation) — read by the drafter's few-shot order (an edited reply first) and its OWNER'S EDITS note. `skipped_at` dates a skip, which counts against the band's trust. **`replied_elsewhere`** (10/2/26) is a review answered outside Cavnar AI — someone replied on Google by hand: `response_status` posted, `posted_at` the reply's own time when Google gave it, `external_reply` / `external_reply_at` the reply Google returned, `external_reply_source` 'google' (read by a Business Profile fetch, `models.apply_google_replies`) or 'owner' (the Replied-on-Google button, `models.mark_replied_elsewhere`). Only a review still waiting (pending, drafted, skipped) takes it; every draft-learning read (style examples, edit summaries, auto-approve trust, the console's reply-adoption read) and value_delivered's `replies_posted` leave it out, and Retract refuses it (Undo, `models.undo_replied_elsewhere`, takes the mark off).

Who approved a reply (memory audit 9/29/26, `ensure_columns`): `approved_by` (the login — for a view-as session, the admin behind it), `approved_role` (`permissions.answer_authority`: principal / delegate / admin, or `rule` for the auto-approve rule) and `approved_via` (normal / view_as / rule), set by the statement that approves it and cleared with it; NULL on replies approved before the capture. `draft_edited_via` is `view_as` once an admin or support login acting through view-as changed the draft since the model wrote it, else `normal`; NULL until a person edits; reset by each fresh model draft. `models.reply_voice_sql` is the one predicate for "approved in the owner's voice": never `approved_via` or `draft_edited_via` `view_as`; the account holder (`approved_role` principal) or a delegate holding the `reviews.voice` grant — and, for auto-approve trust (`trust=True`), the account holder alone; a NULL `approved_role` (before the capture) still counts, behind every known one. The style examples, the OWNER'S EDITS note and auto-approve trust read only through it, banded by star rating (`models.REPLY_BANDS` 1-2 / 3 / 4-5; a short band borrows only from the 3-star band beside it). `idx_reviews_gbp_name` (partial, reviews with a `review_name`) serves `intelligence.provenance.google_connected_ids`.

The Google post of an approved reply (re-audit 10/8/26, `ensure_columns`): `post_attempted_at` (when the last attempt started — claimed by `client_api._claim_post_attempt` as a compare-and-set) and `post_error` (that attempt's owner-facing reason when it failed; NULL while it runs and after a success). An attempt with no error inside `models.REVIEW_POST_IN_FLIGHT_MINUTES` (2) is in flight: `models.review_post_failed` is false for it and no second post starts beside it.

- `reply_draft_rejections` (9/29/26) — a reply draft the owner turned down by asking for another (a regenerate, or Ask rewriting it): `review_id`, `rating`, `draft_hash`, `words`, `signals` (the closed vocabulary of `reply_edits.draft_signals`), `how`, `user_id`, `authority`. **Never the draft's text.** Kept 90 days (registry, floor 90) for the drafter's edit note (`models.get_reply_rejection_signals`).
- `review_retags` (9/29/26) — an owner's or manager's correction to how a review was tagged (`field` category / sentiment / severity / dish, `before_json`, `after_json`, `user_id`, `authority`; `review_signals.retag`). The review row takes the correction; this keeps what it was and who changed it, and the analyser reads the recent ones as this restaurant's examples. One row per changed field; kept forever.

### `review_requests`
Outbound "please leave us a review" asks — tracks `customer_phone`, send state. `review_id` / `matched_at` (9/29/26): the review a request produced, matched by the guest's name when reviews are fetched (`review_signals.match_review_requests`); NULL until matched.

### `response_templates`
Saved canned-response templates per restaurant.

## Labor / Scheduling

### `schedule_history`
One row per generated schedule: `week_start`/`week_end`, `hours_scheduled`/`hours_budget`/`labor_target`, the full `schedule_csv`, `summary_json`, and (added later) `quality_json` — the Shift Quality Engine's full evaluation for that schedule — plus `edited_at`/`edited_by` when a manager hand-edits a published schedule. `published_at`/`published_by` mark the moment it went to staff: the staff portal, the payroll-week hours check and `schedule_publish_trust` read only rows with `published_at` set — a draft is never "the schedule". `review_json` is the rule sweep (`schedule_rules.summarize`), `generation_seconds` the model time, `weather_json` the forecast the draft saw, `economics_json` what it was written against (`projected_revenue` and its source, `labor_budget_dollars`, `daily_target_hours`, `demand_data_through` — the Schedule Studio's labor % and savings for a reopened week, 9/26/26; since 10/10/26 also `requirements_by_date` — `{"<date>|<daypart>": {role: people}}`, what the draft was ASKED for, the base the learned headcount is a residual of (schedule audit LEARNING-3) and the scorecard's requirement error is read against (RM-4); the code and arms that made it are in `schedule_generation_tags`).

### `schedule_shifts` / `schedule_shift_weeks`

One row per shift of each copy of a stored week (schedule re-audit 10/11/26 ARCH-7, contract #5): `history_id`, `copy` (`working` = schedule_csv, `published` = published_csv, or the working text of a week published before the copies were kept apart), `revision` (the week's newest `schedule_versions.version` when written), `seq` (the row's place in its copy's text), `shift_id` (schedule_rows' id; UNIQUE per history and copy), `person_id`, `person_ref` (`p:<person id>`, else `n:<folded name>` — who the double-booking guard compares), `employee_name` / `employee_key` (the fold), `role`, `date` (business date), `day` (as written), `shift_start` / `shift_end` as written, `starts_at` / `ends_at` (`YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM`, the business date's wall clock, an overnight close past midnight), `hours`, `scheduled_hours`, `notes`, `locked`, `week_start`, `overlap_ok_with` (the shift ids this one may overlap). Indexed by (restaurant, date), (restaurant, shift_id), (restaurant, person_id, date), (restaurant, employee_key, date), (history_id, copy, person_ref). `schedule_shift_weeks` marks a week projected: `history_id` primary key, `revision`, `source_sig` (length and crc32 of the two copies' text and the publish stamp), `exact` (1 when the rows are exactly what the text parses to — shape, order, ids), `fmt` (the projection's format, `schedule_shifts.FORMAT`). Invariants: written only by `schedule_shifts.project`, inside the transaction of the week's own write (`schedule_versions.store_week`, `models.save_schedule_history`) — so it never disagrees with the text; it is THE read of a stored week's rows (`schedule_shifts.rows_of` / `rows_by_week` / `live_week_rows` / `revision_rows`), served only while the marker is current, of the current format and exact, the text's parse otherwise (inside `schedule_shifts`; `tests/test_sf11_w_shift_reads.py` holds every other parse of a week's text to a list that may only shrink); one person on two overlapping shifts in a week's PUBLISHED copy is refused by the database (trigger `schedule_shifts_no_double_booking`; its plpgsql twin in `pg_copy.trigger_statements` / `migrations/postgres/0002_triggers.sql`) unless the pair is in `overlap_ok_with` — let through when the publish gate cleared the week (`store_week(allow_overlaps=True)` from `schedule_publish._stamp`), when the pair was already in the copy, or on a week's first projection; the working copy is never refused. One revision per copy is kept, the current one: every older state is `schedule_versions`'. No foreign key on `history_id` on purpose (derived data; `models.delete_schedule_history` and the drafts prune remove the rows). Backfilled at boot (`schedule_shifts.backfill`): newest weeks first, at most `BACKFILL_MAX_WEEKS` (600) weeks and `BACKFILL_SECONDS` (10 s) a boot, resumable from its `job_cursors` cursor (`schedule_shifts:backfill`), re-projecting a week whose marker is missing, of an older format or stale. Size: about 0.5 KB a shift row with its indexes (2,000 weeks x 150 shifts, two copies on published weeks: +260 MB, measured 10/14/26).

### Shift identity on lifecycle rows (schedule re-audit 10/11/26 ARCH-6)

Every row about one shift keeps the shift's id (`schedule_rows` shift_id) beside the old (name, date, start): `shift_change_requests.shift_id` / `person_id` (the holder's leg) and `target_shift_id` / `target_person_id` (a swap colleague's), with a partial unique index `uq_shift_requests_live_sid` (one live request per shift id) beside the name one; `held_consent_rest` (a held claimant's yes to a short rest, recorded only if a decider's yes covers it — BUGSLIFE-7); `shift_sections.shift_id` (a section follows its shift through a retime, cover or swap); `staff_consents.shift_id` and `subject.shift` (a rest waiver allows the short rest before that one shift; status `void` — given for a claim that never went through, set at boot by `staff_consent.void_unused_rest_waivers`); `shift_commitments.shift_id` (a rename keeps the promise, a retime is "retimed"). Lookups go by id first and by the tuple only for rows from before ids (`shift_requests._leg_index`); `shift_requests.backfill_shift_ids` matches old live and covered requests once at boot. `person_questions.kind` gains `two_people` (one record whose history reads as two humans; person_a = person_b; answered "split" → `people.split_by_role`).

### `schedule_versions`
Every state a schedule has been in — `(history_id, version)` unique, `reason` in `generated | edited | fixes | published`, the CSV, `quality_json`, `diff_json` against the previous version, `saved_by`. Since 10/10/26 (scheduler audit SCALE-4) `quality_json` holds only the evaluation's headline (`schedule_versions.quality_headline`: `checked`, `score`, `raw_score`, `band`, `recommendations`, `confidence.level`, the top two `weaknesses` / `strengths`, `week_dimensions` as `{key, score}`, `headline: true`) and `quality_ref` names the whole evaluation in **`quality_blobs`** — `hash` (sha256 of the restaurant id and the evaluation's JSON) primary key, `restaurant_id`, `body` (zlib), `raw_bytes`, `created_at` — written once per distinct evaluation on the version's own transaction (`store_quality`; the identical evaluation on the next version costs nothing) and read back by `version_quality`. It was 100-440 KB of uncompressed JSON on every version (86% of the table; ~12x smaller compressed). Versions written before keep their whole evaluation inline until the nightly prune compacts them into the same shape (`ops._compact_version_quality`, bounded); a blob no version points at is removed by the prune (`ops._gc_quality_blobs`, an hour's grace). Since 10/14/26 (blind re-audit 10/11/26 SCALE-13) `schedule_history` stores its draft's evaluation the same way: `quality_json` the headline, `quality_ref` the whole evaluation in `quality_blobs` (the same blob its `generated` version names — one stored copy), and `review_json` the generation review's headline (`schedule_versions.review_headline`: `hard`, `soft`, `ok`, `inputs_missing`, `stations`, `headline: true`) with `review_ref` the whole review (assignment reasons, unmet, soft requirements, stage failures) — read through `models.history_quality` / `models.history_review`, written by `save_schedule_history`, `schedule_engine._annotate_history` and the Studio save (`models.store_history_review`). It was 227-582 KB of quality JSON and 88 KB of review inline per week (60 / 250 people). Drafts written before are compacted nightly (`ops._compact_history_detail`, bounded, resumable — a compacted row no longer matches); a thinned draft drops both refs; the blob collector keeps any blob a draft names (`idx_schedule_history_qref` / `_rref`). `ops.storage_per_week` measures what a generated week costs (history, versions, stored detail), shown with the storage trend. `schedule_versions.learned_patterns` reads the recurring edits back into the next draft. `saved_authority` (9/29/26) is `permissions.answer_authority` of the save; `admin` for an admin's hand whoever the caller named. Invariant: an admin's save is never the manager's word — and since the schedule audit of 10/3/26 (L-8) it is taken out of the week rather than the week out of the record: every learner reads a week through `schedule_versions.learning_weeks`, which attributes each change from the original draft to the save that last touched its rows and leaves out an admin's (`saved_authority` `admin` or `saved_by` `support:…`) unless adopted (`learnable_version`; `LEARNABLE_SQL` and `NOT_SUPPORT_TOUCHED_SQL` are no longer read by a learner). Columns added 10/3/26 (boot ALTER in `models.init_schedule_versions`): `row_origins_json` — `{"cavnar": {"date|employee|start": source}}`, the rows whose change in this save was Cavnar AI's (`apply_fixes` | `optimize` | `overtime_move`, from the client's `origin` flag or Cavnar AI's own note tags — L-5): never learned as the manager's habit; `adopted_by` / `adopted_at` — an admin's save the account holder counted as theirs (`adopt_admin_saves`). `saved_authority` `system` is the generator, a job and the unattended automatic publish (L-7) — nobody's acceptance; a person's own Send that waited out the undo window is theirs (`delayed.queued_actor`). The learners read only versions up to a week's FIRST `published` one (L-4: later saves are reactions).

Since 10/10/26 (scheduler audit SCALE-34, RM-14) a version is a SNAPSHOT or a DIFFERENCE: `delta_json` (line operations — `[start, count]` runs of the snapshot's lines and literal new lines, `schedule_versions.encode_delta`) and `base_version` (the snapshot it applies to) with `schedule_csv` `''`. A save keeps its difference from the week's newest snapshot when that rebuilds the text byte for byte and is under `DELTA_MAX_SHARE` (half) of it; the first version is always whole, and a difference grown past the share makes the next snapshot — so a Studio save writes the week's whole text once (the working copy in `schedule_history`), not twice. Every reader selects `schedule_versions.VERSION_TEXT` and passes the rows through `schedule_versions.materialize`; the prune never removes a snapshot a kept version is stored against (`ops._prune_schedules`). Both columns added at boot by `models.init_schedule_versions`.

`schedule_standing_patterns` (`schedule_intel.py`, 9/29/26) — what the draft has learned and keeps: (re-audit 10/4/26, LEARN-3/4, boot ALTERs: `owner_kept_at` — when an account holder last kept it, holding it applied whatever its confidence for two half-lives; `answer_authority` — principal | delegate, whose keep or let-go it last was; a delegate's "always" is `source` learned, never owner_said; `retired_reason` manager for a delegate's let-go; a `ruled` row whose rule the owner removed is `active` again) one row per `(restaurant_id, pattern_key)` UNIQUE — `kind` (moved_off / moved_on / retime_start and the live window's other kinds), `employee`, `role`, `day`, `daypart`, `time`, `text`, `editors`, `first_learned`, `last_confirmed`, `times_applied`, `times_overridden`, `checked_through` (the last published week read), `status` active / retired / ruled, `rule_note`, `ruled_by`. A pattern the live window learns (and the owner has not dismissed) becomes a row; every published week that keeps it confirms it; it retires only when a manager's own edits reverse it `STANDING_RETIRE_AFTER` (2) times — never because nobody had to make the correction again. "Make it a rule" writes it into the person's own availability (`staff_settings`) and marks it `ruled`. `refresh_standing_patterns` skips a restaurant that may not teach (`models.learning_eligible`). Kept forever. Re-audit 9/29/26 (boot ALTERs in `init_schedule_intel`): `person_id`; `detail` (JSON: `was_role`, `delta`, `names`); `times_confirmed` (weeks the manager's own hand kept it — the draft had not carried it, or they edited that slot and kept it; `times_applied` also counts weeks the draft merely carried it, and the weeks that taught it count as neither — reading starts after the newest of them); `last_overridden` (two reversals retire it only inside `STANDING_OVERRIDE_WINDOW_DAYS`, 56, of each other); `retired_at` / `retired_week` (the published week that retired it — the live window's older copy is held back from the draft and cannot revive it; only evidence newer than that week brings it back, and the live pattern that is merely the reversal of a retired one is not stored); `dormant_at` with status `dormant` (a person pattern whose person has had no shift for `people.MEMORY_GONE_DAYS` against the restaurant's own newest shift, or is inactive — out of the draft and the memory lines, woken when they return; a week the person did not work does not test their pattern). Names are canonicalised through `people.canonical_names`, and the table is in `people.NAME_STORES` (a rename or merge re-keys it; two rows on one key fold). Headcount rows are confirmed/retired against the draft and, while active, feed `schedule_learning.learned_headcount_adjustments` after the live window forgets them. Schedule audit 10/3/26 (boot ALTERs in `init_schedule_intel`): `opportunities` / `hits` — the published weeks that tested it and kept it, an unedited week a keep (L-6); `last_hand` (ISO date) — when a manager's own hand last confirmed it (the weeks that taught it, a week they re-applied it, the owner's "keep it"); `confidence` — Wilson lower bound of hits over opportunities times the half-life decay since `last_hand` (`standing_confidence`: a person on a slot 120 days, a headcount or start time 180); `retest_since` — status `retest`: one half-life with no hand confirmation and the next draft leaves it out once (live copy included); the manager putting it back confirms it, a week sent without it retires it (L-30); `last_retest_end`, `retests`; `retired_reason` (reversed | retest | decayed — two half-lives unconfirmed and confidence under 0.3 — | owner); `source` (learned | owner_said — the owner's one-tap "always", L-35) and `authority` (whose word made an owner_said row). A week nobody of the restaurant looked at (the automatic publish with no edit, an admin's hand only) tests nothing. Status `ruled` also covers a `headcount_add` made a role floor and a retime made a role time rule (L-33). Schedule audit 10/10/26 (LEARNING-1, RM-5; boot ALTERs in `init_schedule_intel`): `last_kept_week` — the newest published week a person of the restaurant sent that kept it; `last_kept` — the newest date such a week was WORKED as published (`schedule_versions._worked_kept`: the punches on the pattern's weekday do not undo it — the person taken off did not work the slot, the one put on did; a time or headcount once the slot ran; a restaurant with no punches at all reads as having worked what it published), and the decay runs from the newer of `last_hand` and `last_kept` (`renewed_on`), so a habit the draft carries every week no longer fades under the line 9–15 weeks in; the re-test still comes one half-life after `last_hand` (the one check-in); `enforced_since` — when it first bound the passes (confidence at `schedule_memory.ACTIVE_CONFIDENCE` or the owner's word): from then on the memory holds it applied until its lifecycle ends it (a failed re-test, two reversals, dormancy, a let-go — cleared on retirement), never because a confidence decayed. Schedule re-audit 10/11/26 (LEARNING-9/10/11; boot ALTER in `init_schedule_intel`): `evidence_basis` — 'hand': `hits` / `opportunities` count the manager's own hand only (a week the draft merely carried it adds to `times_applied` and renews `last_kept`, never a hit); a row from before it is corrected once at boot (`schedule_versions.migrate_hand_evidence`: the carried weeks, `times_applied` less `times_confirmed`, come out of both counts, and `enforced_since` is cleared where the row bound on them alone). A row is a candidate from `CANDIDATE_MIN_OPPORTUNITIES` (3) weeks and a Wilson lower bound of 0.3 (`candidate_ok`); `enforced_since` is set by `habit_enforceable` (3 hand hits, Wilson ≥ 0.5) or the owner's word, and at once by a one-tap "Always" — a manager's too (still `source` learned, never owner_said).

### `staff_note_holds`
A scheduling note about one person the owner confirmed as a hold (`person_note_holds.py`, 10/2/26): `employee_key` / `employee_name`, `part_text` (the note's words, the match back to the note), `days` (JSON weekdays, NULL every day), `dayparts` (JSON `morning` or `night`, NULL the whole day), `start_date` / `end_date` (inclusive; NULL open), `created_by`; removed by `removed_at`/`removed_by`, never deleted. `schedule_rules.build_constraints` turns every hold in force into unavailability (`blocked_dates`, or `daypart_avail` when every date built is inside the hold); a shift on it is the hard `note_unavailable` breach.

### `schedule_note_rules`
A Studio note the owner confirmed as a staffing minimum (`schedule_note_rules.py`, 10/1/26): `scope` `every` (every week) or `week` (`week_start`, that week's Monday, only), `role` (one of the restaurant's own roles, as typed), `min_people` 1–10, `dayparts` (JSON: `morning` and/or `night`), `days` (JSON weekdays, NULL every day), `source_text` (the note's sentence, named in a `coverage_floor` breach), `created_by`; removed by `removed_at`/`removed_by`, never deleted. `schedule_rules.build_constraints` raises the week's role floors to every rule in force. `kind` (boot ALTER, schedule audit 10/3/26 L-33) — `floor` (every row before it), or `start` / `end` with `at_time` ("4:30pm"; `min_people` 0): a role's start or end on a daypart, from a learned retime the owner made a rule (`add_time_rule`; a newer one for the same role, edge, daypart and days replaces it) — loaded into `Constraints.role_times`, the draft retimed to it (`schedule_rules.apply_role_times`), the model told it, and a row off it a soft `role_time` breach naming it. Since scheduler audit 10/10/26 (RM-9, `owner_rules.confirm`) a `kind` may be any typed rule of the owner's words — `max`, `person_off`, `person_on`, `pair`, `avoid`, `close`, `open`, `weight` — with four boot ALTERs: `person` / `person2` (the roster's spelling), `value` (a count or a weight), `dates` (JSON ISO dates); `apply_note_rules` holds them through `owner_rules.apply` (hard: confirmed). Since the blind re-audit of 10/11/26: a reading of a draft's instruction the owner ticked on the Generate sheet (or Ask Cavnar's card) is stored here as a `scope` `week` rule of its kind (`owner_rules.confirm_for_week`; a start/end too — `add_time_rule` takes `scope`/`week_start`; a reading naming dates holds on their weekdays of that week) — AI-7; and `kind` `waiver` (`add_waiver`, BUGSGEN-4) is the owner's confirmation that `source_text` lets a standing position off (`role` its family, `dayparts`/`days` the slots read, `min_people` 0) — `schedule_dedicated.confirmed_waiver_keys` reads it; never a floor or a typed rule.

### `owner_rule_extractions` (scheduler audit 10/10/26, RM-9)
Each sentence of the owner's words read into typed rules, once (`owner_rules.read_items`): `restaurant_id`, `sentence_key` (sha256 of the sentence, spacing and case folded, and what it was read against — since the blind re-audit of 10/11/26 (AI-23) only the roster names and roles the sentence MENTIONS, whose note it is, and a draft's week: a hire the sentence does not name never re-reads it; UNIQUE per restaurant), `source` (`notes`, `instruction`, `staff_note`), `sentence`, `person` (a staff note's person), `rules_json` (the validated rules — shape and meaning, `owner_rules.validate`; `[]` for a sentence that states none, so it is never asked again), `model`, `created_at`, `dismissed_at` / `dismissed_by` (boot ALTER, AI-10: the owner's "not what I meant" — the reading is neither held nor proposed). Written by the save-time read (`owner_rules.queue_read` → the job queue's `owner_words_read`, one batched call — AI-22), the generation job's fallback for anything no save reached (`prepare`), the owner's "Read my notes" and the Generate sheet's read of the instruction; read by every Constraints build (no model call). Not in the retention registry: a cache keyed by the sentence, a few rows a restaurant. Created at boot by `owner_rules.init_owner_rules`.

### `schedule_observations` / `schedule_memory` / `schedule_memory_state` / `schedule_learning_records`
What the restaurant's scheduling has learned, as one memory, and the log it is built from (`schedule_memory.init_schedule_memory`, called by `init_db`; schedule audit 10/3/26 L-29). `schedule_observations` — the raw log, one row per atomic fact: `kind`, `history_id`, `week_start`, `date`, `daypart`, `role`, `person` / `person_id`, `value_json`, `origin` (manager | cavnar | staff | system), `phase` (pre_publish | post_publish | as_run), `authority` (principal | delegate | system | admin), `editor`, `source`; an event (an edit at first publish, a reaction to a sent week, a redo, a discard, a one-tap why) is appended, a MEASURED fact carries `fact_key` (UNIQUE with `restaurant_id`; NULL keys never collide) and is re-recorded in place (`updated_at`): `actual_hours`, `coverage_gap_actual`, `labor_vs_target_daypart` (record_outcomes), `stayed_late` (the nightly attendance join and the consolidation, one key), `ot_actual`, `sq_as_run` / `sq_planned` (the week scored as it ran beside its planned score), `calibration_suggested`, `ratings_suggested`. Retention 400 days on `created_at` (floor 200: the redo learner reads 24 weeks, the as-run measurement a recommendation accepted 180 days ago); the memory is what outlives it. `schedule_memory` — compact, one row per fact, UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, memory_key)` (the person part of a key is their people id where they have one, so a rename keeps the row): `kind`, `fact_class` (habit | ownership | team | overtime | rejection | staff | attendance | outcome | mentoring), `person` / `person_id`, `role` (a role family), `day`, `daypart`, `value_json`, `text`; the evidence — `opportunities` and `hits` (raw counts: the weeks or shifts the fact could have shown, and did), `weeks` / `of_weeks`, `misses_by_hand`, `last_confirmed_by_hand`, `first_seen`, `last_seen`; `confidence` (the Wilson lower bound of the recency-weighted hits over the recency-weighted opportunities, times — for a manager's habit — the half-life decay since a hand last confirmed it), `half_life_days`; `status` (candidate | active | rule | retest | retired | dormant), `enforcement` (prompt | soft | hard), `source`, `origin`, `authority_of_evidence`, `retired_reason` / `retired_at`, `rule_ref`, `owner_said` (keep | let_go) / `owner_said_by` / `owner_said_at`. Rebuilt nightly by `consolidate` (job `schedule_memory`), the pattern rows again at each generation; every `schedule_standing_patterns` row is carried in whole (its evidence, status and confidence). Kept (bounded by its keys); both tables are in `people.NAME_STORES` (re-pointed on a rename or merge, deleted on an erase). `schedule_memory_state` — per restaurant, `consolidated_at` and the last run's `stats_json`. Since 10/10/26 (LEARNING-16) the log's events are read, not only written: a `reaction` (a change to a sent week) carries `value.class` (`call_off_replacement` | `demand_add` | `demand_cut` | `personal_swap` | `retime` | `role_change`, `schedule_learning.classify_reaction`) and the demand adds and cuts feed the learned headcount (`schedule_learning.reaction_headcount`); the edits observed at first publish (`edit_move`, `edit_retime`, `edit_role`, `edit_headcount`) are how a save knows a change repeats (the one-tap why's second occurrence, LEARNING-2); `sq_planned` beside `sq_as_run` is the scorecard's plan adherence. `schedule_learning_records` (schedule audit 10/10/26 SCALE-33, ARCH-28) — each week as the edit learners read it (`schedule_versions.learning_weeks`' record: the original draft, the manager's own final, the attributed changes, what the draft was asked for), PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, history_id)`, kept under a `signature` of everything it is read from (`schedule_versions._record_plan`: every draft of that week's versions, one-tap answers, redos and economics, whether an automatic publish sent it, a hash of the restaurant's people and aliases, `RECORD_VERSION`); a week nothing touched since is read back instead of replayed from its CSV versions; `record_json` 'null' for a week with no generated draft. A cache — a stale or missing row is only read anew; a rename, merge or erase changes the people hash and re-reads every week. 168 days (the learners' window). Re-audit 10/4/26 (LEARN-3, boot ALTER in `init_schedule_memory`): `owner_said_authority` — whose answer `owner_said` is (principal: an account holder, the owner's word — held applied whatever the confidence, retired as "you let it go"; delegate: a manager's or a member's — a hand confirmation, or retired as "a manager let it go" (`retired_reason` manager); NULL on a row answered before it, read as the owner's). A delegate never answers against the owner's answer. `retired_reason` also reads `rule_removed` (the owner removed the rule the fact was made into — LEARN-5) and `not_closer` (a learned closer who is not one of the closers the owner chose — LEARN-10).

### `schedule_generation_tags` / `schedule_learning_scores` (the Schedule Learning Scorecard)
Schedule audit 10/10/26 RM-4, LEARNING-7, AI-29 (`schedule_scorecard.init_schedule_scorecard`, called by `init_db`). `schedule_generation_tags` — what made each saved draft, one row per `history_id` (PRIMARY KEY), written by `schedule_engine` after the save: `restaurant_id`, `generation_id`, `commit_sha` (`RAILWAY_GIT_COMMIT_SHA`), `arms_json` (`[{experiment, arm, pinned}]`), `origins_json` (`{row signature: origin}` — `model`, `manager_plan`, `model_edited:<stage>`, `added:<stage>`, `post_loop`; `schedule_scorecard.classify_rows` over the rows entering the repair loop, the loop's own record of what each stage added or changed, and the saved rows), `ablation_json` (`{ran, learned, rows_on, rows_off, seconds, offline}`: the repair loop's output, and the same loop over the same rows with `signals["learned"]` empty — the learning ablation, wanted when a learned memory binds the week, `{ran: false, queued: true}` until the bulk `schedule_ablation` queue row re-runs it in the week's stored metrics context after the save (blind re-audit 10/11/26 ARCH-14 — never on the owner's wait; `{ran: false, why: "queue off"}` with `JOB_QUEUE=0`), bounded at 20 seconds, off with `SCHEDULE_LEARNING_ABLATION=0`), `rules_json` (`{rules, broken, rate, unchecked}`: the owner's checked rules the draft honoured), `created_at`. 400 days. `schedule_learning_scores` — one row per published week, PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, history_id)`: `week_start`, `week_end`, `status` (open | frozen), `version`, `looked_at`, `rows_drafted`, `drr` / `drr_lo` / `drr_hi` (the weighted Draft Rework Rate per 100 drafted rows with its 90% interval — the manager's own pre-publish edits: an add or removal 1.0, a move 1.0, a role change 0.5, a retime 0.25 per 30 minutes; NULL for a week nobody of the restaurant looked at), `req_error_asked` / `req_error_punched` (mean |asked − published| and |published − punched| heads per role family, date and daypart), `labor_pct` / `hourly_target` / `labor_vs_target` (the week's hourly labor % against the owner's target less the salaried share), `ot_hours` / `ot_hours_planned`, `unmanaged_minutes` (as run: the punches and the salaried managers' published shifts, by the owner-facing no-manager verdict; NULL with no punches or no manager on the roster), `shortfall_hours`, `uncovered_no_shows`, `issues` (watched nights), `review_rating` (meal-attributed), `forecast_error` / `naive_error` / `forecast_skill` (the frozen week projection, `forecast_log` revenue_week), `ablation_effect` (the ablation's off-minus-on rework — NULL for weeks scored since the blind re-audit 10/11/26 LEARNING-13: anchored to the edited draft, it is not an effect; `metrics_json.ablation` keeps the memory's influence, `changed_per_100`), `metrics_json` (everything, origin survival, the model cost, `engaged` — whether a person reviewed the draft before it went out — and `drr_unreviewed`, the rate of a week sent unread, which counts nowhere; LEARNING-16), `computed_at`, `frozen_at`. Written by the Monday job `schedule_scorecard` (one `learner` queue row per restaurant, `score_one`, when the job queue is on; else `run_scorecard`); a row is FROZEN 14 days after its week ended and never written again. Kept forever (one row per week). `schedule_draft_views` — who of the restaurant had a draft open (LEARNING-16), one row per `history_id` (PRIMARY KEY): `restaurant_id`, `first_viewed_at`, `last_viewed_at`, `views`, `surfaces` (detail | review); written by the week's detail (`mobile_schedule_history_detail`, both twins) while the draft is unsent and by the pre-publish review, never for an admin or view-as; read by `schedule_scorecard.engagement`. Kept with its draft (one row per draft).

### The capabilities nobody ships (scheduler audit 10/10/26, workstream J3)
All created at boot by `init_db` (each module's `init_*`); none is read on a request path before boot.
- `standby_slots` (`standby_insurance.init_standby`, MARKET-26) — one on-call slot, UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, date, name_key, shift_start)`: `history_id`, `role`, `family`, `shift_end`, `employee_name`, `status` (offered | accepted | declined | withdrawn | dispatched | released | expired), `standby_pay` and `pay_basis` (the owner's policy in words), `chance`, `expected_cost`, `terms` (the exact words shown), `offered_by` / `offered_at`, and the person's written, time-stamped consent — `consent_at`, `consent_text` (= the terms they agreed to), `consent_channel` (app | portal), `consent_version` — `answered_at`; the dispatch (`dispatched_at`, `issue_id`, `offer_id` — the coverage issue and the offer `intraday.ask_to_cover` made, `dispatch_error`); `settled_at`, `pay_owed`, `outcome` (on call, not needed | called in | called, did not take it | withdrawn by the restaurant after they agreed). Kept (pay and consent records; in `people.NAME_STORES`). `standby_policy` — one row per restaurant: `pay_hours` or `flat_pay`, `note`, `updated_by` / `updated_at`; absent = the default (2 hours at the person's rate), always said as the default.
- `shift_commitments` (`commitment_ladder.init_commitments`, MARKET-27) — how firm each published shift was, PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, date, name_key, shift_start)`: `employee_name`, `role`, `shift_end`, `history_id`, `level` / `first_level` (firm | likely | tentative — the promise made, kept forever), `reason`, `committed_at`, and once the restaurant changed it `changed_at` / `change` (removed | moved | retimed). Kept (Fair Workweek-style records; in `people.NAME_STORES`).
- `published_event_marks` / `event_week_proposals` (`event_reactive.init_event_reactive`, MARKET-28) — the marks: per `(restaurant_id, history_id, date)` the followed events' `fingerprint` and `events_json` and the measured `effect_pct` as first seen on the published week. The proposals: UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, history_id, date, fingerprint)` — `change` (added | moved | cancelled | changed), `summary`, `pct_then` / `pct_now`, `actions_json` (`[{kind: add, role, date, shift_start, shift_end, hours, offer_to, others, cost} | {kind: cut, employee, ..., level, saves}]`), `cost_json` (`added_labor`, `saved_labor`, `predictability_pay`, `predictability_text`), `status` (open | noted | applied | dismissed), `result_json`, `decided_at` / `decided_by`. Kept (a few rows a week).
- `retention_overrides` (`retention_risk.init_retention`, MARKET-29) — the owner's protect switch per person, PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, name_key)`: `protect`, `updated_by` / `updated_at`. Owner-only; no risk figure is stored anywhere.
- `org_bench_sites` / `org_bench_members` (`org_bench.init_org_bench`, MARKET-30) — the site switch per restaurant (`enabled`: whether its opted-in staff's certifications and attendance are shared with the organization's other locations); the person's consent per `(user_id, restaurant_id)` — the HOME site — with `employee_name`, `consent_at`, `consent_text` (the exact words shown beside the travel switch), `consent_version`, `revoked_at`, written by the travel switch (`org_bench.record_travel`, beside `cross_site_travel`) — in `people.NAME_STORES`. Kept (small).
- `web_push_subscriptions` / `offer_response_platforms` (`staff_pwa.init_staff_pwa`, MARKET-18) — a browser subscription per `endpoint` (UNIQUE): `user_id`, `restaurant_id`, `p256dh`, `auth`, `platform`, `last_ok_at`, `failures`, `revoked_at` (revoked when the push service answers 404/410). An offer answer per `offer_id`: `platform` (ios_app | android_pwa | android_browser | ios_browser | pwa | browser), `accepted`, `offered_at`, `answered_at`, `seconds`; indexed on `answered_at` (the prune and the admin read). Retention below.

### `labor_standard_references` (the pre-go-live labor standard)
Schedule re-audit 10/11/26 LEARNING-8 (`staffing_model.init_staffing_model`, called by `init_db`). PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, family, daypart)`: `reference` — the median work per person-hour at the meal's busiest half hour on the restaurant's own nights before its first published Cavnar AI week (`requirement_provenance.go_live`), `days` (nights it was read from), `go_live`, `source` 'pre_cavnar', `updated_at`. Written by `staffing_model.measure` while its 8-week window still holds pre-go-live nights (a reading from more nights replaces it), or backfilled once from the archive's 8 weeks before go-live; read by `requirement_provenance.cap_unvalidated`, which holds every unvalidated Cavnar-era night to it — the cap used to vanish once the window passed go-live. Bounded (a row per family x meal); kept.

### `staffing_elasticity` / `staff_productivity` (what one more person is worth; each person's measured effect)
Scheduler audit 10/10/26 RM-20, LEARNING-6, MARKET-23, LEARNING-26 (`staffing_elasticity.init_staffing_elasticity`, `staff_productivity.init_staff_productivity`, called by `init_db`). Written by the Monday 8am job `staffing_learning` (`staffing_elasticity.run_elasticity`, bounded and resumable from the `staffing_elasticity_cursor` job cursor); each restaurant's rows are replaced whole each run. `staffing_elasticity` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, family, daypart)`: `move` (+1 / -1 / 0 — one person more or fewer, only where an interval cleared zero), `estimate_json` (`{outcomes: {sales|comps|voids|turn|kitchen|review: {est, lo, hi, se, df, n, method, level, treated_typical, weekdays}}` — the natural experiments, the decision's estimator; `panel: {outcome: {..., method: fixed_effects, report_only}}` — reported only (blind re-audit 10/11/26 LEARNING-5); `move, net: {est, lo, hi, sales, sales_lo, sales_hi, needs}, why, tests` (each one-sided p), `family_alpha, family_tests` (the run's Holm family — LEARNING-4), `level` / `target` (the controls' headcount and the level the move asks for — LEARNING-6), `weekdays` / `weekday_typical` (where it applies — LEARNING-7), `streak` / `binds` (two agreeing weekly estimates), `estimated_on` (a move binds for 21 days — LEARNING-24), `windows` (`[[from, to]]` while a +1 bound the drafts; the baseline's trend detector holds an upward trend inside one — requirement_provenance), `typical_sales, shift_cost, shift_hours, meals, absences}` — sales a log ratio to the meal's same-weekday median, comps a rate, turns minutes), `computed_at`. Written by one `learner` queue row per restaurant when the job queue is on (`staffing_elasticity.estimate_one`). Read by `staffing_elasticity.moves` into the requirements (`schedule_requirements.shift_requirements(elasticity=)`) and `marginal_values`. `staff_productivity` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, person_key)` (`staff_settings.name_key`): `name`, `estimate_json` (`{name, measures: {sales_per_guest|turn_minutes|tickets_per_hour: {est, lo, hi, n, raw, weight, measured}}, window}`), `computed_at` — personnel data, the owner's alone (read only through `schedule_memory.suggested_ratings`'s principal-only route; never in a prompt). Both are bounded (a row per restaurant x family x meal, a row per person who rang tickets) and kept, replaced weekly.

### `schedule_calibration_runs` / `schedule_calibration_cohort`
The Shift Quality weights calibrated where nobody presses Apply (`schedule_calibration.init_schedule_calibration`, called by `init_db`; schedule audit 10/10/26 LEARNING-9). `schedule_calibration_runs` — one row per restaurant per weekly run (`strategy_jobs.run_quality_calibration`): `status` (blind re-audit 10/11/26 LEARNING-1..3: a run is made only when a published week is new since the last one; the candidate is fitted on the OLDER weeks only — shadow — it did not beat the weights now and the weights before Cavnar AI's earlier changes on the held-out newest weeks (Williams' test), or nothing moved | pending — it did; waiting for 2 published weeks it never saw | refuted — on those new weeks it did not (or they never came within 70 days) | held — confirmed on the new weeks, but the restaurant does not learn for itself, auto-promotion is off — the default, `SCHEDULE_CALIBRATION_AUTOPROMOTE=1` turns it on — or the owner undid one less than 56 days ago | promoted — written to `restaurants.quality_weights_json` within the bounded steps | reverted — the owner's Undo), `before_json`, `candidate_json`, `holdout_json` (`{weeks, shifts, current, candidate, gain, p_value, p_better, newest_week, weeks_seen, fitted_on_weeks, vs_anchor}`; a prospective run's `{retrospective, prospective, prospective_vs_anchor, proposed_run}`: the correlation, within each weekday and daypart, of each set of weights' score with what the shifts were worth in dollars), `cohort_json`, `reason`, `reverted_by` / `reverted_at`. `schedule_calibration.fleet_readout()` reads every prospective check — the evidence the promotion switch is decided on. A promotion is also a `capability_changes` row (`quality_weights_promoted`, an undo `quality_weights_reverted`) and a `calibration_promoted` observation. 400 days. `schedule_calibration_cohort` — the pooled evidence per dimension, one row per ISO `week` (a stratified, weekly-rotated sample of the fleet — `learning_stats.fleet_sample` — each restaurant's training weeks only, never its held-out ones): `restaurants` (never under `intelligence.privacy.MIN_COHORT`), `shifts`, `evidence_json` (dimension → evidence, anonymous ratios, `assert_anonymous`), `computed_at`. Kept (one row a week).

### `schedule_publish_overrides`
The publish warnings a person was shown, and the ones a send went out past, by key (`schedule_overrides.init_schedule_overrides`, called by `init_db`; schedule audit 10/10/26 TRUST-28). One row per `(restaurant_id, history_id, key)` UNIQUE: `kind` (the key's stable head — `rule:<kind>`, `input:<source>`, `stage:<stage>`, `roster:<kind>`, `soft:<kind>`, `saved`, `hours_over`, `late_change`, `notice_short`, `quality_unchecked`, `needs_review`, `sweep_failed`), `text`, `shown_at` (the 409's list, or the list a send went out past), and when a send went out past it `overridden_at`, `version` (the `published` version that send wrote), `actor`, `authority`, `reason` (the pre-publish screen's `overrides: [{key, reason}]`), and `covered_by` (schedule audit 10/10/26 ARCH-30: who runs the floor through a manager gap the send went out past — the gate takes a `rule:no_manager:…` / `roster:no_manager_roster` override only with it; boot ALTER in `init_schedule_overrides`). `schedule_overrides.stats` reads the last 180 days per kind in weeks (shown, sent past, the rate and its Wilson bound); `annotate` puts each blocker's record beside it, ranks the ones owners always override last, lists them as `always_overridden`, and moves a DEMOTABLE kind (`hours_over`, `saved:<line class>`) sent past in at least 4 weeks with a Wilson bound of 0.6 to a note — never a hard kind, never for an unattended send. 400 days. Schedule re-audit 10/11/26 (TRUST-9, BUGSLIFE-12): `magnitude` / `magnitude_unit` (boot ALTERs; the hourly budget's overage in `$` or `h` when shown and when sent past) — a sized warning is demoted only while it is within the largest one sent past plus `DEMOTE_MAGNITUDE_SLACK` (10%); a saved line's kind is its normalised wording (`schedule_overrides.saved_class`), read from `key` at stats time so older rows stored under the single `saved` kind are re-classed; and a row is written as SHOWN whenever `prepublish.review` renders the blocker, so a week the owner fixed counts against the rate.

### `schedule_review_dismissals` (schedule re-audit 10/11/26 TRUST-17)
The owner's answers to the pre-publish review's suggestions, kept server-side (they lived in the page and came back on every check of every week). UNIQUE (`restaurant_id`, `key`) — `left_off:<name>`, `floor:<role>:<day>:<part>`, `crew:…`; `scope` (`week` — until `until_date`, the week's last day; `every` — while `evidence`, the suggestion's signature, e.g. `floor=4|suggest=3`, is unchanged, at most `DISMISS_MAX_DAYS`), `history_id`, `answer`, `actor`, `authority`, `dismissed_at`. Boot DDL in `schedule_overrides.init_schedule_overrides`; written by `POST labor/publish-review/dismiss`; read by `prepublish` (`schedule_overrides.dismissals` / `dismissed_now`). Retention 200 days (*Retention*). No guest data; `actor` is a login's name.

### `schedule_rejections` / `schedule_edit_answers`
The owner's no and the owner's why (`schedule_intel.init_schedule_intel`, schedule audit 10/3/26). `schedule_rejections` — one row per redo of some days (`kind` `redo_days`, `dates_json`) or whole week generated over a draft never sent (`draft_discarded`): `history_id` (the draft rejected), `week_start`, `reason_chip` / `reason_text` (the owner's optional reason), `authority`, `requested_by` — written for the generate route once the job has finished with its own draft in force (`schedule_engine._settle_rejection` → `schedule_versions.record_rejection`, L-26; schedule audit 10/10/26 ARCH-18 — never at the press, so a press stopped or failed rejects nothing) and observed (`schedule_memory`).
