A redo links the new draft to the one it replaced, so the learners diff against the ORIGINAL draft and the edits made before the redo are kept; the edit predictor counts the rows of the redone days as rows the manager changed (an admin's redo links the drafts but is not the owner's no). `schedule_edit_answers` — the one-tap "why" a save asked in a restaurant's first four weeks (L-35): UNIQUE (`restaurant_id`, `history_id`, `question_key`); `version`, `kind`, `subject_json` (the change and its offered answers), `keys_json` (the changed rows' `date|employee|start` keys), `phase`; `answer` (always | this_week | call_off, NULL while asked), `authority`, `answered_by`, `answered_at`, `adopted_by` / `adopted_at` (an admin's answer counts once adopted). `this_week` and `call_off` keep the change out of the learned habits; `always` makes it a standing pattern at once; `call_off` writes the call-out to `attendance_events`. Retention 400 days each (floor 168: the learners read 24 weeks).

### `staff_settings` / `staff_pairs`
The roster's facts: `active` (a deactivated person is never scheduled, whatever the shift history says), `employment_type` (full/part), `min_hours`/`max_hours`, `daypart_availability` (JSON weekday → any/morning/night/off), `is_minor`. Keyed by `(restaurant_id, employee_name)` like every staff table. `staff_pairs` is `prefer`/`avoid` between two names, stored once whichever way round it was typed.

### `demand_signals`
Owner-entered facts about specific dates: `kind` event, reservations or post (a scheduled post since the re-audit fix round, 9/29/26, CROSSMODULE-7 — never an event: no `event_memory` flag, no baseline exclusion, never "assumed busier", no trim suppression; its lift only where the marketing module measured posts like it; the boot migration `demand_signals.migrate_post_signals` re-kinds old rows and removes a cancelled or failed post's signal, and `remove_by_ref` does it live on cancel and on a publish that failed with nothing sent), `label`, `covers`, `lift_pct`, `source` (manual, csv, or a future sync — and since 9/29/26 `campaign` / `post`, a marketing signal `demand_signals.record_marketing` writes, its lift measured or NULL; since 10/3/26 `report`, a reservation system's booking export imported as it is — `demand_signals.parse_reservation_export`, one `Reservations` row per date, cancelled and no-show bookings out; schedule audit D-31). Unique on `(restaurant_id, date, kind, label)`. `ref` (`campaign:<id>` / `post:<id>`, what a marketing signal came from) and `menu_item_id` (the dish a post is about, so the prep list can name it) — 9/29/26, boot ALTERs. An event listed with no figure takes its label's measured median from `event_effects` once it has `event_memory.EFFECT_MIN_N` nights (`demand_signals.by_date`); an owner's own figure stands, with the measured record beside it.

### `event_series`, `catalog_events`, `event_follows`, `event_dismissals`, `event_peer_effects` (Event Intelligence, 10/1/26)
The global event catalog (`event_intel`). `event_series`: one recurring thing — a team, a festival, a venue's season, a holiday calendar — by `slug`, with `category` (sports, holiday, concert, festival, local, venue, school, civic), `league`, `metro`, `home_venue`, `timezone`, and where it is felt (`lat`, `lng`, `radius_km`). `catalog_events`: one dated occurrence, UNIQUE (`series_id`, `external_id`): `season`, `season_type` (preseason, regular, postseason, special), `week`, `event_date` (NULL while TBD), `kickoff_local` (HH:MM wall-clock time in the event's `timezone`, else the series'; said on each restaurant's own clock through `engine.local_kickoff`), `home_away`, `opponent`, `venue`, `broadcast`, `is_primetime` (NFL only, `store.PRIMETIME_LEAGUES`: a start at or after 6:30pm in the series' timezone — an NBA, NHL or MLS night game is every game, so none of theirs is prime time), `status` (scheduled, completed, postponed, cancelled), `result`, `attributes_json` (e.g. `{"holiday": "Thanksgiving"}`, `{"if_necessary": true}`, `{"alt_venue": true}` — a home game away from the series' ground, its own segment in `engine.effect_for`; `{"competition": "Leagues Cup"}` on a cup match, which nothing reads yet), `source_url`, `overrides_json` (phase 2, 10/1/26: an admin's corrections — `event_date`, `kickoff_local`, `broadcast`, `status`, `result` — kept apart from the season file and laid over it on every load, so the daily reload never undoes Week 18's date or a flexed kickoff; NULL when there are none). No restaurant column: one game is one row. `event_follows`: PRIMARY KEY (`restaurant_id`, `series_id`), `active` (0 is an opt-out auto-follow never overrides), `source` (auto, owner, admin), `distance_km` (an auto row's is refreshed by `engine.ensure_follows`, and an auto row whose restaurant has moved out of the radius is withdrawn; an owner's or admin's row is never touched). Each followed event is copied into the restaurant's `demand_signals` (source `events`, ref `event:<id>`) by the daily `event_sync`. `event_dismissals`: PRIMARY KEY (`restaurant_id`, `event_id`), `created_at`, `dismissed_by` (the login's name, or "<admin> (Cavnar AI)" for a removal made through view-as) and `source` (owner, admin) — both boot ALTERs (event re-audit 10/1/26, X-4) — a game removed from the restaurant's own list (`demand_signals.delete` on a catalog row) is never written back until it is put back (`store.undismiss`, `POST labor/event-dismissals/<event_id>/restore`, the events card's Put back); a re-follow leaves removals alone. A season file never moves a completed game back to scheduled; a postponed or cancelled game the file reinstates is scheduled again (an admin's status override is laid back over it). The daily job marks a scheduled game completed once its date is before today on its own clock (`store.local_today` of its timezone, never the server's UTC date) — except an unresolved if-necessary game (`store.unresolved`: past, no result, still scheduled), which stays scheduled: its restaurants' copies are labelled "· if necessary", stay flagged (out of every baseline) and unmeasured, and the admin catalog shows it as `needs_result` until a result or a cancellation is entered. Past game nights a sync could not re-record in one pass (more than `engine.RECORD_MAX`) wait in `job_cursors` `event_sync_record:<rid>` for the next pass. Seeded at boot from `event_intel/seasons/*.json` (the 2026 Chicago Bears first; since 10/1/26 also the Blackhawks, the Bulls, the Fire with its cup matches as `special`, the White Sox 2026 regular season and postseason, and the Cubs 2026). A game's season class (`engine.season_class`: regular, preseason, postseason, special) is part of its `demand_signals` label ("Bulls preseason home game · United Center", "White Sox playoff home game · Rate Field") and of `engine.game_class`, so no reader pools one class's nights with another's (event re-audit 2, 10/1/26); the next `event_sync` relabels older copies and re-records their past nights through the same queue. `event_peer_effects` (boot, `event_intel.init_event_intel` → `peers.init_peers`; re-audit 10/1/26, P4-05): PRIMARY KEY (`restaurant_id`, `series_id`, `home_away`, `preseason`), `median_lift_pct`, `nights`, `first_night`, `computed_at` — each restaurant's own measured median for a followed series, side and season class — regular season or preseason, at the home ground (`engine.effect_for(exact=True)`, demo-era nights out; no row is kept for a playoff or special game or a home game at another ground, so `peer_effect` gives those no figure), replaced whole per restaurant by the nightly `intelligence_features` pass, none for a restaurant that may not teach; indexed by segment, read only by `peers.peer_effect` (joined to active follows) — no dollars, no names; small and bounded by follows, so it has no retention entry. No guest data (a removal's `dismissed_by` is a login's name, as `demand_signals.created_by` is): not scrubbed, not retained by age.

### `shift_change_requests`
A member of staff asking to drop a published shift: `history_id`, the shift, `reason`, `status` in `pending | approved | open | denied | covered | withdrawn | cancelled | expired`, `replacement_name`, `decided_by`. `covered` also rewrites the published CSV and appends a version. Employee-app fix round (10/2/26, `models.init_shift_requests` boot ALTERs): `kind` adds `post` (a manager put the shift on the open board; an extra shift nobody holds has `employee_name = ''`); `decision_note` (the manager's note, shown to the requester), `escalated_at` (the open-shift watch told the deciders it is still unclaimed 12 hours out — once), `told_json` (the teammates told it was open, told again when it is taken or withdrawn), `offer_only` (offered to one person instead of the board), `target_agreed_by` (a decider recorded the swap colleague's yes given in person). `cancelled` — another request on the same shift moved it first (voided inside the cover's or swap's transaction); `expired` — the shift passed (`shift_requests.expire_past`, hourly). One live request per shift: the partial unique index `uq_shift_requests_live` on `(restaurant_id, lower(trim(employee_name)), date, shift_start) WHERE status IN ('pending','approved','open') AND employee_name <> ''` (skipped and logged at boot when existing rows already break it; the transactional check still guards). Schedule fix round 10/10/26 (D2, boot ALTERs): `held_claimant` / `held_claim_at` / `held_claim_note` — an employee's claim of an open shift that would put them past their overtime line while a same-role teammate could take it without, kept on the open row for a decider (`decide` on it approves or denies the hold; a claim without overtime still takes the shift; BUGSLIFE-14); `decided_how` (`manager` | `auto` — the earned automatic approval, `request_autonomy`) with `auto_reasons_json` (the checks it passed or the one it failed, kept whenever automatic approval is on — MARKET-14) and `reversed_at` / `reversed_by` (a manager reversed an automatic decision; that kind's trust lapses); `notice_pending` (`moved` | `cancelled` — a write to the live week carried or closed this request in its own transaction, `shift_requests.follow_week`, and the requester is told after the commit, `flush_notices`; ARCH-9). `cancelled` now also means a manager's change removed or reassigned the shift (`decision_note` says which) or kept their edit over a cover ("Keep my edits" with `override_staff`, BUGSLIFE-16). No retention entry yet — it is the parent of `shift_offers` and `schedule_outcomes`, and the learners read it.

Schedule re-audit 10/11/26 (workstream L): `floor_covered_by` (boot ALTER) — who runs the floor when an approved drop leaves part of the day with no manager on; `shift_requests.decide` refuses such a drop (`ManagerGapRefused`) unless a cover or this name is given (STRESS-16). A `covered` request a Send undoes (the person let off the shift back on it, or the taker off it) is closed by the stamp — `cancelled`, `notice_pending='cancelled'`, both people told after the commit (`shift_requests.close_reverted`, BUGSLIFE-1).

### `staff_availability_changes` (schedule fix round, 10/10/26 MARKET-15)
An employee's cut in their own availability (a day off, a narrower window), split from their save by `availability_changes.split` — more availability is written to `staff_availability` / `staff_settings.time_windows` at once, as before. `employee_name`, `days_json` (`{weekday: {status: off|window, earliest, latest, from, until}}`), `summary` ("Saturdays off"), `effective_from` (by default the first week nobody has drafted or published, never sooner than the notice days — `effective_default`), `status` (`pending` → `approved` | `denied`; `withdrawn` when a newer change of theirs replaces it; `applied` once in force), `source` (`app` | `link`), `decided_by`, `decided_at`, `decision_note`, `applied_at`. Approval is the restaurant's `staff_request_policy_json.availability_approval` (on unless the owner turns it off; off, a cut is approved at once for its date). An approved change is laid over the stored availability for the dates of a drafted week on or after its date (`overlay`, from `schedule_rules.build_constraints`) and written into it on the date (`apply_due`, the hourly `open_shift_watch`). Boot DDL `availability_changes.init_availability_changes` (from `init_db`); in `people.NAME_STORES`; retention 730 days (*Retention*).

### `shift_offers` (employee-app fix round, 10/2/26)
An open shift offered to one named person, answered in their app: `request_id` → `shift_change_requests`, `name` / `name_key`, `status` (`offered | accepted | declined | withdrawn | expired`), `note`, `issue_id` (when the offer came from a coverage issue — the answer is written to the issue and a yes resolves it), `created_by`, `created_at`, `answered_at`. Retention 730 days (*Retention*; three years under a predictive-scheduling law — the records hold); in `people.NAME_STORES`.

### The compliance records (schedule audit 10/10/26 RM-15)
Created at boot (`staff_consent.init_staff_consent`, `predictability_pay.init_compliance_pay`, `fair_workweek.init_fair_workweek`, from `init_db`); kept 1095 days (*Retention*).

- `staff_consents` — written, time-stamped consent from a member of staff: `employee_name` / `employee_key` / `person_id`, `kind` (`rest_waiver` — a shorter rest before a shift on `subject_date`; `rest_waiver_standing` — every short turnaround until `revoked_at`; `shift_change` — a change to a posted shift, `subject_json` {before, after}; `added_hours` — an open shift or offer they took, written by `shift_requests._cover`), `text` (the exact words agreed to — what the record proves), `status` (`pending → agreed | declined | revoked | withdrawn`), `asked_by` / `asked_at`, `answered_at` (UTC), `channel` (`portal | app | offer | manager`), `source_ref`. A row is never edited after it is answered, except a standing waiver's revocation. `staff_consent.rest_waivers` is what `schedule_rules.Constraints.rest_waived` reads under a `consent_premium` rest rule.
- `compliance_pay_ledger` — one row per changed shift (or rest-premium instance) inside a covering law's notice window, written at Send (`predictability_pay.record_send`, from `client_api.send_schedule_changes` and a draft replacing a live week in `_publish_schedule`) and when staff cover or swap (`record_request_change`): `history_id`, `version`, `jurisdiction`, the person, `shift_date`, `change_kind` (`add | extend | move | reduce | cut | rest_premium`), `before_json` / `after_json`, `minutes_changed`, `hours_lost`, `hours_added`, `notice_hours`, `initiator` (`employer | employee`), `exempt_reason`, `consent_id` → `staff_consents`, `premium_hours`, `rate`, `amount`, `rule_json` (the tier applied), `source` (the law's URL), `status` (`owed | exempt | not_covered | unknown_coverage`), `sent_at` / `sent_by`, `exported_at` (stamped by the payroll export). Never written for a restaurant no predictive law covers.
- `good_faith_estimates` — a person's written schedule estimate (`hours_low` / `hours_high`, `average_hours`, `median_monthly_hours`, `min_shifts_month`, `days_json` [{day, start, end}], `on_call`, `locations`, `period_days`, `text`, `basis_json`, `jurisdiction`), `issued_at` / `issued_by`, `acknowledged_at` (the person signed for it in the app or portal), `superseded_at` (a newer estimate replaced it).
- `fww_requests` — a person's written request to change their estimate (`kind` `estimate_change | schedule_preference`, `body`, `estimate_id`), `due_at` (the law's answer deadline — 3 days in Chicago and Evanston), `status` (`open | granted | modified | declined | withdrawn`), `answer` / `answered_by` / `answered_at`.
- `fww_access_offers` — additional hours offered to the current staff first: `request_id` → the open shift (`shift_change_requests`, kind `post`), the shift, `hours`, `posted_at`, `closes_at` (the law's posting or acceptance window, or the owner's where the law sets none), `status` (`open | filled | closed_unfilled | cancelled`, `fair_workweek.sync_access_offers`), `filled_by` / `filled_at`.

### `schedule_outcomes`
One row per published week, date and daypart: scheduled hours and people, the day's sales (a FINAL day's — `canonical_facts.FINAL_SQL`) split by this restaurant's MEASURED morning share, labor %, coverage and no-show issues that day, the mean review rating. Written by the daily outcomes job (`schedule_intel.record_outcomes`; Mondays only until 10/10/26), keyed `(history_id, date, daypart)`; read into the prompt as "what published weeks actually did" and by the chemistry suggestions and the cohort ratio features. Since 9/29/26 (boot ALTERs in `init_schedule_intel`): `split_basis` is `measured` or `unmeasured` — a weekday whose morning share was never measured has NULL daypart `sales` (it used to fall back to a stated 40%, read everywhere as measured); NULL on older rows reads as unmeasured. `review_attribution` / `review_rating_attributed` / `reviews_attributed` / `review_lag_days`: each review is put on the one shift it was about — `daypart` when the analyser read the meal in the review and it was posted within `REVIEW_LAG_DAYS` (2) of that shift, `hours` when it went on the posting day's busiest daypart for want of anything better; calibration reads the `daypart` kind only. Schedule audit 10/3/26 (boot ALTERs in `init_schedule_intel`): what HAPPENED beside the plan (L-12) — `actual_hours` / `actual_people` (the punches, `shift_facts`, placed by their clock-in's daypart as a planned shift is; the salaried don't clock in, so their planned shifts count as worked), `actual_basis` (`punches`; NULL when the date has no punch at all — unknown, never 0), `missed` (no-shows and call-outs), `late`, `left_early` (`attendance_events`), `stayed_late` (published shifts whose punch ran `schedule_memory.STAYED_LATE_MINUTES` or more past their end); and the DAYPART's own labor (L-13) — `labor_cost_daypart` (each punch's hours at its own pay, the person's rate or the role's — `labor._shift_rate` — split at the 3pm line the sales split at) and `labor_pct_daypart` (that over the daypart's measured sales; NULL unless the split was measured). Scheduler audit 10/10/26 LEARNING-11: `measured` now means the DATE's own split — its tickets (`pos_tickets`), else its intraday captures, else its nightly report's hourly split (`demand_drivers.date_part_sales`); a date with none of them but a weekday median share is `estimated` (its `sales` written from the share, its `labor_pct_daypart` NULL), and every learner reads `sales` only `WHERE split_basis='measured'`, so an estimate never trains one. `labor_pct` stays the DAY's figure (labor_daily_history), the same on both rows — calibration reads `labor_pct_daypart`. The prompt's outcome block and the chemistry suggestions read the worked figures beside the planned ones.


### `schedule_week_reads` (scheduler audit 10/10/26, SCALE-15/16)
What the daily outcomes job last read of each ended published week, by `step` (`outcomes` | `as_run`): `history_id` + `step` primary key, `restaurant_id`, `fingerprint` (`schedule_intel.week_fingerprint` — the plan's CSV, the week's punches in `shift_facts`, and for `outcomes` the week's `labor_daily_history` and `attendance_events`), `read_at`. A week is read while it settles (`WEEK_SETTLE_DAYS`, 14 days after its end) and after that only when its fingerprint changes; it is scored as run once its last POS day is final and again only when its punches or plan change. One row per published week and step, kept with its week; created at boot by `schedule_intel.init_schedule_intel`.
### `schedule_experiment_weeks` / `schedule_experiment_pins`
The live A/B of schedule generation (`schedule_experiments`, admin-only). One row per generated week and experiment, keyed `(history_id, experiment)`: the `arm` (a hash of restaurant and week, so a regeneration keeps it), whether it was `pinned` (and by `env`, `restaurant`, `promoted` or `underpowered` — an experiment that cannot conclude yet, fewer than `MIN_RESTAURANTS_PER_ARM` restaurants generating, runs its `until_powered` arm on every week, schedule audit 10/3/26 P-21; pinned weeks stay out of the comparison), the draft's `quality_score`, and whether the solver's week was kept (`solver_applied`). `schedule_experiment_pins` pins one restaurant to an arm (`off` = the control), keyed `(restaurant_id, experiment)` — the per-restaurant kill switch; `SCHEDULE_EXPERIMENT_PIN` is the global one. Both created at boot by `init_db`.

### `schedule_experiment_promotions`
The reviewed step from a winning arm to every restaurant's default (ROI audit #46): `experiment`, `arm`, `flags_json` (the arm's flags, so the promotion holds after the experiment is retired in code), `verdict_text` (the readout's words it rested on), `note`, `promoted_by`/`promoted_at`, `reverted_by`/`reverted_at`. A partial unique index keeps one live (unreverted) promotion per experiment; reverted rows stay as the trail. `schedule_experiments.promote` refuses any arm the readout does not call the winner. Created at boot by `init_schedule_experiments`.

### `schedule_experiment_verdicts`
What the readout said, week by week (memory audit 9/29/26): one row per ISO `week` and `experiment` (UNIQUE) — `state`, `call`, `text`, `method`, `arms_json` (each arm's generated weeks, restaurants, acceptance, quality, issues, labor % and difference from control). Written by the Monday `schedule_experiment_verdicts` job (`schedule_experiments.record_verdicts`, INSERT OR IGNORE: the first of the week stands). The readout it snapshots counts only restaurants that may teach, at most `MAX_WEEKS_PER_RESTAURANT` recent published weeks per arm, on the PUBLISHED version's quality. Kept forever.

### `schedule_generation_metrics` (scheduler audit 10/10/26, AI-2, AI-23, RM-1)

One row a saved schedule generation (`schedule_metrics.record`, from `schedule_engine._record_generation_metrics` after the save): `restaurant_id`, `history_id`, `generation_id`, `week_start`, `kind` (`week`, `redo`, `gate_fill`, `gate_rewrite`), `draft_source`, `model`, `contract`, `roster_size`, `calls` and `cost_usd` (every `schedule_model_calls` row of the generation priced at `ai_utils` rates), `model_seconds`, `seconds` (the job's stages), `model_rows` (rows the model wrote — not the manager plan, a kept day or a code-drafted day), `model_kept` and `survival` (the share still in the saved week unchanged), `role_violations` (model rows in a role the person does not hold — `role_not_held`), `final_rows`, `by_stage_json` (each repair stage's changed/trimmed/staggered/added rows) and `context_z` (zlib JSON, `schedule_output.encode`: the post-model context — the week's figures, the Constraints, the quality signals, requirements and curve — scrubbed with `redact_pii`, which `schedule_eval.freeze` copies into the frozen set). `schedule_metrics.summary` reads cost per accepted week (published weeks), p50/p95 seconds, survival and the role-violation rate. Created at boot by `schedule_metrics.init_schedule_metrics` (from `init_db`); retention 365 days.

### `schedule_check_snapshots` (schedule audit 10/10/26, ARCH-35)

What a saved week was checked and scored against, kept with it
(`schedule_output.record_check_snapshot`, written by the generation right
after its draft is saved; read by `schedule_output.check_snapshot` and
`schedule_engine.replay_checks`): `history_id`, `generation_id`,
`rules_version` (the deployed commit, else a hash of the rule, time and
scoring modules' source), `constraints_z` (the post-model
`schedule_rules.Constraints`, tagged JSON through `schedule_output.encode`,
zlib), `requirements_z` (the SHIFT REQUIREMENTS) and `signals_z` (the quality
signals and weights). Created at boot by `schedule_output.init_schedule_output`.
Retention 90 days with the calls (`RETAIN_SCHEDULE_MODEL_CALLS_DAYS`). Staff
names and the restaurant's rules, never guest data.

### `schedule_model_calls` (schedule audit 10/3/26, PR-31)
Every schedule model call's full input and answer, so a model, effort or prompt change can be replayed against real weeks (`schedule_output.py`; `scripts/schedule_model_eval.py`). One row per call `labor.generate_optimized_schedule` makes — the parts of a big roster, a missing-day retry, a contract fallback each their own — written as the call returns or fails: `restaurant_id`, `generation_id` (one id per generation, made by `schedule_engine._build_schedule_result`), `history_id` (the `schedule_history` row the draft was saved as — set by `schedule_output.link_calls` right after the save, `ON DELETE SET NULL`; NULL for a generation that failed or was never saved), `week_start`, `dates_json` (the dates the call wrote), `ai_call_id` (the `ai_calls` / `ai_usage` call id), `model`, `effort`, `contract` (`schema` — the generation's own enums; `plain_schema` — the shape alone, after the API refused the enums; `compact` / `plain_compact` — the same rows with one-letter keys; `shape` / `plain_shape` — slots without names, their people solved in code — AI cost audit 10/7/26 #69, #70, chosen per generation by `SCHEDULE_CONTRACT`; `csv`), `stop_reason`, `outcome` (`ok`, `truncated`, `refused`, `unparseable`, `error`; an auto-draft batch group's stored item — `job_id` the group, no request kept, its prompt hash in `usage_json.prompt_sig` — `batch_answer`, `batch_failed` or `batch_blocked`, ARCH-15; `salvaged` — not cut, but its JSON broke partway and was read up to the break, the days before it kept, #71), `error`, `seconds`, `usage_json` (input, output — thinking included — and cache tokens), `rows`, `answer_chars`, `call_kind` (schedule re-audit 10/4/26 PROMPT-3: `week`, `slice` — a date or department part of a fresh generation, a retry or split of one included — `redo` or `gate`; NULL before 10/4/26; a boot ALTER in `init_schedule_output`), `tier` (AI orchestration 10/7/26: the call's route on the `ai_workflows` `labor_schedule` ladder — `T3` Sonnet 5.5, `T4` Opus 5.5, `default` under a `SCHEDULE_MODEL` pin; NULL before 10/7/26; a boot ALTER in `init_schedule_output` — so the eval and the learner compare calls by tier; every call of one generation also carries the generation's `ai_runs.run_id` as its `ai_usage.correlation_id`), and three zlib-compressed JSON blobs: `request_z` (the exact request: model, max_tokens, system, messages, thinking, output_config with its schema), `inputs_z` (the generator's own arguments, tagged so tuples, sets, int and tuple keys and `ShiftProfile`s come back as they were — `schedule_output.encode` / `decode`), `shared_z` (the arguments every call of a generation shares and that are large — `analysis` and the shift history — stored once, on the generation's first call) and `answer_z` (the answer text, never the thinking). Every string of the request, the arguments and the answer passes `ai_utils.redact_pii` (a guest's contact details or labelled name), the trace's own rule; staff names and the restaurant's figures stay, and the shift history and labor analysis — the restaurant's own records — are stored as they are. Since 10/10/26 (scheduler audit SCALE-2, SCALE-22): `job_id` — the queue job (`job_queue`, the async job id) the call ran for — and `call_key` (`schedule_batch.call_key`: model, dates, output schema), so a generation the queue runs again after a deploy reads each answer its earlier try paid for (`schedule_resume`, `schedule_output.stored_answers`: `ok`, `truncated` or `salvaged` answers only; a re-read answer's `usage_json` says `resumed`); index `idx_sched_model_calls_job`. With `BLOB_STORE` set the four blobs hold a `blob_store` pointer and live in object storage (RM-14). Indexed on `created_at`, `generation_id`, `history_id` and `(restaurant_id, id)`; created at boot by `schedule_output.init_schedule_output` (from `init_db`); retention 90 days (*Retention*; 180 until AI cost audit 10/7/26 #94). `schedule_output.measured_tokens_per_row` reads it: the median output tokens a row really costs, from the last 60 days; `schedule_output.call_costs` reads the same 60 days as a call's fixed cost plus a cost per row (tokens and seconds, fitted over the calls, with medians per `call_kind`) — what `schedule_engine.rows_per_call` sizes a call by and `call_seconds` times it by (PROMPT-3, PROMPT-4), read at the model and effort of the route in force (`schedule_engine.call_cost_model`: a Sonnet 5.5 week is never sized from Opus calls, nor the reverse) and on the generation's contract family (`schedule_output.contract_family`: a compact row is never sized from schema calls — #70); a `salvaged` call is left out of both (its tokens bought days it did not keep).

### `schedule_eval_items` / `schedule_eval_runs` / `schedule_eval_verdicts` (scheduler audit 10/10/26, RM-1)
The frozen golden evaluation set for schedule generation (`schedule_eval.py`, `scripts/schedule_model_eval.py`). `schedule_eval_items`: one row per (`version`, `item_key`, UNIQUE) — `restaurant_id`, `week_start`, `generation_id`, `history_id`, `roster_size`, `tags_json` (size band, holiday, first week, redo, gate), and zlib JSON blobs: `calls_z` (every stored call of the generation, request and answer, scrubbed as `schedule_model_calls` is), `context_z` (the post-model context the job scored it in — `schedule_generation_metrics.context_z`'s snapshot) and `published_z` (the rows the owner published for that week — the ground truth). `schedule_eval_runs`: one row per arm run on an item (`run_key`, `version`, `item_key`, `arm`, `run_index`, `metrics_json`, `cost_usd`, `seconds`). `schedule_eval_verdicts`: one paired comparison per metric and roster-size band (`metric`, `arm_a`, `arm_b`, `band_lo`/`band_hi`, `weeks`, `restaurants`, `diff`, `ci_lo`/`ci_hi`, `winner` — named only when the 90% interval excludes zero in the metric's better direction AND from at least `MIN_VERDICT_WEEKS` (20) weeks of `MIN_VERDICT_RESTAURANTS` (5) learning-eligible restaurants, `method`, `tag` — a verdict for a kind of week (`ROUTE_TAGS`: holiday, closure, first_week, many_rules, escalated) instead of a band, `note` — why no winner was named; both added 10/11/26); the pre-router reads the newest T4-vs-T3 verdict on `pre_repair_defects` (hard breaches plus unmet items in the arm's own rows, before the repair — re-audit 10/11/26 AI-3/AI-4) for the roster's band (`schedule_eval.route_evidence`) and for each kind of week (`schedule_eval.tag_evidence`, AI-27). `freeze` takes no demo, test or internal account (`models.learning_eligible`, AI-5). None of the three is in the retention registry: the set is frozen on purpose — a replay's baseline must outlive the 90 days `schedule_model_calls` is kept (AI-6; `tests/test_sf10_e_eval.py` holds them out of `ops._RETENTION_DAYS`). Created at boot by `schedule_eval.init_schedule_eval` (from `init_db`).

### `schedule_recommendation_events` / `schedule_pattern_dismissals` / `staff_first_seen`
The accept/dismiss ledger for recommendations; the owner's dismissed learned patterns (`schedule_pattern_dismissals.employee` / `person_id`, re-audit 9/29/26: in `people.NAME_STORES`, so a rename or merge re-keys the dismissal with the pattern; `authority` / `adopted_by` / `adopted_at`, schedule audit 10/3/26 L-10: an admin's view-as dismissal is kept but counts only once the account holder adopts it — the owner dismissing the same key counts at once; NULL from before is read as it always was); and the earliest date and most shifts ever seen per name, so tenure survives the rolling upload window. `staff_first_seen.last_seen` (Benchmarking audit BM4-8, boot ALTER in `init_schedule_intel`) is the newest shift date ever seen per name — `remember_tenure` keeps the MAX, so an older upload never makes a current employee look departed; NULL until the next upload. Restaurant DNA's retention dimension reads it. `schedule_recommendation_events.authority` (9/29/26) is whose showing or answer the row is: only the OWNER's count toward suppressing a kind. A kind shown `SUPPRESS_AFTER_SHOWN` (10) times and never accepted, or declined "not for us" twice, is held as durable state in `rec_kind_states` (family `schedule`, `schedule_intel.suppression_state`) with a review date `SUPPRESS_REVIEW_DAYS` (60) out and a `RETEST_WINDOW_DAYS` (14) re-test — it used to be recounted from this log, which is pruned at 365 days; kinds about whether a shift is safe to run (`shift_quality.PROTECTED_REC_KINDS`) are never suppressed.

### `staff_settings` (extended) / `shift_change_requests` (extended)
`time_windows` (per-weekday earliest/latest), `certifications`, and the employee's own `preferred_dayparts` and `desired_hours`. Shift requests carry `kind` (drop | swap) and the target shift for a swap.
`minor_age_band` (NS5 H4, 9/24/26) — `'14-15'`, `'16-17'` or NULL, added at boot by `init_staff_settings`. Picks the minor rule table `schedule_rules.minor_rules` applies as HARD rules (the federal floor for 14-15: no start before 7am, finish by 7pm — 9pm June 1 to Labor Day — 3h on a school day, 18h in a school week, 8h/40h otherwise; `JURISDICTION_MINOR_RULES` tightens it per pack and is deliberately empty until a value is checked at source). Setting a band marks the person a minor; switching `is_minor` off clears it. A minor with no band keeps the generic `minor_latest_end`/`minor_max_daily_hours` rule and carries a soft `minor_age_unknown` flag.
The owner's scheduling facts about a person (schedule audit 10/3/26 F1; boot ALTERs in `init_staff_settings`, written by `staff_settings.upsert`, each change in `change_log` as a roster field, read by `schedule_rules.build_constraints`): `floor_manager` (INTEGER: 1 runs the floor, 0 never, NULL automatic — their role, a held role or a manager role worked in the last 8 weeks, or the `manager` certificate, now labelled "Floor manager (can run the shift)"; a department manager's title and the separate `food_protection_manager` certificate never count — `schedule_rules.manager_basis`); `paid_hourly` (INTEGER: an Owner-role person held to the overtime line; unset, an Owner role is salaried-style); `acting_manager` (JSON `[{"from", "until", "note"}]`, ISO dates — `Constraints.acting_managers`); `standing_shifts` (JSON `[{"day", "start", "end", "role"}]`, one weekday each, 1-16h, no overlap — `Constraints.standing_shifts`); `trainee` (JSON `{"target_role", "trainer", "from", "until"}` or NULL — a training shift is no coverage; `Constraints.trainees`); `closes_for` (JSON list of roles a person marked to close closes for; empty is their own role — `Constraints.closers_by_role`); `work_days` (JSON list of weekdays they work with the hours left to the draft — "any time" in the managers' question, 10/9/26; `Constraints.work_days`, which counts a manager's days as known and leans the split toward those days); `doubles` (INTEGER: 1 works doubles — two legs a date with a break, 0 never, NULL their own history decides — `Constraints.doubles_ok`, blind re-audit 10/11/26 LOGIC-19; a minor never). `floor_manager`, `acting_manager` and `paid_hourly` are the account owner's to set.

### On Postgres (db_dialect)

The same tables and columns, built by the same boot (`models.boot_conn`), with Postgres's types (`BIGINT`, `DOUBLE PRECISION`, `BYTEA`; CREATE TABLE and ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN alike). Additions that exist only there: the `sqlite_master` view and the functions `cavnar_ts`, `cav_name_key`, `cavnar_json_array` (`migrations/postgres/0000_sqlite_compat.sql`); the triggers' plpgsql form (`pg_copy.trigger_statements`: the tracker change stamp, the intel features version, `guest_consent_events` and `intel_pattern_history` append-only); and a `rowid BIGINT GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY UNIQUE` column on every table with no integer primary key (`db_dialect._with_rowid` — SQLite's stable rowid, which the app reads rows by; left out of a `SELECT *` that does not name it). Indexes on a nullable column are built in SQLite's NULL order (NULLS FIRST ascending, LAST descending) so they serve the translated ORDER BY.

### `schedule_history` (extended)
`quality_score`, `quality_band`, `quality_confidence` (the headline, so the list never parses the blob), `what_if_json`, `superseded_by` (a draft replaced by a newer draft of the same week), `republished_at` (the changes to a sent week were sent — `schedule_publish.send_changes`, the explicit Send after a save; a save alone tells nobody since F2-2. Who is still owed a notice is computed, not stored: `schedule_versions.unsent_changes` diffs each person's shifts in the published copy against the working copy — the people taken off the week included). `detail_thinned_at` (9/29/26): when the nightly prune thinned a superseded, never-published, never-shared draft's `quality_json` / `review_json` to their headline and cleared `what_if_json` (*Retention*); the CSV, economics and score columns stay. `hours_hourly` / `hours_salaried` (schedule audit 10/3/26 E-7, P-6): the week's hours split by pay — `hours_hourly` is what the hourly `hours_budget` is spent from, `hours_salaried` the salaried people's hours (`models.salaried_staff`), which it never is; `hours_scheduled` stays the all-in total. Written by `save_schedule_history` (the generation passes its split, else it is worked out from the rows through `schedule_rules.hours_split`), kept current by `schedule_versions.write_on` on every edit, cover and swap, and filled for older rows at boot (`models.backfill_history_hours`, bounded). The publish gate recomputes the split from the rows as they stand; History's line, Home, the phone's receipt, the activity feed and Ask compare `hours_hourly` (else `hours_scheduled`, `models.history_hourly`) to the budget. The generation passes the split of the rows it saves, worked out with the week's own rules. `stage_seconds_json` / `total_seconds` (schedule audit 10/3/26 P-24): how long each stage of the generation took — `inputs`, `model`, `parse`, `rules`, `signals`, `repair` and `repair.<stage>` for every stage of the repair loop, `sweep`, `score`, `save` — and `total`, end to end; written with the draft by `save_schedule_history` and the save's own seconds added right after (`schedule_engine._store_stage_seconds`) (boot columns in `init_db` and `_ensure_history_columns`). Since the 10/4/26 re-audit (PIPE-7) `review_json`, `generation_seconds`, `weather_json`, `economics_json` and the timings are written in the same transaction as the row itself and the superseding of the week's other unsent drafts — the review was a second commit, and a failed one left a draft in force that published without its partial-week and failed-stage blockers; a redo's save also checks, inside that write, that the draft it was built from is unchanged (`base`, `models.DraftChanged` — PIPE-2); `ops.schedule_generation_latency` reads the recent totals for the platform check's p95. `review_json` (10/3/26, P-26, P-15, P-3): besides the sweep's counts and lines, the generation's own review — `fixes` (each with its row's final `index`, its `row_id` and the `row` it describes), `unfixed` and `needs_review` (from the final sweep), `stage_failures` (`{stage, tier, blocks_publish, error, line}`), `input_problems` (an unreadable opening or closing time is one, with `hours: {day, which, value}` — BUGSGEN-20), `inputs_missing`, `inputs_held` (the safety-relevant inputs the draft was made without — `schedule_engine.input_class`, ARCH-25 — named in a "⚠" lead line that holds the publish), `repair` (cycles, settled, refusals, the budget reconcile), `manager_coverage` (each stretch `left` carries its own `gap_start`/`gap_end` minutes on the business timeline beside its words, so a stretch after midnight is still found after a Studio edit — BUGSGEN-10), `min_hours`, and which lines are the sweep's (`rule_lines`), the budget's (`budget_lines`) and the generation's own leading ones (`lead_lines`). A Studio save keeps the generation's part and refreshes only what the edited rows decide (`schedule_engine.refresh_review`), marking `edited`.

### `restaurants.staff_request_policy_json`

`TEXT`, JSON (added 10/10/26, `ensure_columns`; schedule fix round MARKET-14/15). The restaurant's staff-request policy (`request_autonomy.policy`, over `DEFAULTS`): `auto_approve` (bool, default off — drops and swaps that pass every rule approve themselves, once earned), `kinds` (`["drop", "swap"]`), `notice_hours` (24 — closer to the shift a manager decides), `max_quality_drop` (5 — the most the touched shifts' Shift Quality may fall), `availability_approval` (bool, default on — an employee's cut in availability waits for a manager). NULL = the defaults. Written by `POST labor/request-policy` (SCHEDULE_PUBLISH); a tracked `change_log` field ("Staff request approvals"). Since J2 (MARKET-25) also `auto_offer` (bool, default off — an open shift is offered by name to its best legal fit first, once the managers' own named offers earned it; `request_autonomy.auto_offer_open`, its reasons on `auto_reasons_json`).

### `restaurants.floor_settings_json`

`TEXT`, JSON (added 10/10/26, `ensure_columns`; schedule fix round J2, MARKET-7/8/24). The owner's switches for the schedule on the floor (`floor_settings.get`, over `DEFAULTS`): `pos_push` (bool, default off — each published week is written to the POS time clock, `pos_schedule_push`; turned on only from the preview, and only for a POS with a schedule write), `pos_push_on_at` / `pos_push_on_by`, `live_moves` (bool, default on — the live labor read suggests cuts, extensions and call-ins), `autopilot` (bool, default off — an extension or call-in the managers have approved often enough is offered to the person without the tap; a cut never), `live_target_margin_pts` (1 — how far the projected labor % may run over the target before a cut is looked for). NULL = the defaults. Written by `POST labor/floor-settings` (SCHEDULE_PUBLISH); a tracked `change_log` field ("Schedule on the floor (POS push, live labor)").

### `pos_schedule_pushes`, `pos_schedule_push_shifts` (J2, 10/10/26 MARKET-7)

One row per push of a published week to the POS (`pos_schedule_push`): `history_id`, `week_start`, `provider`, `status` (`queued` → `sending` → `sent` | `partial` | `unchanged` | `failed` | `refused` | `held_local`), `reason` (`publish` | `manual`), `payload_hash` (the body sent — the same body is never sent twice), `shifts_total` / `shifts_sent`, `error`, `attempts`, `next_try_at` (a POS-side failure retried at 10, 30, 120 minutes; never an "isn't enabled for this token"; a row a dead process left `sending` longer than `SENDING_LEASE_MINUTES` (15, judged on `updated_at`, the claim's time) goes back to `failed`, due at once while its tries last — scale audit 10/19/26 A5-F15), `actor`, `people_json` (who was on the body — a person missing from a corrected week is listed `removed_unconfirmed`), `created_at`, `sent_at`. Each shift's outcome in `pos_schedule_push_shifts`: `push_id`, `shift_key` (the row's `shift_id` where it has one), `employee`, `role`, `start_at` / `end_at` (local wall clock), `status` (`sent`, `would_send`, `skipped`, `problem`, `started`, `salaried`, `unchanged`, `failed`, `removed_unconfirmed`), `reason`. Boot DDL `pos_schedule_push.init_pos_schedule_push`; retention 400 days (*Retention*).

### `live_labor_moves`, `live_labor_reads` (J2, 10/10/26 MARKET-8/24)

`live_labor_moves`: each move the live labor read suggested, once per `move_key` — `business_date`, `kind` (`cut` | `extend` | `call_in`), `employee`, `role`, `start_label` / `end_label`, `hours`, `dollars` (wages; a cut's saving), `premium` (predictability pay a law makes it owe, where priced), `text`, `payload_json`, `status` (`suggested` → `pushed` → `acted` | `offered` | `dismissed` | `reversed`), `how` (`manager` | `auto`), `request_id` (the open-shift request an extension or call-in became), `pushed_at`, `decided_at` / `decided_by`, `reversed_at`. The managers' own approvals of a kind earn autopilot for it (60 days since the last lapse, `labor_live.trust`). `live_labor_reads`: the newest live read per business date (`body_json`), what the screens show between the 15-minute runs. Boot DDL `labor_live.init_labor_live`; retention 400 / 30 days.

### `cross_site_travel`, `cross_site_offers` (J2, 10/10/26 MARKET-16)

`cross_site_travel` (`restaurant_id` = the person's home site, `name_key`, `employee_name`, `source` `staff` | `owner`, `opted_in_at`): who picks up shifts at the group's other locations — nobody is offered one otherwise. `cross_site_offers`: an open shift at `target_restaurant_id` (`request_id`) offered to a person from `home_restaurant_id`; `status` `offered` → `accepted` | `declined` | `withdrawn`. A yes is judged again at the target and written into its live week (`cross_site.respond`). Boot DDL `cross_site.init_cross_site`; `cross_site_offers` retention 730 days.

### `restaurants.dedicated_shifts_json`

`TEXT`, JSON (added 10/9/26, `ensure_columns`). A restaurant's standing positions (`schedule_dedicated`): `[{"label", "family", "parts": {"morning"|"night": [days]}, "waive_words", "since"?}]` — one person of the role family on every listed daypart and day, whatever the forecast says. Simple EJ's: `Bar tables 201–205`, one more bartender every night and Saturday and Sunday daytime (Anthony). The plan places and pins one row per covered slot before the model writes. A position the restaurant has been running is one of its role's usual crew — the punches the usual crew is read from already count that person — so the shift's requirement is the usual crew, at least the position (`requirement_adjustments`: `delta` 0, `at_least` 1); only a position whose optional `since` (ISO, when the restaurant started running it) is within `IN_USUAL_DAYS` (28) of the week is one MORE on top (a firm +1) (sched audit 10/10/26 LOGIC-16: EJ's usual Friday bartenders already include bar tables). The person is matched by role FAMILY, never a role name that contains it (BUGSGEN-24), and chosen as a GM would: last week's person in that slot (a history row carrying the position's note), then whoever usually works the slot, then the fewest hours in the payroll week counting the managers' plan and every fixed row (LOGIC-17); overtime and the restaurant's limits only when nobody in the role can inside them (`FILL_ROUNDS`) (`schedule_skeleton.plan_for_generation`, `_pinned: "dedicated"`, note `<label> — Cavnar AI: standing position`, which staff read as the label); a slot nobody can legally take is said in the review; a note sentence naming the position with an allowance word (`no`, `skip`, `without`, `don't need`, `waive`) waives the slots it names, for the restaurant notes or the week's "Anything for this week?". Set on prod by Will; no owner screen edits it yet.

### `restaurants.compliance_json` / `restaurants.role_floors_json` and the new columns
`jurisdiction` (a compliance pack code — the state; kept in step with the profile's state), `compliance_profile_json` (schedule audit 10/10/26 RM-15: `{"state", "city", "employees", "locations", "employees_here", "kind": "full_service" | "fast_food" | "other", "franchise", "confirmed_at", "confirmed_by"}` — what `compliance_packs.evaluate` decides coverage on: a state's rules hold every restaurant in it, an ordinance only where the restaurant is in its place and meets its size thresholds; `employees`/`locations` are the whole business's, worldwide), `school_calendar_json` (LEARNING-18: `{"district", "years": [{"start", "end", "breaks": [{"start", "end", "label"}]}], "hours": {"start", "end"}?, "updated_at", "updated_by"}` — `school_calendar`; a date no year covers falls back to every weekday a school day except June 1 to July 31; `hours` are the bell times a 14-15 year old may not work inside on a school day, 7:30am-3:30pm when unset — sched re-audit 10/11/26 STRESS-5), `role_arrival_json` (`{"Server": 5}` — minutes before their own shift start a role clocks in; `attendance.clock_in_leads`), `role_requirements_json`, `foh_roles_json`, `patio_roles_json`, `role_cross_training_json` (`{"Server": 40}` — the % of a role on a shift that should cover a second station; unset roles take `shift_quality.CROSS_TRAINING_DEFAULTS`), `trim_to_budget`, `reservation_provider`, `reservation_api_key`. Schedule audit 10/3/26 F1 (boot ALTERs in `ensure_columns`, scheduling settings in `change_log`): `role_families_json` (`{"Server AM": "Server"}` — the owner's map of job codes to roles; a code left out is its name without the daypart words, `shift_quality.role_family`; the section cap, a role's certificates, the stays-after-close rule, the closers, the owner's rules and cross-training read roles by family), `closer_roles_json` (`["Bartender", "Server"]` — the roles with chosen closers; unset, every role somebody is marked to close for), `salaried_cap` (REAL hours, 20-84 — a salaried person's weekly cap with no limit of their own; NULL reads as `schedule_rules.SALARIED_CAP_DEFAULT`, 55). `role_criticality_json` (`{"Dishwasher": 1.6}` — how hard each role is to run without, the solver's weight on a half hour short of it, 0.3-3.0; unset roles take `schedule_objective.ROLE_CRITICALITY` moved by the restaurant's record — blind re-audit 10/11/26 LOGIC-24; `Constraints.role_criticality`). `role_close_min_json` and `role_close_buffer_json` are one "stays until close + N min" setting per role since 10/3/26 (D-43): the rules screen writes both; read, the stay is the must-stay value where set, else the allowance (`schedule_rules.role_close_stays`), and the close-time clamp allows the larger (`role_close_caps`, `models.get_role_close_buffers`); a role with neither set stays as long as its closing punches show, else the stated default (the dish pit and a role's closers 45 minutes — `Constraints.learned_stays`, `schedule_objective.shoulder_habits`, LOGIC-11), and a restaurant open around the clock has no close cap (STRESS-4).
The scheduling rules (`schedule_rules.DEFAULTS` keys) and the per-role, per-daypart staffing floors — the setting that replaced the compiled-in pizza-cook rule. Since NS5 (9/24/26): `notice_days` is enforced (a week published with less notice is a `notice_short` blocker — auto-publish never sends it, a person must acknowledge it), `leaders_set_own_hours` (default on — since 10/11/26, ARCH-22, a setting instead of a rule compiled in from one client: on, the shift-length maximum holds no owner or manager and the hours rules hold no owner or salaried manager; off, they are held like everyone), `keyholder_until_close` (default on) makes the closer rule hard — since 10/3/26 per role (D-9): for each role with closers, on each trading day it works, its last person out is one of its closers and stays until close plus the role's minutes; a day none of its closers can work is the soft `closer_unavailable` — and the floors are a hard `coverage_floor` rule. `nobody_at_close` is hard when the day's close time is known. Since 10/2/26 the highest rule (owner: "ZERO shifts without ONE manager. Always."): every minute anybody is on, a manager is on — a roster person the owner set as a floor manager (`staff_settings.floor_manager`), else one whose role, held role or recent role names a floor manager or owner (`schedule_rules.is_manager_role`: owner, manager or supervisor of no single department, GM, AGM, MOD, shift lead) or who holds the floor manager certificate, or somebody standing in on that date (`acting_manager`) — whatever role their row is in. A stretch without one is the hard `no_manager` — except the two ends of the day the owner said are not gaps: the closing manager stays until the last person leaves, so the end of the night from their scheduled end is covered for up to `CLOSING_MANAGER_STAYS_MINUTES` (60, 10/9/26), and the prep before an opening manager who is in at least `OPENING_MANAGER_LEAD_MINUTES` (60) before the open on file is not a gap (both per restaurant since 10/11/26, see below) (`schedule_rules.opening_prep`, 10/10/26: Erik, EJ's only Tuesday manager, is in an hour before the 11am open; the kitchen's 8:45am prep before him is fine) — the manager plan (`schedule_skeleton`) does not chase or report that stretch either. Both allowances are the owner's verdict only (sched audit 10/10/26 BUGSGEN-4): the closing stay only at the end of the day's last staffed block, and no repair pass may shorten a manager into either (the judges hold the raw minutes, `manager_facts`); the assumed stay is said per day with a one-tap to write it in, and priced for an hourly manager (`assumed_stays`, TRUST-21); a roster with no manager at all is the soft `no_manager_roster` (nothing could satisfy it, so it never holds every week). It is not a setting and cannot be switched off. Since the schedule fix round (10/3/26, A2): `min_shift_hours` (1-12; `None` = the 3h default since 10/11/26 — `schedule_rules.min_shift_minutes`, contract #2, BUGSGEN-6) — the one shortest shift every pass that cuts or adds holds (lowered for a minor to the law's day, `shortest_for`), a shorter one the soft `shift_too_short`. Since 10/11/26 (LOGIC-12, LOGIC-13): `closing_manager_stays_minutes` and `opening_manager_lead_minutes` (0-180 minutes, 0 turns the allowance off, unset = 60, Simple EJ's), `second_manager_from_staff` (0-80: a second manager on once this many others are on — the soft `second_manager`; 0 never; unset = the line the restaurant's own punches show, `learn_second_manager`, else none); a rules save that does not send it keeps the stored value — since the re-audit of 10/4/26 (UI-2) every rule does: a save merges key by key (`schedule_rules.merged_compliance`), `rules_default` puts a rule back to its default, and the per-role JSON columns (`role_floors_json`, `role_arrival_json`, `role_requirements_json`, `role_cross_training_json`, `role_close_min_json`, `role_close_buffer_json`) merge role by role (`merge_role_map`). A person's `max_hours` above `weekly_hours_ceiling` allows them those hours, overtime included; a salaried person with no maximum of their own is scheduled by code to at most `SALARIED_HOURS_CAP` (55h) a week unless the restaurant's salaried cap says otherwise. New soft kinds: `payroll_tail_full` (a payroll week that runs into next week is at the overtime line by Sunday) and `shift_too_short`; `minor_hours` and `daily_ot` are summed over every leg of the day.
`full_time_min_hours` (10/3/26, D-41; default 30, 0 turns it off): a full-timer with no minimum of their own has this one (`schedule_rules._person_settings`, never a salaried person) — `Constraints.full_time_default` names them, the prompt and the `under_min_hours` flag say "full-time" — but only once the owner confirms it: `full_time_min_confirmed` (bool, default false, sched audit 10/10/26 LOGIC-29; the rules screen's "Full-time line is a minimum" switch or the setup review's one tap, `POST labor/rules {"rules": {"full_time_min_confirmed": true}}`). Until then the line is the person's wanted hours (`Constraints.full_time_unconfirmed`, `preferred[...]["desired_hours"]`): never filled, protected from the trim or flagged as a minimum. The `_pack["law"]` keys (`compliance_packs.apply_for`) are the values a jurisdiction that COVERS the restaurant sets — Illinois's six days in a row (One Day Rest in Seven), a covered Chicago, Seattle, NYC fast-food, Philadelphia, Oregon, Evanston, Berkeley or Emeryville employer's rest between shifts and notice — kept as law when the owner's own value is at least as strict: `TIER_LEGAL`; the rest are the restaurant's policy (`TIER_POLICY`, below the manager rule and coverage). Rest is no law in Illinois outside Chicago (schedule audit 10/10/26 MARKET-3, J1).
**A floor's unit** (schedule audit 10/10/26, contract #2): a `role_floors_json` number is people of the role's family **on at once** at every half hour of the shift's core window (11am–2:30pm, 5:30–8:30pm, clipped to the hours on file; a family's job codes' floors add up) — the `coverage_floor` test (`shift_quality.floor_shortfall`). Floors set before then as a count of different people (Simple EJ's 10/10/26) are translated once by `scripts/crew_floors_on_at_once.py` (`crew_matrix.floors_on_at_once`, never raising one).

### `restaurants.crew_answers_json`
`TEXT`, JSON (added 10/10/26, `ensure_columns`; `change_log` "Crew answers"). The Studio's Crew answers (`crew_matrix`), keyed `"role|Weekday|part"` (part `morning`|`night`): `{"answer": "hold"|"lean", "unit": "concurrent", "usual": people on at once when answered, "at", "by", "review": ISO date it asks again}`; a `hold` also carries `floor_set` (the minimum it wrote) and `floor_before` (`{"present", "value"}` — that day's exact override before, an explicit 0 included, which Undo restores only while the floor is still `floor_set`); a `lean` carries `accepted` (`{"held", "peak", "people"}` — the draft's crew, which `crew_matrix.bind_answers` holds the shift's usual crew to in `labor.staffing_baseline` until `review` or until the usual moves) and `history_id`. Written with `role_floors_json` as one compare-and-swap on `row_version` (`update_restaurant(..., expected_version=)`). An answer from before 10/10/26 (no `unit`; `floor_before` a number) still undoes; a lean with no `accepted` binds nothing and asks again.



### Inventory system columns (9/26/26, `inventory_sync.py`)
- `restaurants.backoffice_api_key` (encrypted at rest, `credentials.FIELDS`), `restaurants.backoffice_account_id` — Back Office (Buyers Edge) credentials, entered by Cavnar, never the owner.
- `client_data.inventory_source` now also names a connected inventory system (`backoffice`); `client_data.inventory_synced_at` (UTC, when its data last landed) and `client_data.inventory_sync_error` (why the last fetch failed). `inventory_sync.status()` reads "synced" only when the connected provider is the source AND a sync time exists.
- `ingredients.external_ref` — the ingredient's id in the inventory system; a sync matches by it first, then by name. A synced count is a `recount` event with `source` = the provider, one per ingredient and day.
- `restaurants.gbp_rating` / `gbp_review_count` / `gbp_rating_updated_at` are now also written from the Places details call every competitor refresh makes (`competitor._remember_own_listing`) — the public Google rating, for a restaurant without a Business Profile connection. The columns are overwritten in place, so since 9/29/26 every writer of `gbp_rating` (`competitor._remember_own_listing`, `gmb.fetch_location_rating`) also keeps the week's reading in `own_rating_history` (*Events and public history*).
### `restaurants.cut_floor_default`
`INTEGER DEFAULT 2`, 1..10 (added 9/24/26, `ensure_columns`). "Never cut a role below N people": the fewest people any cut or send-home suggestion leaves in a role **with no floor of its own**. `schedule_rules.cut_floor(restaurant, role, day, daypart)` is the one reader, and its order is fixed: the owner's `role_floors_json` floor for that role, day and daypart; else the admin `role_minimums_json` entry for the role (case-insensitive); else this column (2 when missing or unreadable, `response_validation.CUT_FLOOR_DEFAULT`); never below 1. Read by the pre-dinner pulse (`strategy_jobs.staffing_move`) and, through `schedule_rules.cut_policy` → `ValidationContext.policy["cut_floor_default"]`, by A2 on Ask, labor insights, schedule notes, the weekly plan and the digest. **Cuts only:** schedule building, `shift_requirements` and the `coverage_floor` violation never read it, so a published week with one bartender raises nothing new. Owners set it in Labor → Schedule rules (web and iOS); Will sets it on the admin client settings page.

### `staff_capabilities`
The Operational Score layer. `(restaurant_id, employee_name, attribute)` unique — `attribute` is things like `overall`, `can_close`, per-role scores. `score` (1–5 scale) or `flag` (boolean), `notes`, `updated_by`. Unrated is absent, never a row with a zero score — a name only appears here once someone has actually rated them. Whose judgement it is (memory re-audit 9/29/26, PEOPLE-15; boot ALTERs in `init_staff_capabilities`): `updated_by_user_id` (the admin behind a view-as, never the owner it views as), `authority` (`permissions.answer_authority`) and `via` (`view_as`) — stamped by `models.set_capability(user=)` or from the request's login. An `admin` rating is shown and **not** counted in the Operational Score (`get_operational_scores`) until someone at the restaurant rates them.
A score for one role (schedule audit 10/3/26 D-12) is the attribute `role:<family>` — `models.role_score_attribute` ("Server AM" and "Server PM" are `role:server`), a score like `overall`, counted by the same rule (`models.get_role_scores`); the scorer (`shift_quality.build_contexts`) and the solver (`schedule_solver.Problem._sc`) read it for a row in that role, the overall score otherwise. A counted rating older than `models.RERATE_AFTER_DAYS` (90) is due a re-rate (`models.rating_ages`: "Rated 6/1/26 — still right?"; `capability_coverage.due_for_rerate`) and keeps counting.

### `capability_changes`
Audit trail of every capability edit (who changed what score, when) — feeds the "why is this schedule different" explanation path. Who, as data (PEOPLE-15; boot ALTERs in `init_capability_changes`): `changed_by_user_id` (the admin behind a view-as), `authority`, `via` — beside the `changed_by` text (`support:<admin> (as <owner>)` through view-as).

### `staff_availability`
Days an employee has said they can't work, submitted from the staff app or their own schedule link — the AI scheduler treats this as equal priority to hard staff constraints. Employee-app fix round (10/2/26, M5): `day_bounds` (JSON) holds the off days that apply only between two dates (`{"Friday": {"from": null, "until": "2026-12-15"}}`); `unavailable_days` still lists every blocked weekday, dated ones included, so a reader that knows nothing of dates errs toward "can't work". The week-aware readers (`schedule_rules.build_constraints` — a dated block becomes `blocked_dates` for that week's dates inside it — the labor prompt's EMPLOYEE AVAILABILITY block, which lists the stored available days less this week's blocked ones and never invents availability, the engine's coverage top-up and `get_unavailability_map(week_dates=)`) read rows through `models.availability_blocked_days` / `availability_blocked_dates` / `bounds_apply`. `updated_at` is the record's version: a microsecond stamp that always moves forward (`models.availability_stamp`), sent back by the app and the `/s/` link on every save (`staff_settings.save_own_availability`: a moved version is a 409), and moved by an owner's change to the person's time windows too (`models.touch_staff_availability`). Hours windows ("not before 5pm on Tuesdays") are the owner's `staff_settings.time_windows`, which gain optional `from` / `until` (`schedule_rules.window_holds`) — one store, written by both.

### `shift_sections` / `restaurants.foh_sections_json` (V12, 10/2/26)
A server's floor section on one shift: `date`, `employee_key` (`staff_settings.name_key`), `employee_name`, `shift_start` (normalised `HH:MM`), `section`, `updated_by`, `updated_at`; UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, date, employee_key, shift_start)`. Its own table, not a `schedule_csv` column (the CSV's eight columns are read by position in places, and a section has to survive a re-publish). The names come from `restaurants.foh_sections_json` (the owner's list, in order); a name removed from the list disappears everywhere. A section follows a shift that changes hands — `models.carry_shift_sections`, called by `schedule_versions.write_on` inside the writer's transaction (one person left a slot and one arrived; two changes in one slot are left alone). `authority` (`ensure_columns`, schedule audit 10/3/26 L-22): whose word the assignment is (`models._write_attribution` — an admin's through view-as or support is `admin`); the scheduling memory learns each server's usual section from 24 weeks of these and never counts an admin's. In `people.NAME_STORES`; retention 730 days on `date` (floor 168).

### `staff_contacts` / `staff_notes`
Contact info for schedule delivery; freeform per-employee scheduling constraints. `staff_notes` is one row per person (`UNIQUE(restaurant_id, employee_name)`, matched case-insensitively): a second constraint is APPENDED to the first, joined with "; " (`models.save_staff_note`) — it used to be written over it (fix round #142). A removal is recorded with its restaurant and text and can be undone (`replace=True` puts back exactly what was). Since 9/29/26 (`init_staff_notes`): `parts_json` holds the person's constraints one by one (`[{text, noted, expires, confirmed, by}]`) — an end date belongs to ONE constraint, never to the row — beside `updated_at`, `updated_by`, `expires_on`, `confirmed_at`; `notes` stays the joined text older readers and the admin page read. A constraint ends on its `expires` date or the date its own words give ("out until 6/1", `models.staff_note_expiry`), after which every reader leaves it out (the admin page keeps it, marked); one nobody has confirmed in `STAFF_NOTE_STALE_DAYS` (90) is shown to the owner as "still true?" and stays in force until they answer.

### `labor_history` / `labor_daily_history`
Per-period and per-day labor % / sales / hours — the source for the Labor ribbon chart and Ask Cavnar's "how is today going" answer.

Invariants (CA3 F3):
- `labor_daily_history.sales` is the day's NET sales by business date (`pos.py`'s sales contract), or **NULL when the day has no figure** — never 0. `labor_pct` is NULL whenever `sales` is. `models.save_labor_daily_history` writes NULL for a missing figure and never lets a NULL overwrite a figure already on file for that date (the day keeps its sales, re-costed against the new labor). Readers filter `sales IS NOT NULL AND sales > 0` — rows from before this rule carry 0 for a missing figure, and the one-time boot backfill `models.backfill_missing_sales_null` (idempotent; data only) rewrites those to NULL when labor was on the books and no POS evidence of a sale exists that date (`dsr_metrics sales.net`, `pos_intraday`, `menu_item_sales`). A $0 row with POS evidence is left for a person.
- `labor_history` holds a period only when it had sales (`save_labor_snapshot` refuses `total_sales <= 0`), and `get_labor_history` never returns a legacy `total_sales = 0` row — a 0.0% "previous upload" is not a period. The labor alert (`notify.check_daily_alerts`) also needs the period to be at least `LABOR_ALERT_MIN_PERIOD_DAYS` (7) long.
- Provenance (Data Freshness fix round, DH1-9 / DH2-3): `labor_daily_history.source` (the writer — a provider name for a POS pull), `provider`, `synced_at` (UTC) and `final` (boot ALTERs). A POS pull (`pos.save_synced_shifts` → `save_labor_daily_history(provenance=)`) stamps the days inside its window; a day past `pos.complete_through` — the last business day that had ENDED when it was read (its close from the restaurant's hours, else the 5am boundary) — is `final=0`, provisional, until a later pull re-reads it whole. NULL (older rows, uploads) reads as final. `data_freshness` dates `pos`, `sales` and archive-dated `labor` by final days only (`COALESCE(final, 1) = 1`), so "Sales through" always names a whole day. Days outside the pulled window keep their provenance.
- Learners read final days through ONE predicate (memory audit 9/29/26): `canonical_facts.FINAL_SQL` / `final_sql(alias)` — `COALESCE(final, 1) != 0` AND `models.own_history_sql()`, so a converted demo's seeded day (`source='seed'`) is no final day either (a demo's own screens keep them). The ONE nightly net per business date is `canonical_facts.net_series`, each figure with its basis (`dsr_net` — the report's own net, an imported workbook, or a POS whose daily total is built the same way — or `pos_daily_total`); two figures are compared only on one basis. `labor_daily_history.sales` and `dsr_metrics` `sales.net` are both kept forever where they live and neither is in the retention registry.
- `labor_daily_history.basis` (9/29/26, `ensure_columns`): the costing basis the day was last costed on (the rates, the salaried set, where the labor came from).
- `labor_history` periods (9/29/26, `ensure_columns`): `kind` is `payroll_week` (the only kind the product writes now — calendar payroll weeks derived from `labor_daily_history` by `models.refresh_labor_periods`, kept current `LABOR_PERIOD_WEEKS` (53) back), `period` (one a caller stored explicitly) or `rolling_window` (the legacy overlapping 14/28-day windows every sync used to append — marked once at boot by `models.backfill_labor_periods` and **never read**); `basis`, `days`, `updated_at`, and `recosted_from` (the labor % the row held before a recost on a new basis). Invariant: a period is REPLACED IN PLACE (`save_labor_snapshot`) — a payroll week keyed by its start, an explicit period by start and end — never a second row for the same period, so a recost cannot read as "labor's down 1.6 points from last upload".

### People and shifts (memory audit 9/29/26)
An employee used to BE a display name: a POS rename stranded every rating, setting, note and tenure record. Identity (`people.py`, boot DDL from `init_db`):
- `people` — one row per person per restaurant: `display_name` (the spelling every name-keyed store uses), `name_key` (its `staff_settings.name_key`), `organization_id`, `active`, `merged_into` (a merged person keeps its row, pointing at the survivor), `created_via`. Invariant: **one live person per `name_key`** — the partial UNIQUE index `idx_people_live_key` on `(restaurant_id, name_key) WHERE merged_into IS NULL`.
- `person_aliases` — every way a source has named a person: `(source, external_id)` for a POS's own id — UNIQUE per restaurant, source and external id (`idx_person_alias_ext`) — or `(source, name_key)` for a spelling (`idx_person_alias_spelling`), with `first_seen` / `last_seen`. Ingest (`people.resolve_rows`) resolves by the POS id first, then the exact name key; a known POS id under a new spelling is the same person renamed and is applied (`rename_person`).
- `person_questions` — a possible match ("Kim T." / "Kim Tran") for the OWNER to decide: `person_a`, `person_b`, `kind` (`same_person` / `same_name`), `reason`, `evidence_json`, `status` open → merged / different, `answered_by`, `answered_authority`; UNIQUE per pair and kind. **Nothing similar is ever merged automatically.** A guess from the shift history (a new name that "took over" a departed one) is never asked, and an open one never binds, when the POS gave the two names employee ids of their own — a POS keeps a person's id through a rename — and an open one of those is closed `different` with `answered_authority` `pos` (`people._withdraw_told_apart`, 10/15/26); one departed name hands off to one new name at most (two hires after one departure are two people).
- `person_merges` — every merge and rename (`kind`, `from_person`, `into_person`, the names, `moved_json` — what moved and what was folded — `actor_user_id`, `source`), so either can be read back. Re-audit 9/29/26: a merge's `moved_json` keeps what an undo replays — each moved row's values before, each fold's surviving row as it was and the folded row, the pairs a tidy-up removed, the shift-file rows renamed, and under `_undo` the aliases, questions and salaried entries it touched (a record over 4M characters keeps counts only and cannot be undone); `undone_at` / `undone_by` (boot ALTERs in `init_people`) mark a merge `people.unmerge_people` took back (for `UNMERGE_DAYS`, 30, while neither person has been renamed or merged since), and the undo adds a `kind='unmerge'` row. A merge's `person_quarters` fold sums every count (covers and mentions too), adds the role/daypart/weekday maps and keeps the wider date range. `people.erase_person` (a departed employee's request) deletes every `NAME_STORES` row about them, their shift-file rows, aliases, questions and merge records, and leaves the `people` row as the tombstone `Erased #id`. Since the employee audit (10/2/26) `NAME_STORES` also holds the staff app's name-keyed stores — `shift_offers` (`name` / `name_key`), `staff_running_late`, `staff_announcement_recipients`, `staff_threads` (its `staff_thread_messages` go with it), `staff_notices`, `staff_certs` — and `shift_change_requests.target_name` (a swap asked of them: renamed with them, cleared on an erase); `people.MEMBERSHIP_STORES` are the stores keyed by the login alone (`staff_shift_pulse`, `staff_calendar_links`, `staff_language`, and the login-keyed rows above), whose rows for every login the person held here an erase deletes too.
- `person_id` beside the name on the older name-keyed stores (`people.NAME_STORES`: `staff_settings`, `staff_capabilities`, `staff_availability`, `staff_contacts`, `staff_notes`, `staff_first_seen`, `manual_team_members`, `staff_time_off`, `shift_change_requests`, `schedule_shares`, `memberships`, `capability_changes` — every store in the list but `staff_pairs`; boot ALTERs in `people`, on the stores that exist when it runs — `init_auth` runs after `init_db`, so on a brand-new database `memberships` gains the column at the second boot). The stores keep their names (the display); a merge or rename re-points exactly the `NAME_STORES` list, so a new store that keeps a name belongs in it.

What happened to each person, each with an explicit reader window (the floors in *Retention* are at least the longest):
- `shift_facts` (`shift_facts.py`) — every shift every person worked, one row each: `business_date`, `person_id`, `employee_name` / `employee_key`, `role`, `shift_start` / `shift_end`, `scheduled_hours` (NULL when the source carried no real schedule — RPOWER, Square, Clover and Toast without one: a copied actual is not a schedule), `actual_hours`, `pay_rate`, `source`, `external_id`, `ingested_at`; UNIQUE per restaurant, date, person key and start. ONE ingest (`shift_facts.ingest`) for a POS sync, an owner's upload and an admin's upload, with the POS rule for all of them: **inside the new rows' date range the new rows are the record, outside it everything is kept** (a manual upload used to replace the whole history). `client_data.shifts_csv` is still written for the older readers, and since the re-audit (9/29/26) every ingest trims it to the same window (`shift_facts.blob_edge`). Kept 1095 days, then per person per quarter in `person_quarters`. **Since the scheduler audit of 10/10/26 (SCALE-5) shift_facts is the shift record:** `row_json` holds every field of the row but the date and the name, as the file carried it (sales, notes, the day, pay, the POS's own fields), so `shift_facts.history_rows(rid, since, until)` reads a window back exactly as the file's rows read (`idx_shift_facts_rid_date`), and `labor.shift_history` / `load_shifts_for_restaurant` / the DSR's readers read through it. A restaurant is adopted (`shift_facts.adopt`: every row of its file upserted whole, each fact keeping its person; `client_data.shifts_store` = `facts`) at its next sync or upload, or by the nightly people job (`adopt_pending`, bounded) — only when every row is one fact (a person, a date, no second row of the same date, person and start), else it stays on its file. Adopted, a sync or upload replaces its window in the facts and respells the kept facts to their person, and marks `client_data.shifts_stale`: the three-year `client_data.shifts_csv` is no longer rewritten (measured 250 people x 3 years: the nightly ingest 2.7 s -> 0.8 s; one adoption 10.6 s). The file is an export: `models.get_client_data` rebuilds it from the facts when a reader of the whole file asks and it is stale (`with_shifts=False` reads the row without it and says `has_shifts`). Since 10/14/26 (blind re-audit SCALE-14) an adopted restaurant's export is stored compressed in `client_data.shifts_csv_z` with `shifts_csv` NULL (`models.store_shift_file`; read with `models.shift_file_text`; "has a shift history" is `models.SHIFTS_PRESENT_SQL`) — the plain export duplicated the facts (5.6 MB for two years of a 250-person restaurant); the nightly prune compresses the ones stored before (`ops._compact_shift_exports`). A later sync whose rows a fact cannot hold puts the restaurant back on its file, rebuilt from the facts first (`_release`) — the file keeps what was sent.
- `attendance_events` (`attendance.py`) — one row per scheduled shift somebody watched: `outcome` (`attendance.OUTCOMES`: on_time / late / no_show / called_out / left_early / covered / excused), `minutes_late`, `covered_by`, `source`, `history_id`, `note`, `notice_minutes` (10/3/26, L-18: how far ahead a call-out told the restaurant — read off the drop request it asked and was never approved, `shift_requests.shift_release`; NULL when unknown, e.g. a closer's "who didn't make it"; added at boot by `init_attendance`). `excused` (E-5) is a drop a manager approved that nobody claimed — never a no-show; it and `covered` are not shifts the person owed, so no reader counts them (`attendance.NOT_OWED`); UNIQUE per restaurant, person key, date and scheduled start. Source precedence (`attendance.SOURCE_RANK`, strongest first): `manual` > `closeout_confirmed` > `coverage_check` > `schedule_vs_punch_join` — a weaker source never overwrites a stronger one's outcome. A person nobody watched is absent — unknown — never "reliable". Kept 730 days.
- `person_signals` — something that happened to a person (`people.SIGNAL_KINDS`: `cover_accepted`, `cover_declined`, `review_mention`): `polarity`, `signal_date`, `ref`, `status` proposed / confirmed / rejected, `detail`, `created_by`, `authority` (whose word it is; `system` for what a job inferred); UNIQUE per kind, ref and person key. A guest naming a person is `proposed` until the owner confirms it; a confirmed or rejected signal is never downgraded back to proposed. Kept 730 days.
- `person_roles` — the roles a person holds: `role`, `source` (default `owner`), `qualified_since` ("trained on bar from 9/1"), `is_primary`, `removed_at`; UNIQUE per person key and role. Kept forever.
- `person_quarters` — each person's quarter (shifts, hours, scheduled shifts, roles, dayparts, weekdays, attendance outcomes, covers taken and declined, positive and negative guest mentions, first and last date), PK `(restaurant_id, employee_key, quarter)`, written by `shift_facts.roll_all_quarters` (the rollup the three raw tables declare) while the quarter's raw rows are whole; `shift_facts.tenure` reads it for what the raw rows no longer hold. Kept forever.

### `schedule_shares` (share links)
Public, expiring links a schedule is published to for staff to view (and submit availability against). Expiry is ~60 days out, pushed back on republish; an expired link is flagged rather than serving stale data or vanishing. Employee-app fix round (10/2/26, SEC-09): `token` holds only `sha256:<hex>` of the link (`models._share_hash`; lookup is by hash, and `models.migrate_schedule_shares` hashed the plaintext rows at boot and gave every row with no `expires_at` one 60 days after `sent_at` — a NULL expiry now ages out the same way instead of meaning "never"). Every send writes its own row, and a re-send pushes that person's earlier rows for the week out again, so links already in inboxes keep working; `get_schedule_share_status` folds a person's rows into one. A link follows its week to the live copy (`models.live_week_row`) and shows its PUBLISHED rows — never a manager's unsent edit (10/10/26 ARCH-1). A holder whose logins are all inactive, or whose roster row is, reads as expired; `staff_insights.expire_links_for` ends the links durably on deactivation, unlink and account deletion. `sent_to = 'app'` is written only once the publish push was delivered; `viewed_at` is also stamped when the app shows the week (`mark_schedule_shares_seen_in_app`). In `offsite_backup.KEEP_COLUMNS` (a hash, not the link).

### `staff_time_off`, `covers_daily`, `manual_team_members`, `team_messages`, `task_templates` / `task_completions`
