Time-off requests (owner decides in place); covers per day for the labor-per-cover read (`covers_daily.source`: `manual`; `pos_confirmed` — a POS guest count the owner accepted from `covers.pos_offers`; or, since 9/30/26, `pos` — the night's POS guest count written by `covers.sync_from_pos` when the nightly report's metrics are saved and on the nightly event_memory pass (last 35 nights). `pos` never overwrites `manual` or `pos_confirmed`, and follows a corrected report); team members added by hand where the POS has none; the owner↔staff message threads; and the flat task checklist per job role, carried over (boot, once) into All day task sheets and no longer read by any screen.
`staff_time_off.start_time` / `end_time` / `daypart` (10/3/26, schedule audit D-39; `ensure_columns`): part of each day off — "off until 4pm" (`end_time`), "off from 6pm" (`start_time`), both for a stretch, or `daypart` `morning` (lunch) / `night` (dinner); none of them is the whole day. Times are stored as "4:00pm" (`time_off.clean_part`). Approved, a part is `Constraints.blocked_parts` (`time_off.approved_parts_in_window`) — the rest of the day stays workable; `approved_in_window(..., whole_days_only=True)` leaves them out.

### `task_sheets`, `task_sheet_lines`, `task_assignments`, `task_line_completions`, `task_proof_media`, `task_signoffs` (`task_sheets.py`, 9/30/26)
A **sheet** is one job code × shift kind (`opening` | `closing` | `mid` | `any`), optional `days_of_week` ("0,4"), `requires_signoff`, a `version` bumped by every edit, `migrated_from='task_templates'` for a carried-over list. Its **lines** are ordered (`sort_order`), with an optional `section`, `due_offset_min` (after the shift starts; before it ends for a closing sheet), `proof` (`none` | `photo` | `number` | `note`) with `proof_label` and `min_value`/`max_value`, `critical`, and a soft-delete `active`. An **assignment** is one sheet for one business date (UNIQUE sheet_id, task_date — `time_utils.business_date`, so a closer ticking at 1am is on last night's sheet), issued from the newest PUBLISHED week covering the day: `assignees_json` are the opener (earliest start on that job code), the closer (latest end), everyone on it (`any`) or the ones in between (`mid`); with no published schedule it is `unassigned=1` with the service hours as its window. `lines_json` snapshots the lines and each one's `due_at` as issued, with `sheet_version` — a line added later is not on today's. `status` open → done | partial | missed when the shift has been over `CLOSE_GRACE_MINUTES`. A **completion** is immutable (`completed_by` the login's name, `completed_local` the restaurant's wall clock, `proof_value`, `proof_media_id`, `late`, `flagged` for a number out of range); an un-tick is a new row with `undone=1`, and the newest row per line is its state. `task_proof_media` holds the photos (resized JPEG, never public — served only to this restaurant's signed-in viewers); `task_signoffs` one sign-off per restaurant × business date × shift kind with the sheets as they stood. Misses open `ops_issues` (`task_missed` texted for a critical line past due, `task_sheet` quiet for a sheet left unfinished, `task_pattern` for one line missed 3 times in 14 days by one person). Employee-app fix round (10/2/26, B4): an assignment stores `schedule_sig` — the signature of the published week its people came from (week id, newest `schedule_versions.version`, `edited_at`, `published_at`) — and every read (`ensure_day`) re-resolves the rows still `open` or `none` whose signature moved: assignees, `shift_start` / `shift_end` and the due times follow the live week (a same-day cover gets the sheet; `task_sheets.refresh_assignees` makes it immediate), while the lines stay as issued; a row someone already started keeps its people. `status` adds `none` — nobody on that job code and shift today, recorded so the day settles without re-parsing the CSV, never shown. A tick is refused outside the business day ± 1, and `evaluate` sweeps every open row: rows older than `STALE_DAYS` (3) close quietly (no text, no issue) but still count as misses. `task_proof_media.uploaded_by_user_id` (indexed, `idx_task_media_uploader`) holds the per-login cap (60 photos an hour); a photo is stored only after the sheet is the caller's, with its completion row in one commit. A critical number line read out of range opens a high `task_flag` issue (`taskflag:<assignment>:<line>`, routed and texted like `task_missed`, Labor; one per line and assignment, so a second reading texts nobody again).

### The staff app's stores (employee-app fix round, 10/2/26)
Each has a `restaurant_id`, so `delete_restaurant` removes its rows; each is made at boot (`models.init_db` → `staff_comms.init_staff_comms`, `staff_insights.init_staff_insights`, `staff_knowledge.init_staff_knowledge` — which also makes `staff_briefs` — and `push.init_push` → `staff_reminders.init_staff_reminders`). Rows are keyed by the staff login (`membership_id`), never by a typed name, unless noted.
- `staff_running_late` (`staff_comms`): one row per login, business date and shift start — `eta_minutes` (1–120), `note`, `reported_at`, `updated_at` (a second report replaces the ETA), `told_at` / `told_count` (the deciders are told once). It also writes attendance `late` from the `self_report` source, the weakest (`attendance.SOURCE_RANK` 0): any clock-in reading overwrites it and it never fills a measured row's minutes.
- `staff_announcements` / `staff_announcement_recipients` (`staff_comms`): a manager's note to all staff, one role or one day's crew — `priority` (`normal` | `urgent`), `audience` / `audience_value`, `expires_on` (inclusive), `created_by` / `created_by_name`, `withdrawn_at` / `withdrawn_by` (withdrawing hides it everywhere and keeps the record). The audience is fixed at send: one recipient row per active employee login (`delivered_via`, `delivered_at`, `acked_at` — "Got it", the first stamp stands — and `created_at`, the retention stamp, back-filled from the announcement on an older database). A recipient row goes before its announcement, and an announcement only once none is left (`ops._RETENTION_ONLY`).
- `staff_threads` / `staff_thread_messages` (`staff_comms`): one thread per login with the managers on duty (not `team_messages`, which is one console login to another); each message `sender_kind` (`staff` | `manager`), `sender_user_id`, `sender_name`, `body`, optional context (`shift_date`, `request_id` + `request_kind` `shift` | `time_off`, which must be the employee's own), `read_at` / `read_by` (any manager reading reads it for the team). No staff-to-staff messages.
- `staff_shift_pulse` (`staff_insights`): one rating (1–5) and optional note per login per business date of their own published shift; the owner reads aggregates only, below 3 answers a count.
- `staff_calendar_links` (`staff_insights`): a login's calendar feed — `nonce`, `key_version` (the kept secret `staff_calendar`'s version), `token_hash` (`sha256:` of the HMAC token, UNIQUE; in `offsite_backup.KEEP_COLUMNS`), `revoked_at`, `last_fetched_at`, `fetch_count`. The URL is re-derived from the nonce, never stored. A live link (no `revoked_at`) is never pruned.
- `staff_briefs` (`staff_brief`): one row per restaurant and business date — the day's items (`items_json`, `items_hash`), the one model draft (`draft_text`, `draft_status`, `draft_reason`, `model_attempted_at` — claimed atomically before the call, so at most one a day), the approved text (`approved_text`, `approved_by`, `approved_at`, `edited`) and the focus item (`focus_item`, `focus_line`, `focus_by`, `focus_at`). Approvals and focus changes are also `change_log` rows (entity `staff_brief`).
- `staff_language` (`staff_knowledge`, `membership_id` PK): the language a login reads in (12 codes, default `en`). `staff_translations`: a cache per (restaurant, text hash, language) of manager-approved text — `translated`, `status`, `reason`; a content failure is remembered 24 hours, a budget stop or an open breaker not at all.
- `staff_docs` (`staff_knowledge`): the house rules (one per restaurant, kind `house_rules`) and menu specs, allergens, how-we-do-it notes, training and other reference — `kind`, `title`, `body`, `roles_json` (empty = everyone), `active`. Written by people, shown as written.
- `staff_certs` (`staff_knowledge`): a certificate per (restaurant, `employee_key`, `cert`) with `expires_on`, `issued_on`, `note`, and `reminded_for` / `reminded_at` (one reminder per expiry date; a renewal re-arms it). Name-keyed: in `people.NAME_STORES` (a merge folds on `(employee_key, cert)`, the newest wins). `schedule_rules.build_constraints` subtracts the expired ones (`staff_knowledge.expired_certs`, names normalised by `schedule_rules.cert_key`).
- `staff_notices` (`staff_reminders`): the reminder claims and the texts held overnight — `kind` (`shift_reminder` | `task_reminder` | `held_text`), `claim_key` UNIQUE (one per person + shift or line + kind; a held text's own id), `employee_name` (name-keyed: in `people.NAME_STORES`, so a rename moves a held text), `purpose` (the consent scope a held text needs), `title`, `body`, `meta_json`, `release_at` (UTC, the next 8am), `state` (`claimed` → `sent` | `failed`; `held` → `sending` → `sent` | `failed` | `dropped`), `channel`. A reminder is marked `sent` only on delivery. Pruned by its own job after 30 days (never a held or sending row).

### `shift_profiles`
Demand-level profiles (low/normal/high/peak) per daypart, used by the Shift Quality Engine's demand-match dimension. `config_json` holds the profile as `shift_quality.profile_to_dict` writes it (`profile_from_dict` reads it) — since the schedule fix round (10/3/26, SQ-22, SQ-23) it may carry `floors` (`{dimension key: floor}`, its own critical floors over `shift_quality.CRITICAL_FLOORS`, 0–100, 0 = never caps, unknown dimensions dropped; no migration: a profile without the key reads the defaults) — written by the owner or by an applied calibration. A restaurant with none stored is judged on the built-in set, always re-levelled per weekday from its own sales (`shift_quality.profile_set`, SQ-15). Each stored score's shift `dimensions[]` keeps the `floor` it was held to, which calibration reads.

### Overstaffed days / overtime
Computed on read from `labor_daily_history` + shift data, not a table — see `labor.py`.

### The job queue, the model gateway and Postgres readiness (scheduler audit 10/10/26, workstream H2)

- **`jobs`** (`job_queue`, RM-7): one row per unit of background work — `kind` (`schedule_generate`, `auto_draft`, `learner`), `grp` (`generation` | `bulk` | `pos_io`: the concurrency pool it draws on — `pos_io` the nightly POS archive rows since the scale audit, 10/19/26 INF-6; rows queued before keep `bulk`), `restaurant_id`, `tenant_key` (`org:<id>` for a restaurant in an organization, else `r:<id>` — the fairness unit), `priority_class` (`interactive` | `scheduled` | `bulk`), `state` (`queued`, `running`, `done`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `expired`), `payload_json` (what the handler needs; an owner's press carries the attribution and a credential-free copy of the login), `idempotency_key` UNIQUE, `external_id` (the `async_jobs` id an owner polls), `not_before` / `deadline` / `lease_until` / `enqueued_at` / `started_at` / `finished_at` (epoch seconds), `attempts` / `max_attempts`, `lease_owner`, `stage_checkpoint` (JSON: the stages reached and what a resume needs — a generation's saved payload, compressed), `phase` (a running row: NULL doing code work and holding a CPU slot, `model` waiting on a model answer with its slot given back, `await` back from the model and waiting for a slot — `job_queue.model_wait`, blind re-audit 10/11/26 SCALE-1; `ensure_columns`), `result_json`, `error`, `created_at` (the retention column; nightly bulk and POS rows carry a `deadline` `QUEUE_BULK_DEADLINE_HOURS` past their `not_before` since the scale audit — INF-6), `parked_until` (epoch seconds, added 10/11/26 — a running row waiting on the model gateway's next minute; until then it holds no CPU slot, `job_queue.parked`, SCALE-25). Indexes `(grp, state, not_before)`, `(grp, priority_class, state, tenant_key, id)` (each tenant's head of a class), `(grp, priority_class, state, deadline)` (the scheduled class by deadline), `(grp, state, deadline)` (rows past their deadline), `(restaurant_id, state)`, `external_id`, `created_at`. Claimed in one write transaction after a plain read (`job_queue.claim`). Retention 14 days.
- **`job_tenants`**: per `(tenant_key, klass)` the token bucket (`tokens`, `refilled_at`) and the weighted-fair-queueing virtual time (`vtime`, `served_at`). For the scheduled class the bucket is the restaurant's (`r:<id>`, also for an organization's location — its row's `vtime` unused) and the virtual time the tenant's. One row per class with `tenant_key` `~system` holds the class's system virtual time (self-clocked fair queueing: the start tag of the last row served; a tenant behind it ranks at it). A row unserved for `TENANT_IDLE_DAYS` (7) is pruned by the minute duty (`job_queue.prune_tenants`); the `~system` rows are kept.
- **`job_workers`**: one row per consumer process (`worker_id`, `role` web | worker, `pid`, `host`, `started_at`, `heartbeat_at`, `draining`) — the web process consumes when no `worker` row is fresh. Rows a day silent are deleted by the heartbeat; the web row is beaten every minute (`job_queue.tick`) even while the worker consumes, since a recycled queue worker waits for a web row started since its container did (scale audit 10/19/26 INF-4).
- **`generation_watchers`** (SCALE-24, ARCH-22): `(job_id, user_id)` primary key, `may_publish`, `created_at` — who pressed Generate for a running generation, taken (read and deleted in one transaction) when the draft lands, whichever process finishes it. Was `schedule_engine._GEN_WATCHERS`, process memory. Retention 7 days.
- **`model_token_windows`** / **`model_tenant_windows`** (`model_gateway`, SCALE-32): the organisation's tokens a minute by class (`minute`, `klass`, `input_tokens`, `output_tokens`, `calls`; since 10/19/26, scale audit A4-02, `klass` holds the lane `"<model family>|<class>"` — `model_gateway._lane`, read back by `_split_lane` — so each model family has its own minute, as the provider meters it; a bare class is a row written before, gone within two hours) and one restaurant's (`minute`, `restaurant_id`, `output_tokens`) — reserved before a call, settled to its usage; rows older than two hours are deleted as new minutes are written.
- **`ai_tenant_budgets`**: one restaurant's own AI ceilings (`daily_usd`, `monthly_usd`, `reason`, `set_by`, `updated_at`) over its tier's (`model_gateway.set_tenant_budget`, read by `ai_utils.ai_budget_status`).
- **`ai_breakers`**: one row per provider (`open_until`, `opened_at`, `reason`, `failures`, `updated_at`) — the breaker's transitions shared across processes (`ai_utils._breaker_publish` / `_breaker_sync`).
- **`pg_shadow_outbox`** (`pg_shadow`, RM-13): `(history_id, version)` UNIQUE, `restaurant_id`, `created_at`, `sent_at`, `attempts`, `next_at`, `error` — a schedule revision to write to the Postgres shadow, noted in the version's own transaction while `PG_SHADOW_WRITE` is on. Retention 30 days.
- **Postgres only** (`migrations/postgres/`, applied by `scripts/run_pg_copy.py`): the `sqlite_master` view; the `sched` schema — `schedule_weeks` (working and published revision pointers), `schedule_revisions` (immutable, `UNIQUE (week_id, version)`), `shifts` (`PRIMARY KEY (revision_id, shift_id)`, `person_id` → `people`, `start_ts` / `end_ts` timestamptz, `span` tstzrange, and `EXCLUDE USING gist (revision_id WITH =, person_id WITH =, span WITH &&) WHERE (person_id IS NOT NULL AND published)` — a published revision cannot hold a double booking), `schedule_events` (`PRIMARY KEY (week_id, seq)`, `payload` jsonb), `schedule_scores`; and the fact tables `shift_facts`, `attendance_events`, `pos_tickets`, `pos_ticket_lines`, `pos_punches` (by `business_date`), `ai_usage`, `schedule_model_calls` (by `created_at`) PARTITION BY RANGE one partition a month, their primary key and UNIQUE constraints carrying the partition column (`db_dialect.PARTITIONED`).

## Ask Cavnar

### `ask_cavnar_conversations` / `ask_cavnar_messages`
Chats, each a thread a login can reopen or delete (`user_id`, `title` — the first question, set once — `created_at`, `updated_at`); a message belongs to exactly one (`conversation_id`) and carries `role` (user/assistant), `content`, `proposals`. Since 9/29/26 (boot ALTERs in `init_db`): each assistant turn keeps `tools_json` (the tool calls it made, names and arguments — so a follow-up can re-read the same data) and `meta_json` (its depth contract, modules, verdict, confidence — what a rating copies); user turns leave both empty. A conversation keeps `summary_json` / `summary_through_id` / `summary_at` — the rolling summary of the turns that scrolled out of the replayed window (`ask_conversations`, validated like any output, replayed as a per-turn context block after the snapshot) — and `topics`, the modules it touched; `idx_ask_conversations_user` `(restaurant_id, user_id, updated_at)`. A chat held through view-as is support's own thread (memory re-audit 9/29/26, PEOPLE-7): its `user_id` (and its messages', its topics', its ratings') is the admin behind the session (`permissions.acting_login_id`), never the owner it views as, and the conversation's `via` is `'view_as'` (`ensure_columns`) — so it never reaches the owner's history, "often asks", notes or past chats. The meta of an assistant turn carries `read_public_text` (the turn read text a member of the public wrote — the next turn in the chat starts tainted, PROMPTS-5), and `tools_json` keeps only the READ calls that ran (never an action or a refused call). Since 10/8/26 (iOS readability round) the meta also carries `view` — `models.ASK_TURN_VIEW_KEYS`: the K1 confidence object, modules consulted, untraced figures, unsupported causes and names, declined repeats, the validation's caveats and the iPhone `card` — returned with the turn by `get_ask_history` as `meta`, so a reopened chat shows what the answer was shown with. `meta_json` is written whole by `models._ask_meta_text` (16,000-character cap; past it the view's confidence object, its declined repeats and caveats, then its card, then the whole view are dropped — never a slice that leaves invalid JSON, which the old 4,000-character cut did).

A login reads only its own chats (`models._viewer_clause`: `user_id = ?`). A chat, message or kept topic with no `user_id` (written before per-login attribution) used to read as anyone's — a manager's past conversations and "often asks" carried the owner's legacy chat — and is now given to the restaurant's primary owner login at every boot (`models._attribute_ownerless_ask_chats`, idempotent; a restaurant with no owner login keeps its NULL rows, which only an account holder reads (`ask_conversations._reads_legacy`, R9); memory re-audit PEOPLE-18 / PROMPTS-17).

Retention: turns are kept per chat (`_ASK_TRANSCRIPT_KEEP`, 200), and chats **per login** — a login's oldest fall off past `models._ASK_CONVERSATIONS_KEEP` (50), anyone's past the restaurant ceiling `_ASK_CONVERSATIONS_KEEP_RESTAURANT` (250), whenever a chat is created. (The cap was 50 per restaurant, so a manager who chatted daily pushed the owner's history out.) An evicted chat is first written to `ask_topics` (kept 400 days, *Retention*; deleted with the chat, and a login's Clear history deletes its own and — for an account holder — the ownerless legacy rows; only an account holder reads ownerless rows, `ask_conversations._reads_legacy`). The action audit (`ask_cavnar_actions`) is never touched by either.

### `ask_topics`
What an evicted chat was about (`models._topic_row`, 9/29/26): `user_id`, `conversation_id`, `title`, `summary_json`, `topics`, `tools` (the tools it ran), `message_count`, `started_at`, `ended_at` — copied from the chat's own rows, nothing a model writes. An empty chat leaves no row. Read by the "questions you often ask" line (`ask_conversations.often_asks`) and `read_past_conversations`. Kept forever: one small row per chat.

### `ask_answer_checks`
What the figure check found in each Ask answer this server wrote: `(restaurant_id, answer_hash)` primary key, `unverified` (JSON list), `created_at`. `answer_hash` is a sha256 of the stripped answer: each answer is written twice, under its whole text and under its first 800 characters (`_ANSWER_HASH_CHARS`, the length an older turn replays at) — rows from before 10/7/26 are keyed on the first 2,400 or 800 characters. Written by `ask_cavnar.record_answer_check` on every answer; read by `_verified_history`, which lets an earlier assistant turn into the next answer's verification corpus only when its own check was clean, and only as much of it as the server wrote: the whole turn when its whole text matches, else the recorded prefix it starts with — never characters a client appended after it (context re-audit 10/7/26 #9) — and never a user turn (the owner's figure is not their data). Rows older than `ANSWER_CHECK_KEEP_DAYS` (30) are pruned on write. Created at boot (`init_db`).

### `ask_cavnar_actions`
Logged write-tool proposals and whether they were confirmed — the audit trail for anything Ask Cavnar was asked to *do* rather than just answer. A proposal row's own `id` is its proposal id (sent to clients as `proposal_id`, rec_ledger key `ask:<id>`); a confirm/dismiss row carries the proposal it answers in `proposal_id` (NULL from clients older than that — those settle by action + summary), and a dismissal's optional `reason`. A proposal row's `surface` is where it was made (`ask` from a chat, `command` from the palette or a Home button — a `command` row is never a Still-open item and its answer is never written into an Ask chat; NULL on older rows) and `target` the path ids its route carries (JSON: `review_id`, `request_id`, `import_id`, `po_id`), so a reopen rebuilds the same card (re-audit F1-7, F1-8; both via `ensure_columns`). A confirm/dismiss row's `authority` (`permissions.answer_authority`, stamped at the action route or derived from the request) and `acting_admin_id` (the admin behind a view-as) — memory re-audit 9/29/26, PLATFORM-3: the platform sync files only the restaurant's own answers (never an admin's, never a delegate's dismissal); NULL is a system or pre-column row.

### `ask_feedback`
"Was this useful?" on one Ask answer (ROI audit #48): `(restaurant_id, message_id, user_id)` unique — `user_id` is whoever rated (the admin behind a view-as, never the owner it views as — PEOPLE-7, so support's rating is its own row and never replaces the owner's) — rating an answer again replaces the rating (`helpful` 0/1, optional `note` ≤ 500, `updated_at`). `conversation_id` is kept beside `message_id` because the transcript trims old turns and the rating is the record. Written only through `models.record_ask_feedback`, which refuses a message that is not an assistant turn of the same restaurant given to the same login. `ask_feedback_summary` (aggregate + the two latest "not helpful" notes) feeds the Ask opening and the context snapshot; no model reads it on load. What each rating was ABOUT (9/29/26, boot ALTERs), copied from the rated answer's `meta_json`: `depth`, `tools`, `modules`, `verdict` (the Response Validation verdict), `topic`, `confidence_pct`; and `authority` — an admin's rating (a view-as session is the owner's login) is kept for the record and never read as the owner's preference or record.

### `ask_memory`
Durable facts told to the assistant across conversations — deliberately written (a tool call, or typed in Account), never harvested from behaviour; the one derived fact, an answer-length preference read off the owner's own ratings (`owner_memory.derive_rating_preferences`), is labelled as such. `(restaurant_id, fact, COALESCE(user_id, 0))` unique — `idx_ask_memory_fact_author` (memory re-audit 9/29/26, R3: it was `UNIQUE(restaurant_id, fact)`, so another login's identical sentence took the fact over; `models._migrate_ask_memory_key` rebuilds an old table once at boot). The same login saying it again updates it (the narrower audience holds: author < principals < team; `created_at` kept, `confirmed_at` stamped); another login saying words it can read CONFIRMS the original (`confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at`), and words it cannot read are its own row, never wider than the one already there. `kind`, `source`, `user_id`, `created_at`. Since 9/29/26 (boot ALTERs in `init_ask_memory`): `modules` (comma list of `ASK_MEMORY_MODULES`; empty = every generator), `subject` (a ledger tag or advice signature), `audience` (`ASK_MEMORY_AUDIENCES`: team / principals / author — who may read it), `author_label` ("Erik, owner"), `authority` (`permissions.answer_authority` at write time), `valid_until` (a time-bound fact ends after this day), `due_on` (a follow-up's due date), `origin` (ask / account / audit / ratings / home — legacy), `updated_at`; since the re-audit (R3): `last_used_at` (the last time a model prompt carried it — `memory_context` stamps it through `owner_memory.mark_used`, at most hourly), `pinned` (an account holder pinned it; at most `ASK_MEMORY_PIN_MAX` = 10 per kind), `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at` (a restatement by another login, or a derived preference's last supporting rating). `scope` (re-audit 9/29/26, R6 PEOPLE-13): `'org'` makes a fact every location's — read at the organisation's other locations (`preferences.org_location_ids`, `models.org_ask_memory`, `owner_memory.facts_for`) under the same audience rules, forgotten and pinned only where it is kept, and counted in that location's lane budget (its use at a sibling stamps `last_used_at` where it lives); set only by a group owner (`preferences.may_apply_to_all`); NULL is this location's. The key rebuild copies every column, `scope` included, and the archive keeps it (`_ASK_ARCHIVE_EXTRA_COLUMNS`), so a restored org fact stays one. A `constraint` or `preference` whose `authority` is `principal` is an **owner rule** (`owner_memory.is_owner_rule`, memory re-audit 9/29/26): prompts read it in the OWNER_RULE fence as a rule to follow (`memory_context` section "owner_rules"), and a staffing rule is also checked in code by the schedule (`schedule_rules.apply_owner_rules`); everyone else's facts are information in the guest fence.

**Lanes, not one pool.** Each kind has its own budget (`models.ASK_MEMORY_CAPS`: constraint 30, context 30, preference 30, goal 10, followup 20; `ASK_MEMORY_LIMIT` is the context lane's, kept as a name). Each kind's budget is split by whose it is (re-audit R3, `models._ask_bucket`): an account holder's (or a seeded, system) shared fact counts against the kind's cap; teammates' and support's shared facts together against half of it (`ASK_MEMORY_DELEGATE_SHARE`), so no one else's notes displace the owner's; each login's own `author` notes against its own per-login budget, outside the restaurant's lanes; the sales audit's founding facts against their own lane (`ASK_MEMORY_AUDIT_CAP` = 40). A write past a budget evicts that bucket's LEAST-USED unpinned fact of the same kind (`last_used_at`, else `confirmed_at`, else `created_at`) into `ask_memory_archive`. A login that leaves (revoked or switched off — `users.is_active=0` — or its membership of this restaurant ended; never inferred from a missing membership, which a group owner reaching a sibling location has) takes its `author` notes with it — archived as `author_left` by `owner_memory.retire_departed` (on revoke and on every expiry pass); its shared facts stay, marked in Account. Constraints, context and preferences stay until someone retracts them; a fact past `valid_until`, and a follow-up 7 days past its due date, are archived by `owner_memory.expire`. Home's "Not doing X: reason" answers are no longer copied here — they live on the answer in `rec_events`. Measurable goals belong in `owner_goals`; the `goal` kind is for aims no metric reads.

### `ask_memory_archive`
What left the memory without the owner asking (9/29/26): the fact with its kind, author, modules, subject, audience, dates and origin, `archived_at`, `reason` (evicted / expired / retracted / forgotten — a derived fact / replaced — a newer note or rating preference / contradicted — a rating preference the newer ratings retired / author_left — a departed login's private note), `archived_by`; since the re-audit (R3) `authority` and `pinned`, so a restore puts it back as its author's. Account lists it — filtered for the viewer in SQL before the page is cut, paged by id — and restores or dismisses it by id (`owner_memory.restore` / `dismiss`); a restored fact whose date passed comes back without it (or with a new one). Kept 400 days (registry, floor 365 — Account offers a year to restore), EXCEPT an account holder's evicted constraint, which is kept until someone dismisses it (`ops._RETENTION_ONLY`).

## Alerts / Notifications

### `alert_log`
Every notification that has fired: `restaurant_id`, `alert_type`, optional `review_id`, `fired_at`, `value` (the figure it fired on), `priority` (`push.PRIORITY`, P0–P5, stamped at write time), `ref_kind` + `ref_id` (what a non-review row is about: `delayed_action` — the queued send an Undo stops — or `shift` / `time_off` — the staff request Approve / Deny answers; written by `strategy_jobs._reach` from the push payload's `delayed_action_id` / `request_id`, so the bell can act on the row — or `issue`, the `ops_issues` row an `issue` / `coverage` / `issue_escalated` row is about, written by `issues.py`). The bell's `resolved` reads the subject through it (`client_api._notification_resolution`): no longer pending, or the issue resolved. Read back by both notification centers and Ask Cavnar's `read_alerts` tool — "outstanding" is computed by joining to the linked review's `response_status`, not stored as a separate flag. `channels` (JSON, 9/29/26) records which channels the alert actually went out on — a row is written for the firing decision, and `channels` says whether anything was delivered. One row is ever taken back (event re-audit 2, 10/1/26): `notify.withdraw_notification` deletes a row written for a push that `push.fire_push` then queued for no device — only at that restaurant and only while nobody has opened it — so the bell never lists a push no phone showed and the briefing budget is not spent on it (the big-game push, `event_ahead`, is its one caller). An `event_ahead` row opens Ask (the bell payload's `module` is `ask`) while the bell, its badge and Home list it only for logins with the Labor view (`push.audience_of`).

It is two things at once: the HISTORY both clients read, and the tally the daily cap and the 50/day ceiling are counted from. The advisory types in `models.NON_ALERT_TYPES` (brief, pulse, closing summary, drafted schedule, issues, coverage, outcome wins, the `daily_briefing` wrapper) write history rows but are excluded from `count_alerts_today` — they are not alerts, and an issue text goes to a manager rather than to the owner whose cap it would spend.

### `alert_holds`
Alerts raised mid-service, waiting for the rush to end: `alert_type`, `subject`, `html`, `sms_text`, `review_id`, `value`, `release_at`, `sent_at`, and `meta_json` (the recommendation keys, push audience and brief-covered types the release must honour). `release_due_alerts` sends at most `MAX_RELEASE_PER_RESTAURANT` per pass and drops anything `HOLD_MAX_LATE_HOURS` past its release. A hold is marked `sent_at` whether or not delivery worked (so a failing channel cannot replay it every tick) and `outcome` (9/29/26) says which: `released`, `suppressed_cap`, `failed` or `dropped_stale`. A `dropped_stale` hold is reported to the operator and folded into that restaurant's next morning brief, which stamps `folded_at`.

### `notification_reads` / `notification_opens`
Per-LOGIN read state (PK `user_id, restaurant_id`) and per-type open events. An open carries `alert_log_id` (from the push payload, accepted only when that row is this restaurant's — so time-to-open is `opened_at - alert_log.fired_at`) and `rec_key`. `seen_at` is written in SQLite's own `"%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S"` **deliberately** — the old stamp on `restaurants.notifications_seen_at` used isoformat's `T`, and since the unread query is a TEXT comparison and `' ' < 'T'`, every alert fired on the same date as the last read counted as already seen. The badge could only ever show yesterday.

### `alert_contacts`
Who gets alerted and on which channels (email/SMS/push), per restaurant — separate from the login `users` table since an alert recipient need not have dashboard access.

### `device_tokens` / `push_deliveries`
APNs tokens per user/restaurant; delivery attempts and outcomes for push. `disabled_reason` PARKS a token rather than deleting it — `get_device_tokens(for_delivery=True)` skips it and the next app launch re-registers and clears it. Only Apple's own "this token is gone" (`_PERMANENT_FAILURE_REASONS`) deletes a row. `tier` (`owner` | `staff`, boot ALTER in `push.init_push`, default `owner`; employee audit C4, 10/2/26): a staff-app phone registered through `/staff/api/device-tokens` is `staff` — left out of every "who has a phone here" reader (`get_device_tokens(include_staff=False)`), reached only when `fire_push` names its login, only by `push.STAFF_ALERT_TYPES` (`token_may_receive`), only while its membership is active, and never given a badge. One token is one row: a phone the owner app registers moves back to `owner`. `push.unregister_staff_devices` removes a login's staff rows on sign-out, a PIN change or reset, deactivation, unlink and account deletion.

### `home_dismissals`
Attention items a user has dismissed from the Home brief, so a handled issue doesn't keep resurfacing. Every answer here is also written to rec_ledger (see Recommendations below), which is what the rest of the product reads.

## Marketing

`web_analytics_daily` (10/2/26): the website's figures, one row per (restaurant, source `ga4`|`gsc`, day, metric), upserted by `web_analytics.sync`; metrics `sessions`, `users`, `pageviews`, `engaged_sessions`, `channel:<group>`, `click:<domain>`, `booking_clicks`, `ordering_clicks`, `checkout_clicks`, `other_clicks`, `menu_views`, `search_clicks`, `search_impressions`, `search_position`. `web_search_queries`: the latest 28-day searches only, up to `web_analytics.STORED_QUERIES` (500; replaced each read) — the Website screen lists the top 25 and Intel's AI-visibility check asks the non-branded ones (`web_analytics.ai_questions`). `restaurants.ga4_property_id`, `gsc_site_url`, `web_analytics_synced_at`, `web_analytics_error`: the connection and its last read — cleared with the connection and on offboarding (`offboarding.INTEGRATION_FIELDS['website']`).

`external_bindings` (10/19/26, scale audit SEC-01): whose a GA4 property or Search Console site is — one row per (`provider` `ga4`|`gsc`, `external_id` normalised: the property's digits, the site's host without `www.`, `restaurant_id`), `status` `verified`|`pending`, `verified_how` (`website_match`, `stream_match`, `admin`, `grandfathered`), `verified_at`, `detail`. `web_analytics.sync` reads a source only when its row is verified for that restaurant; an id verified for another live, non-demo restaurant that still holds it is refused at save (`external_bindings.conflict`). Rows go with the connection (`POST /api/web-analytics` clearing one, the disconnect); the one-time `external_bindings_grandfather_v1` migration recorded every binding already in place as verified. Small (one row per connected source), no retention.

`restaurants.fb_page_name` / `ig_username` (10/2/26): the Facebook Page and Instagram account the Meta connection bound, named on the Connections card; cleared with the connection and on offboarding. `ig_token_expires` / `fb_token_expires` are Meta's own expiry (NULL for a Page token that never expires).

`marketing_drafts`, `marketing_scheduled_posts`, `marketing_content_log` (each row tagged with `menu_item_id`, `occasion`, `post_kind`), `marketing_media`, `marketing_links`, `marketing_attribution` (`verdict` lifted/dropped/no_clear_change against `noise_band_pct` — readers colour from it, never from the sign of `lift_pct`), `content_calendar_cache`, `guest_campaigns`, `guest_campaign_recipients` (which guests each campaign reached, matched back through the connected POS), `guest_newsletters` (`design` JSON — headline, preheader, button, `image_media_id` — and `segment` / `segment_label`, Campaign Studio 9/28/26; a `marketing_media` photo any newsletter's `design` names is never deleted — guests' copies load it when opened, `marketing_media.newsletter_sent_with`, re-audit 10/8/26), `guest_newsletter_recipients` (`message_id`: the send's Resend id, joined to `email_log` for opens and clicks; `idx_email_log_message` indexes it; `email_token`: the unsubscribe token that email carried, so an old link names the address it went to after the contact's address changed; `retryable` 1/0 on a `failed` row — a Resend 5xx/408/429, timeout or missing key can be retried, a rejected address or an interrupted send cannot; `status` pending/sending/sent/failed/skipped, a suppressed address is `skipped`), `guest_email_optouts` (fix round B, 9/28/26: `restaurant_id`, lower-cased `email`, `source` link/one_click/complaint/backfill, UNIQUE per restaurant — a guest's email opt-out belongs to the ADDRESS and outlives the contact row, like `guest_sms_optouts`; `guest_contacts.email_unsubscribed` mirrors it on every row with the address; the public form never clears it), `guest_contacts` (consent scopes, 9/28/26: `consent` is MARKETING consent — set only by a confirmed opt-in, the join form's double opt-in Y — and `review_consent` / `review_consent_at` is the narrower review-link consent a YES to the invite gives; every marketing send and count reads `guest_marketing.marketing_text_sql()` and review requests `review_text_sql()`; `source` manual / join / toast_invite, NULL on older rows; neither a join nor a YES touches `last_visit` / `visit_count`; `idx_guest_contacts_phone`), `sms_optin_invites`, `guest_consent_events` (append-only consent evidence — a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger refuses rewrites and contact deletion leaves it: `restaurant_id` (0 = the shared number), `channel`, `phone`, `email`, `event` requested / confirmed / opted_out / resubscribed / review_only, `source` join_form / sms_reply / owner / backfill / explicit_optin, `ip`, `user_agent`, `disclosure_version`, `disclosure_hash`, `message_sid` (the inbound Twilio MessageSid), `detail`, `created_at` UTC; only a full restaurant erasure deletes it), `guest_optin_requests` (the public join's pending opt-ins and its durable per-phone / per-IP limit: `status` pending / confirmed / already / blocked_stop / send_failed / stopped, `requested_at` UTC, confirmed by the phone's own Y within 48 hours; unconfirmed rows go after 90 days), `sms_pending_questions` ("which restaurant did you mean", open 24 hours; a bare restaurant name answers only one of these), `guest_campaign_drafts` (a campaign Cavnar drafted for the owner — the win-back text for a lapsed segment: `segment`, `segment_size`, `message` as drafted, `sent_message` as sent, `status` pending/sent/dismissed, `rec_key` `winback:<segment>`; never sent without the owner). `marketing_drafts`, `marketing_scheduled_posts`, `marketing_content_log` (each row tagged with `menu_item_id`, `occasion`, `post_kind`), `marketing_media`, `marketing_links`, `marketing_attribution` (`verdict` lifted/dropped/no_clear_change against `noise_band_pct` — readers colour from it, never from the sign of `lift_pct`), `content_calendar_cache`, `guest_campaigns` (fix round 9/28/26, MB-10: `status` sending / waiting — the 8am-9pm window closed, pending texts go at 8:00 AM / done / cancelled, default `done` for rows written before the queue; `total` recipients queued, `sent_count`, `failed_count`, `skipped_count` left out at queue time or at send, `deferred_count` waiting for 8:00 AM, `body` the exact text sent — "{Name}: " + words + link + STOP line, `content_hash` of segment + words so a second press while it is still going resumes it, `target_day` the fill-a-night weekday whose tracker starts when it finishes having SENT something, `created_by`, `winback_draft_id`, `completed_at`, `rec_key` — the Opportunity Feed card it began on, marked acted on when the campaign finishes having sent; no row at all when nobody is eligible), `guest_campaign_queue` (one row per recipient, written before any text: `campaign_id`, `restaurant_id`, `contact_id`, `phone`, `status` pending → sending → sent / failed / skipped, or cancelled / expired; `claimed_at`, `sent_at`, `error`; UNIQUE(campaign_id, contact_id). A row is claimed pending → sending and the guest claimed on `guest_contacts.last_campaign_at` with consent re-read before the text goes; a 'sending' row older than 30 minutes is failed, never re-sent; a pending row older than 24 hours expires — `guest_marketing.run_campaign_sends`, a scheduler minute duty), `guest_campaign_recipients` (which guests each campaign reached, matched back through the connected POS), `guest_newsletters` (`design` JSON — headline, preheader, button, `image_media_id` — and `segment` / `segment_label`, Campaign Studio 9/28/26), `guest_newsletter_recipients` (`message_id`: the send's Resend id, joined to `email_log` for opens and clicks; `idx_email_log_message` indexes it), `guest_contacts`, `sms_optin_invites`, `guest_campaign_drafts` (a campaign Cavnar drafted for the owner — the win-back text for a lapsed segment: `segment`, `segment_size`, `message` as drafted, `sent_message` as sent, `status` pending/sent/dismissed, `rec_key` `winback:<segment>`; never sent without the owner). `marketing_content_log.origin` is owner / job / marker, so "pieces this month" (`marketing.pieces_this_month`) counts content a person made or published, not calendar markers or the weekly job's drafts. `marketing_content_log.metrics_synced_at` (9/28/26, MB-7) is when the platform last answered for that post; a post is MEASURED when it is stamped or a metric is non-zero (`marketing_signals.MEASURED_SQL`), and an unmeasured post's `reach` / `impressions` / `engaged` / `likes` / `comments` / `shares` are NULL — new rows write NULL, and boot (`init_db`) blanks legacy rows that hold nothing but the columns' DEFAULT 0. Readers sum reach alone (never reach + impressions) over measured rows. `marketing_content_log.removed_at` (10/6/26) is when the post was found deleted on Facebook or Instagram: the refresh (`social_routes.refresh_post_metrics`) stamps it only when every call for the post said it does not exist while another post on the same account answered in that pass, never asks about it again, and `marketing_signals.performance_window` leaves it out. `posted_at` is stamped on every publish path (direct Instagram / Facebook / Google and the queue); a publish completes the generated row it names by id (`content_log_id`), else it is its own row. `marketing_drafts.status` can be `expired` (a quiet-night draft past its night): never approved, sent or revived by an edit.

The owner's marketing voice (memory audit 9/29/26, `marketing_voice.py`; DDL in `init_db`): `marketing_drafts.original_body` keeps the model's first text on a saved draft when the owner edits it (an edit used to overwrite `body`); `marketing_drafts.model_draft_id` is the `marketing_model_drafts` row it began as, so its approval files the outcome on the run that wrote it (AI cost audit 10/7/26 re-audit #8). `marketing_model_drafts` — every piece of copy a model drafted for a person (the post composer, the Campaign Studio's text and email: `channel`, `source`, `body`, `content_log_id`, `user_id`, `used_at`, `workflow` — the orchestrator workflow that wrote it, NULL on older rows meaning the channel's, re-audit #9; `marketing_voice.record_draft`), kept 90 days (registry, floor 90) to match what went out to what was drafted and to see which drafts were regenerated rather than used. `marketing_edits` — one row per marketing piece that went out (a post published or approved, a guest text or an email sent; `marketing_voice.record_final`): `channel`, `source`, `ref_id`, `draft_id`, `original_body` beside `final_body`, the edit measured (`edit_distance`, `edit_category`, `edit_signals`, `words_before` / `words_after`), `user_id`, `authority`, `via`; UNIQUE per restaurant, source and ref. It is the owner's voice record and is kept forever.

## Food Cost / Inventory

`ingredients` (`count_discrepancy_qty` — the ledger's own sum when it went below zero, NULL otherwise: `current_stock` stays clamped at 0 for orders and valuation, and the item is listed under `count_discrepancies` and kept out of `critical_low`, CA3 F14; `rollup_at` — UTC stamp of the last `inventory_ledger.recompute_rollups`, apart from `updated_at`, which any edit moves. Invariant: the cached `waste_last_week` is never read as "this week" for an item that has waste events — `inventory.load_inventory_for_restaurant` sums each item's waste from its dated events over the restaurant's local today−6..today (`inventory_ledger.waste_in_window`, one grouped query, DH1-1); an item never logged keeps its typed figure, marked `waste_basis: "manual"`; `rollup_at` is the note on the `waste` freshness source; `par_changed_at` (9/29/26, `ensure_columns`) — when `par_level` last CHANGED by any path (a hand edit, a suggestion accepted, a sync; stamped only when the value differs), the cutoff `ordering.par_suggestions` counts 86s from, since nights before it were measured against another par), `invoice_aliases` (9/29/26 — the owner's own invoice matches per supplier: `supplier_key`, `description_key` → `ingredient_id`, `times_used`, `created_by`, `last_used_at`; UNIQUE per restaurant, supplier and line description; written at `invoices.apply`, read first by `invoices.propose`; kept forever — owner corrections, bounded by what the suppliers print), `ingredient_stock_events` (ordering invariant, F2-7: the stock anchor is the recount TAKEN last — newest `event_date`, then newest id on that date — and receiving/depletion/waste count after it when dated later, or the same day and entered after it; a recount's inferred waste compares against the stock as of its own date. By id alone, a count typed in after a delivery but dated before it erased the delivery. `source` values added in the friction round, 9/25/26: `purchase_order` — a delivery received from a PO, note = PO number and supplier; `logged` — waste an owner or manager logged, which `waste_sources` counts as counted waste; only `inferred` is the unexplained recount gap), `inventory_history` (`inv_value`, the anchor a food COGS window brackets, is the food side only since 10/15/26 — an ingredient `cogs.is_bar_item` reads as alcohol is the Bar's and is left out of it and of `cogs.purchases_in_window`; the analysis' `total_stock_value` still counts everything; since 10/16/26 the scheduled writer stores `cogs.food_stock_value` — no alcohol, no negative stock — FC-CALC-23 / AI-03. `waste_json.total_waste_cost` is NULL for a week nothing was logged in (no waste event in the trailing seven days, no typed figure — FC-CALC-12): unmeasured, never a $0 week, and the trend drops it. `waste_json.counted` / `counted_on` / `counted_share`: whether the stock value is a FULL count — the items recounted on its count day, the snapshot's date or the day before, carry `cogs.COUNTED_SHARE` (80%) of its food stock value (`waste_trend._counted`, recomputed from `items_json` on read); only a counted snapshot anchors food cost %, dated by `counted_on` (FC-CALC-02) — between counts the ledger's stock is the recipes' theoretical figure. `inventory_weekly_summary.waste_json` carries `counted` / `counted_on` for a week past retention, and its `inv_value` is the food stock value from the row's items), `menu_items`, `menu_item_sales` (per-item units from the POS, the basis of dish lift and depletion), `recipe_ingredients`, `recipe_drafts` (model-drafted, accepted line by line; `accepted_lines_json` and `edited_lines` record what the owner actually accepted beside the draft's `lines_json`; `image_sha` — the sha256 of a photographed card (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #87, `ensure_columns`): the same photo again opens the pending draft it made rather than paying to read it twice, NULL on a drafted recipe), `recipe_ingredients.source` (owner / draft_accepted / draft_edited — a plate cost resting on a draft accepted unedited is not confirmed, and the menu cost driver's confidence says so), `purchase_orders` (`source` owner/automatic, `draft_items_json`, `edited` — supplier trust counts only owner-sent orders, and an edited one counts against the supplier: `ordering.ORDER_TRUST_EDIT_RATE`; `authority` — `permissions.answer_authority` of the login that sent it, `admin` for a view-as send, which neither supplier trust nor `ordering.order_corrections` counts; NULL = before 9/29/26 or automatic), `reprice_decisions` (every price set that followed a reprice suggestion: `old_price`, `suggested_price`, `chosen_price`, `source` one_tap/manual; `authority` — `admin` for a view-as reprice, kept and left out of `menu_intelligence.reprice_acceptance`, the owner's usual share of a suggested rise), `forecast_log` (every stated forecast — kinds in `forecast_log.KINDS`: waste_week, profitability_month, revenue_week, labor_week, marketing_reach_week, review_rating_week, event_order_week — labor_week, marketing_reach_week and review_rating_week written once per ISO week by `insight_store.record_weekly_forecast`; `event_order_week` (10/16/26, AI-11) is the guessed holiday order bump, frozen by `inventory.record_event_bump` for the holiday's ISO week: `predicted` = the bumped items' average day × 7 × unit cost × `inventory._EVENT_SCALE_FACTOR`, `basis` JSON `{holiday, date, factor, baseline, basis: "guess", items: [{ingredient_id, item, unit_cost, week_units}]}`, scored against those items' depletion events that week at the frozen unit costs (`inventory.event_usage_actual`; none → unscorable, never 0). Invariants: one row per restaurant × kind × period, `horizon_end` = the period's last day (ISO week Sunday / month end), insert-once — the first prediction for a period is the forecast; scored only once the whole period is before today; `error_pct` and `signed_error_pct` share one denominator, the actual; accuracy reads one row per period, the earliest, so legacy daily rows never count twice; boot ALTERs (confidence re-audit round 2): `naive_last` / `naive_mean` / `naive_n` — what the latest closed period and the mean of up to 8 closed periods said for the same period AS OF the day it was frozen, filled at scoring (older scored rows backfilled 50 per pass; `naive_n` NULL = not read yet) — the base of `accuracy`'s skill; `unscorable_at` — set when a closed period's actual still cannot be measured `UNSCORABLE_AFTER_DAYS` (21) after it closed, and such a row is never selected again; `explained_by` (9/29/26) — the measured event that explains a scored period's miss, in words ("Cubs home game on 9/15/26, +22% here", `forecast_log.explained_by` from `event_memory.night_facts`), set at scoring for `forecast_log.EVENT_SENSITIVE_KINDS` (revenue_week, labor_week, waste_week) only; such a period is left out of the bias correction's lean while explained periods are under half of the scored ones. A `revenue_week` row's `basis` (frozen at publish by `demand`) carries `modelled` — `{date: [labels]}`, the events whose measured effect the projection already applied — and an event the forecast already carried never explains its miss), `food_cost_diagnoses` and `review_diagnoses` (the stored root-cause reads the 6am jobs write; `unsupported_figures` keeps the figures the verifier could not trace, so every surface shows the caveat; `operational_evidence` holds only entries the validator verified against the module line the model was handed, each `verified: true`, and the readers drop any other; `confidence` is the model's band capped — medium at most with no verified operational evidence, low with an unverified figure — and `model_confidence` keeps the model's own band apart, never read as the confidence; `get_diagnoses` returns current clusters only, ranked like `complaint_clusters`; `food_cost_diagnoses.retired_at` / `retired_reason` (9/29/26, `ensure_columns`) — a food diagnosis whose lead driver is no longer ranked, or with no driver clearing the floor, is retired, not deleted: kept as history and never served as the current cause. `evidence_hash` / `confirmed_at` on both (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #12, #28, `ensure_columns`): a hash of the evidence the diagnosis was written from — the cluster's category, window, mention count and review ids; the food read's ranked drivers with their figures and evidence lines — and when a pass last found that evidence unchanged. The daily pass reuses a diagnosis while the hash matches (or, as before, inside 24 hours with the same mention count / lead driver) and rewrites it once its words are 7 days old; `stale` reads from `confirmed_at` (falling back to `generated_at`), so a re-checked cause is current while `as_of` keeps the date its words were written. Every diagnosis written is also kept in `ai_reads`, since these tables upsert per subject — see *AI reads*). `reviews.severity` is NULL when the analyser was given no tier — counted as unclassified everywhere (`severity_breakdown.unclassified`, each cluster's `unclassified`), sorted like `service` (`analyser.UNCLASSIFIED_RANKS_AS`), never folded into a tier.

### Back Office invoices and accounts (`backoffice_ap.py`, 10/15/26)

What a restaurant pays through Back Office (Buyers Edge Platform), from the two files it lets an owner take out (no member API). All tables created at boot (`init_backoffice_ap` from `models.init_db`); kept (financial records, no personal data — the equity accounts' owner names are never imported: only GL number, name, subtype and category are kept, and only from the Chart of Accounts rows themselves). Not scrubbed off-site.

- `bo_files` — one row per file read, UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, sha256)`: `filename`, `kind` (coa | invoices_by_vendor | invoice_page), `intake_id`, `status` (stored | conflict — a vendor held because its rows don't add up to the total the printout shows, rows under a vendor name the reader couldn't read, or stored rows that don't add up to the printout; `detail` names them), `detail`, `created_at`. The file's bytes are not kept. Invariant (fix round 10/16/26, ING-10): the row and everything read from the file are written in ONE transaction (`read_file`), so a failed write leaves no row and the intake retry reads the file again.
- `bo_gl_accounts` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, gl)`: `name`, `subtype` (Back Office's GLAccountSubTypeName), `category` (food | na_beverage | beer | wine | liquor | bar_consumables | discount | other_purchase | inventory_adjustment | not_cogs: a 'Purchase' account by its name, a 'Purchase Inventory' account is a count's adjustment, anything else not cost of goods; a discount or rebate account whose name says food books to food, so its credits net against food purchases — FC-CALC-17; every stored row is re-read through `gl_category` at boot), `updated_at`.
- `bo_invoices` — one row per Invoices By Vendor entry, UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, vendor_key, entry_key)` (`entry_key` an invoice's `inv|<number without padding>|<year>` plus `|cr` for a credit and `|2`… for the same one shown again — so a credit memo under its invoice's number, or the same number another year, is its own row, and a corrected amount replaces the row (FC-CALC-04; older number-only keys are re-keyed at boot by `_migrate_entry_keys`) — or a check-mode entry's date, amount and its ordinal among identical entries that day — so an overlapping printout updates in place). A printout is the record for its vendors over its dates (its first to its last entry): an entry on file there that it no longer shows is moved to `bo_invoice_removals`, an invoice stored under another spelling of its vendor (same number, date and amount) takes the printout's spelling, and each vendor's stored rows over those dates must add up to the printout's total (ING-12, FC-CALC-25): `vendor`, `vendor_key` (fintech_invoices.distributor_key), `invoice_number` (NULL for a check-mode entry), `entry_kind` (invoice | cm), `itemized`, `invoice_date` (ISO), `amount`, `gl` (the entry's account when the printout shows one — check-mode entries do, itemized invoices do not), `file_id`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. Index `(restaurant_id, invoice_date)`.
- `bo_invoice_lines` — one invoice's line items from its Edit Invoice page printed to PDF (10/15/26), PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, vendor_key, invoice_key, line_no)`: `item_code` (the vendor's, or Tax / Delivery), `description`, `gl` (each line's own account), `purchase_unit`, `qty`, `unit_amount`, `ext_amount`, `invoice_date`, `file_id`, `updated_at`. A page re-read replaces its invoice's lines. With lines on file, `backoffice_ap.entries` splits that invoice by account (category_source 'line') and what the lines leave of its total goes to the vendor's category ('invoice_rest'); a page read before any printout lists its invoice also writes the `bo_invoices` row under the printout's own key. Index `(restaurant_id, item_code)`.
- `bo_recipe_books` — PRIMARY KEY `restaurant_id`: the restaurant's Back Office recipe book whole as JSON (`payload`: recipes with their ingredients, batch yields and group; items with their vendors' item numbers, packs, prices and unit conversions; `pos_names`, each menu recipe's POS item names), `source`, `loaded_at`. Replaced whole on each load (`backoffice_recipes.load`); read by `backoffice_recipes.plates` (10/15/26).
- `bo_inventory_counts` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, period_end, gl)`: one submitted Back Office inventory period's value per inventory GL account (`period_start`, `gl`, `gl_name`, `category` by the account's name — food, beer, wine, liquor, na_beverage, bar_consumables — `amount`, `updated_at`), read off Back Office's inventory pages (`scripts/backoffice_read_counts.js` → `backoffice_import.py`, 10/16/26). Only Submitted periods are stored; a period read again replaces its rows. `category` is set by the account's GL number through `bo_gl_accounts` before the name read off the page, and `backoffice_counts.counts` re-derives it the same way on read; a count with no food account is not a kitchen count (never $0 of food on hand), and a count lacking a food account the counts around it carry is flagged (`gl_gap`) and not measured. `backoffice_counts.waste_periods` measures food waste between consecutive food counts (on hand + bought − on hand, less the recipes' cost % of food sales, priced as of the period's end; withheld below 60% recipe coverage of food sales, when negative, or when food sales miss more than a fifth of the trading nights) and `waste_trend.load_waste_history` merges those periods into the waste trend, per week.
- `bo_waste_periods` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, period_end)`: the materialised waste periods (`backoffice_counts.waste_periods`, SCALE-01, 10/16/26) — `payload` (the period as JSON), `stamp` (`backoffice_counts.inputs_stamp`: a digest of the counts, Back Office invoices and lines, chart of accounts, vendor categories, recipe book, Fintech invoices, POS archive days and newest POS night, and the nightly report's sales categories, plus `WASTE_VERSION`), `computed_at`. Rewritten whole for the restaurant when the stamp moves; one row per count period, so bounded without a retention entry. Created at boot (`backoffice_counts.init_backoffice_counts` from `models.init_db`).
- `bo_inventory_count_items` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, period_end, location, line_no)`: the count sheets' rows behind a period (`location`, `item`, `gl_name`, `unit`, `qty`, `total`), replaced with the period.
- `bo_vendor_categories` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, vendor_key)`: the account holder's category for a supplier whose itemized invoices carry no account on the printout (`set_by`, `set_at`); only a vendor on the restaurant's `bo_invoices` (SEC-04).
- `bo_invoice_removals` (fix round 10/16/26, ING-12) — an entry a later printout no longer shows over its dates (voided, a corrected check-mode amount, a re-keyed row), as it was (`vendor`, `vendor_key`, `entry_key`, `invoice_number`, `entry_kind`, `invoice_date`, `amount`, `gl`, `file_id` it came from), with `removed_by_file`, `why`, `removed_at`. Index `(restaurant_id, removed_at)`. Kept (a financial audit record, bounded by corrections; no personal data).
- `bo_entry_decisions` (fix round 10/16/26, LEAD-01) — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, vendor_key, entry_key)`: the account holder's word on a Back Office alcohol entry from a Fintech distributor that matches no Fintech invoice (`outside_fintech`'s `to_confirm`) — `decision` count | not_purchase, `set_by`, `set_at`; no row means still to confirm. Written by `backoffice_ap.decide_entry` (`POST food-cost/purchases/entry`).

### Bar product categories (`bar/classify.py`, behind the `bar_cost.py` facade; 10/14/26)

`bar_product_categories` — the owner's correction to how one bought product is sorted, PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, product_key)` (`<canonical distributor>|<item code without leading zeros>`, else the lowercased description — `bar.classify.product_key`; rows written under the 10/14/26 form are moved once at boot by `migrate_product_keys`, the later correction holding where two become one): `category` (beer | liquor | wine | na | supplies | other), `set_by`, `set_at`. Without a row the product's category is `bar_cost.classify`'s guess from its description, unit and distributor. Kept.

`bar_stopped_distributors` (barfix 10/15/26, audit V9) — a distributor the owner said the bar stopped buying from: PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, distributor)` (the name lowercased with its spaces collapsed), `set_by`, `set_at` (UTC). Created at boot (`bar.judge.init_bar_judge`, from `init_db` beside `init_bar_cost`). Read by `bar.judge.stopped`: a house named here is "stopped" (`why: owner`) and never a missing-invoice gap again; without a row a house is stopped only when it has been silent over 4x its own rhythm (at least four weeks) while another house supplied each of its categories, or silent 91 days. `set_stopped` writes and clears it (the route is W7's). Kept: a handful of rows per restaurant; no person's data.

### The owner's bar products and pours (`bar/products.py`, barfix 10/15/26, audit F3 / F8 / F11, Decision 3)

Created at boot by `bar.products.init_bar_products`, which `bar.classify.init_bar_cost` calls (so `models.init_db`). All are the owner's own settings, one row per thing set — small, kept, never pruned (not in *Retention*), not scrubbed off-site (no person's data, no credential).

- `bar_settings` — PRIMARY KEY `restaurant_id`: `count_mixers` (0/1; Decision 3 — cocktail mixers, `bar.classify.is_mixer`, stay non-alcoholic unless this is 1, when `bar.purchases._lines` sorts them as liquor; a product the owner sorted by hand keeps its own category), `liquor_oz` (0.25–6), `wine_oz` (1–12), `draft_oz` (4–40), `draft_yield_pct` (50–100) — NULL = the default (1.5 oz, 5 oz, 16 oz at 90%), `set_by`, `set_at`. Read by `bar.products.settings`.
- `bar_product_packs` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, product_key)`: `units_per_case` (1–120), `container_ml` (50–60,000): the owner's pack where the description does not say it (the parser's assumption otherwise, labelled). Both empty deletes the row.
- `bar_product_links` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, product_key)`: `same_as` (another product_key): the owner's word that two codes are one product where no valid UPC joins them (`bar.products.canonical_keys`).
- `bar_expected_prices` — `id`, `restaurant_id`, `product_key`, `min_qty` (1–1,000), `price` (a unit), `valid_from` / `valid_to` (ISO or NULL), `note`, `set_by`, `set_at`; index `(restaurant_id, product_key)`. The owner's deal sheet (F8): a line on an invoice in the lookback billed above the price its quantity earns is a `price_watch.tier_flags` row; where the sheet is silent the learned ladder decides.
- `bar_drink_map` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, provider, item_id)`: `product_key`, `pour_oz` (NULL = a bottle or can sold as it comes), `set_by`, `set_at`. A POS menu item (`pos_menu_prices`) the owner confirmed is one bought product (F11). `bar.margins.margins` reads only these rows; its name suggestions are never stored or used.

### Bar counts (`bar/counts.py`, barfix 10/15/26, audit F1)

`bar_counts` — the bar's shelf value by category on a count date, the input that makes pour cost a measurement: `id`, `restaurant_id`, `count_date` (ISO; the shelf at the CLOSE of that day), `category` (beer | liquor | wine), `value_usd` (≥ 0, ≤ 1,000,000; full kegs valued without their deposit), `method` (category_value — typed by category | items — reserved for item counts | import — a file), `source` (owner — typed in the app | backoffice — Back Office's emailed valuation, `email_intake` kind `backoffice_valuation` | import), `entered_by` (the login's id; NULL for a file), `entered_at` (UTC), `note`. UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, count_date, category)`: a save writes one category's value at a time (a category left out is untouched — never a whole-count overwrite), and an import never replaces a value whose `source` is owner. Created at boot (`bar.counts.init_bar_counts`, from `init_db` beside `init_bar_cost`). Read by `bar.counts.true_pour_cost` / `counted` — used = begin + purchases (invoice lines dated after the begin count up to the end count, net of deposits) − end, over that span's category sales, withheld with a `missing` list unless two counts bracket the span within 10 days of its edges, no distributor gap, sales on ≥ 90% of trading days, and a used figure between 0 and the sales. Kept: a financial record, never pruned (not in *Retention*). Not scrubbed off-site: dollars by category, no person's data and no credential.

`bar_waste` — the bar's waste, spill and breakage log (bar fix round 10/15/26, F14): `id`, `restaurant_id`, `waste_date` (ISO; a day already over, at most 120 days back), `product_key` (a beer, liquor or wine product the invoices carried — `bar.purchases._lines`), `qty` (> 0), `unit` (each — a bottle or can | oz | keg), `reason` (spill | breakage | comp — comped off the POS | line_clean | other), `note`, `entered_by`, `entered_at` (UTC). Index `(restaurant_id, waste_date)`. Created at boot (`bar.waste.init_bar_waste`, from `init_db`). Entered by the account holder and FOOD_COST_ENTER logins (as counts, no dollars), removed by the account holder. Read by `bar.variance.attach_waste`: shown beside each product's gap, never netted from it; a category's counted variance prices it at the product's cost and names it as one of its parts (FC/Bar audit 10/16/26 BAR-CALC-05). A POST runs once per Idempotency-Key (`idempotent_responses`, route `bar_waste` — SCALE-10). Kept: a financial record, never pruned (not in *Retention*). Not scrubbed off-site: no person's data beyond the entering login's id.

`bar_drink_specs` — a POS drink's alcoholic ingredients as bought products and ounces (bar fix round 10/15/26, F2): `id`, `restaurant_id`, `provider`, `item_name` (the POS button as the card names it), `item_id` (resolved from `pos_menu_catalog` / the archive's names; NULL until the POS lists it), `ingredient`, `product_key` (NULL when no bought product matched), `oz`, `status` (suggested | confirmed), `source` (recipe_card | owner), `recipe`, `note`, `set_by`, `set_at`. UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, provider, item_name, ingredient)`. Created at boot (`bar.variance.init_bar_variance`). Loaded from a bar's recipe cards as `suggested` (`bar.variance.load_recipe_cards`, `scripts/bar_load_recipe_cards.py` — a reload never touches a confirmed or owner-changed row); only `confirmed` rows count in bought vs sold. Kept with the restaurant (owner configuration).

### Fintech distributor invoices (`fintech_invoices.py`, 10/14/26)

The alcohol invoices a restaurant pays through Fintech, read from the files Fintech emails (`email_intake`, kind `fintech`). `fintech_files` — one row per file received, UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, sha256)` (a file sent twice is one row): `intake_id`, `filename`, `format` (printout — the daily `LegacyRetailerReceivedDetailWithPageBreak` text PDF | export — the portal's payment-activity CSV or the My Invoices "Invoice Details" CSV | NULL, unrecognized), `content` (the extracted text; an export keeps only the columns the reader reads plus Retailer Name, PPC, Pack UPC and GL Code — an allow-list, so a payment column under any name is never kept; bar fix round 10/15/26, R21; since R5 held in the object store when `BLOB_STORE` is set, the column keeping the string pointer `cavnar-blob:<backend>:fintech-files/<restaurant>/<sha256>` — every reader goes through `fintech_invoices._content` / `blob_store.resolve_text` — and cleared, with its object, 365 days after the file arrived: `ops._special_retention` "fintech_file_content", `email_intake.expire_file_content`; index `idx_fintech_files_created`), `invoices` / `lines` (written from it), `status` (stored — an invoice written | unrecognized — neither format, a NUL in the text, or every invoice undated | unchanged — every invoice already on file as well or better | conflict — a balanced invoice on file disagrees, kept | location_unmapped | reading — a read in progress, committed every `COMMIT_EVERY` (500) invoices; a retry of a file left `reading` reads it again whole | too_many_rows — past `MAX_FILE_LINES` (20,000) lines or `MAX_FILE_INVOICES` (2,000) invoices, nothing written and no text kept | too_many_pages / too_slow / unreadable_pdf — a PDF past 50 pages or 30 seconds in its child-process read (`email_intake.pdf_text`) | purged — `fintech_invoices.purge_file` took its invoices out; the row stays so the same bytes are still a duplicate), `detail` (JSON: kept, conflicts, near_duplicates, skipped_locations, routed, undated). `fintech_invoices` — UNIQUE `(restaurant_id, distributor, invoice_number)` (the display strings); the invoice is matched on `number_key` (the number upper-cased, spaces, a leading '#' and leading zeros out — separators stay significant since 10/16/26, ING-18: '1-2' and '12' are two invoices; recomputed at boot for rows written before; a credit document's key is `<number>|CR|<date>`, see below) and `distributor_key` (casefolded, punctuation, entity words — LLC, Inc, Co — and "dba …" out, state names as codes) or `vendor_id` (Fintech's Retailer Vendor ID when the file has one), with a same-date same-total guard for a spelling the keys miss (R3) — the number by `loose_invoice_key` (letters and digits only) and the distributor the same or one key's words all in the other's. A number that differs only by a zero or letter prefix from the same distributor on the same date and total is a near-duplicate: both invoices are kept and the file's `near_duplicates` names them, never one replaced (ING-14). A copy running the other way from the invoice on file under its number (a credit memo, negative, against a positive invoice) is its own document — `number_key` `<number>|CR|<invoice date>` (`|DB|` for an invoice that arrives after its credit), `invoice_number` shown as "4471 credit 10/3/26" — never a version or a conflict (ING-11): `invoice_date` / `due_date` (ISO; ISO with a time, dashes, month names and YYYYMMDD are read), `total`, `location_id` (Fintech's store number), `source_format`, `file_id` (the file whose copy is current), `lines_total`, `balanced` (1 when the lines add up to the total within 2 cents and every line was read), `ref_invoice_number` (the invoice a credit memo refers to — the printout's Ref Inv Number, the export's Ref / Reference Invoice Number; NULL when it names itself), `po_number`, `po_date`. Index `(restaurant_id, number_key)`, `(file_id)`. `fintech_invoice_versions` — every copy of an invoice a file carried, UNIQUE `(invoice_id, file_id)`: `restaurant_id`, `source_format`, `total`, `lines_total`, `balanced`, `line_count`, `state` (current — its lines are the invoice's | superseded | kept — a copy that did not outrank the current one, or the same lines again | conflict — a copy whose total differs from a balanced current one: never written, reported). One copy is current: balanced over unbalanced, then the export over the printout, then the newer file; a balanced invoice is never replaced by a copy with another total (V12). `fintech_locations` — PRIMARY KEY `(restaurant_id, location_key)`: a route restaurant's Fintech store number (leading zeros out) → `target_restaurant_id`, `set_by`, `created_at`. An invoice for a store with no row is skipped and reported, never written to the route's restaurant; with no rows yet, a file naming one store teaches it as the route's own (R4). `fintech_invoice_lines` — `invoice_id`, `restaurant_id` (since 10/15/26, backfilled at boot; index `(restaurant_id, item_code)`), `line_no`, `item_code`, `description`, `upc`, `uom` (CA case, BO/BL bottle, KE keg, EA each, DS deposit), `uom_inferred` (1 when a printout line took its unit and UPC from the same distributor's latest export line of that code at the nearest price — within 1.5x, or any price for a code only ever sold by the keg), `qty` (NULL when it could not be read, never 0), `unit_price`, `cost` (extended — the truth), `deposit` (a keg's deposit inside its cost, on both formats, only on a keg line and only in whole steps of the distributor's per-keg deposit — learned from its own DS / EMPTY lines, the most common per-unit amount between $5 and $200, else $30 (Illinois); on an export line, also an Item Adjustments of one whole-dollar step of $10–$100 on every keg (BAR-CALC-11, 10/16/26); an EMPTY / EMPTIES line's own credit), `adjustment` (the rest of cost − qty × price: a discount, folded tax), `discount` (the export's Total Discount, negative), `unparsed` (1 when a figure could not be read), `kind` (item | charge — a few-dollar line on a code whose real price on any invoice from the distributor is $5 or more; a $0 line is free goods, an item | fee | adjustment — Summary Level Adjustments and the like, a code-less line is still an item | keg_credit — every EMPTY / EMPTIES / POS EMPTY KEG line, whatever its unit | unparsed — a printout line the reader could not read, no cost; its invoice reads unbalanced). `idx_fintech_lines_item` (item_code alone, cross-tenant) is unused — candidate for future cleanup after additional verification. Kept forever (cost history); a restaurant's delete takes all five tables (`restaurant_id` on each). Read by `bar.purchases._lines` (the Bar page); re-read every stored file with `scripts/bar_reread_fintech.py`.


### The Bar's tables: retention and the off-site copy (bar fix round, 10/15/26)

Every table the bar fix round added, with how long it is kept and what an off-site backup does with it (the registries are `ops._RETENTION_DAYS` / `_special_retention`, the table under *Retention* below, and `offsite_backup.SCRUB_TABLES` / `SCRUB_COLUMNS`). Each one carries `restaurant_id` except `rpower_rate_bucket`, so `models.delete_restaurant` removes it with the restaurant; nothing here holds a guest's or an employee's personal details, so none is scrubbed whole.

| Table | Grows with | Kept | Off-site copy |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fintech_files` | each file received | the row for good (a resend stays a duplicate); its `content` cleared and its object-store copy deleted past 365 days (`RETAIN_FINTECH_FILE_CONTENT_DAYS`, `fintech_file_content`) | copied |
| `fintech_invoices`, `fintech_invoice_lines` | each invoice and line | for good — they are the bar's purchase history | copied |
| `fintech_invoice_versions` | each file's copy of an invoice (one row per invoice per file) | for good, with its invoice (the record that a balanced invoice was never silently replaced) | copied |
| `fintech_locations` | each store a route's files name | for good (the store map) | copied |
| `email_intake` | each received email | 180 days (`RETAIN_EMAIL_INTAKE_DAYS`); no-route rows 30 | `address` scrubbed |
| `email_intake_routes` | each intake address | for good (a retired address stays, `active=0`) | `address` scrubbed (a write credential) |
| `bar_product_categories`, `bar_settings`, `bar_product_packs`, `bar_product_links`, `bar_expected_prices`, `bar_drink_map`, `bar_drink_specs`, `bar_stopped_distributors`, `bar_sales_map` | an owner's choice (bounded by the products and POS names) | for good | copied |
| `bar_counts` | one row per category per count date | for good (a counted pour cost needs both ends) | copied |
| `bar_waste` | each waste entry an owner or manager logs | for good (counts, never a dollar; small) | copied |
| `bar_sales_nights` | each archived night × POS cell | 1095 days (`RETAIN_POS_ARCHIVE_DAYS`, with the ticket archive) | copied |
| `pos_menu_catalog`, `pos_sales_categories` | the POS's own menu and categories (bounded) | for good, rewritten by each sync | copied |
| `rpower_rate_bucket` | one row per shared bucket | for good (a few rows; no restaurant) | copied |

## The owner's day

- `alert_holds` — an alert raised mid-service, waiting for the rush to end (`release_at` UTC, `sent_at` once handled or dropped).
- `close_outs` — one per restaurant per business date; a close-out filed after midnight belongs to the night before. The four quick lines (`went_well`, `went_wrong`, `eighty_sixed`, `callouts`) and the six the DSR reads (`equipment`, `vip_guests`, `maintenance`, `shift_notes`, `general_notes`, `influence` — Erik's "Influence/Result"), all optional free text, all the manager's own words (never rewritten; the DSR narrative is given them marked as manager-written). A re-file replaces only the fields it sends, so a pre-DSR app sending four keys never clears the other six; an empty string clears one.
- `action_snoozes` — PK (restaurant_id, key); "not today" is a date, never a dismissal.
- `pos_intraday` — PK (restaurant_id, business_date, captured_hour); net sales so far, and the same weekday/hour profile it builds for later weeks.
- `restaurants` columns: `alert_hold_during_service` (default on), `preshift_nudge_hour` (0 = off).
- `restaurants.optin_invites_enabled` (INTEGER DEFAULT 0), `optin_invites_ack_at` (UTC), `optin_invites_ack_by` (users.id) — the Toast review-link invite texts, off until the owner turns them on in Campaigns → Settings; turning on is the acknowledgement of `guest_marketing.OPTIN_INVITES_DISCLOSURE`, stored with who and when (9/28/26, `ensure_columns`, all four touch points). `run_toast_optin_invites` reads both and never a demo row.
- `optin_invite_runs` (`guest_marketing.py`, MOD-MKT-12) — PK (`restaurant_id`, `business_date`), `done_at`: a restaurant whose opt-in invites finished for that business day, so the next pass does not fetch its POS orders again. A restaurant cut off mid-list is not marked done. Grows by one row per restaurant per day; no retention entry yet.
