- `home_snapshots` (`home_snapshots.py`, scale audit 10/19/26 FE-01) — PK (`restaurant_id`, `block`, `scope_key`): Home's four slow reads kept as read models — `block` is `morning_brief`, `good_news`, `monthly_review`, `cross_module` or `location_record` (one location's row of the group view, `home_brief._location_record` — FE-09); `scope_key` who it is for (`v:<hash>` a login: id, role, grants, admin/view-as and answer authority — the brief and the cross-module links; `d:<denied modules>:s1|s0` — good news; `all:s1|s0` — the monthly review's build, filtered per login by the route, and a location's group-view row; `s1`/`s0` is whether the viewer may see the salaries, `models.viewer_sees_salaries`, since an all-in labor figure gives them away); `as_of` the restaurant's business date (a row from another day is never served); `stamp` (`home_snapshots.data_stamp`: answers/silences, issues, goals, tracked results, the nightly report, reviews by reply state, the shift history's stamp and the restaurant's settings less its `last_*` / `*_at` fields); `built_at` (epoch seconds), `build_ms`, `payload` (the build's JSON as the route's jsonify writes it); since the blind re-audit 10/20/26 (R6-F4) `viewer_json` (`{viewer, salary}`: the login it was built for as `home_snapshots.viewer_ref` keeps it — ids, role, grants, view-as fields, never a credential, email or phone — and its salary view) and `opened_on` (the last business date a request read it, written once a day per row; the precompute builds scopes opened in the last `HOME_PRECOMPUTE_ACTIVE_DAYS`), with `idx_home_snapshots_opened (opened_on, restaurant_id)`. The stamp also reads `close_outs`, `schedule_history` (drafts, `published_at`, `republished_at`) and `inventory_history` (R6-F6), and fingerprints the raw restaurant row whatever view a route passes (R6-F9). A row is replaced, never appended; one nobody opened for 45 days is deleted by the precompute (`home_snapshots.prune`), so the table is bounded by restaurants × blocks × scopes in use. Rows are built in the queue worker (`job_queue` kind `home_snapshot`, group `home`) under the viewer's salary view (`models.salary_view`), never on a request thread. The morning send keeps every recipient's brief, each built in that recipient's salary view. Created at boot (`init_home_snapshots` from `models.init_db`, which also adds the two columns to an older table).

## Recommendations (rec_ledger)

One identity and one event trail for every recommendation on every surface (`rec_ledger.py`, created at boot by `init_rec_ledger`; the owner-facing reads are `rec_learning.py`).

- `rec_instances` — one **episode** per (restaurant, key): from the first time any surface shows a recommendation until it is answered, goes stale, or is replaced. `rec_id` (uuid hex), `key` (`kind:subject`, ≤160 chars — "trim_day:Saturday", "reprice:Carbonara"), `module`, `kind`, `title`, `status`, the attributes it was first shown with (`dollar_value` — the predicted $/month, `confidence_band`, `evidence_sources` JSON, `cross_module`, `model_written`, `cavnar_completes`, `expected_metric`, `expected_by`, `first_surface`, `first_position`, `target` — a price or % it asks for), `silenced_until` (an answer holding the key quiet everywhere), `snoozed_until`, `created_at`, `last_event_at`, `closed_at`; and, added after it shipped (ROI audit, boot ALTERs in `init_rec_ledger`): `tags` (JSON list from `rec_ledger.tags_for` — weekday/weekend, daypart, topic, focus, dish, item, review category, ingredient category; stored when shown, backfilled nightly by `backfill_tags`), `implemented_at`, `tracker_id` (the `recommendation_outcomes.id` measuring it — permanent), `owner_only` (rests on figures only an owner sees — the DSR sets it from its cites; a later showing can only set it, never clear it), `superseded_by` (the rec_id that replaced it), `chain_started_at` (re-audit B11: when the recommendation an episode carries on was first shown — set on an episode that superseded an unanswered one, so the 14-day expiry runs from the chain's start; NULL = its own `created_at`). An open episode first shown without a `dollar_value`, `target`, `expected_metric` or `title` takes them from the first showing that has them (B16). The confidence snapshot (confidence audit K3, boot ALTERs): `confidence_pct`, `evidence_pct`, `accuracy_pct`, `accuracy_n`, `freshness_pct`, `freshness_as_of` (ISO), `trust_version` — written at the first showing that carries a Recommendation Confidence and never overwritten (a showing with none records accuracy and freshness only, `confidence_pct` NULL = nothing was said); every `shown` event's meta carries the same fields and `confidence_moved: {from, to}` when the overall moved 10+ points since the episode's last showing; `confidence_band` is filled from it when missing. `intel_rec_events.confidence_at` is filled from `confidence_pct / 100` by `intelligence.feedback.sync`. The memory audit (9/29/26, boot ALTERs): `silence_rule` (which rule set the answer's silence — `rec_ledger.answer_silence`'s situational_done / recurring_done / recurring_decline / done / decline / hide / measuring / safety_cycle / bad_timing / distrust — and, once lifted, `rearmed`, `reopened:<why>`, `verified` or `link_recurred` — a Done cross-module link found again, `link_memory`), `silenced_at` (when it began), `trigger_clear_at` (when a build that evaluated the kind last saw a situational trigger CLEAR after the answer), `signature` (`insight_store.advice_signature`, computed when shown — a staffing signature that keeps or adds people carries `:hold` / `:add`, re-audit 9/29/26, and one stored before that is read with the direction its key gives now; '' = computed, none; indexed `idx_rec_inst_sig` `(restaurant_id, signature)`, backfilled nightly with the tags) and `reopened_from` (the episode an answered key reopened from). Indexes: (restaurant_id, key, created_at), (status, last_event_at), (created_at), (tracker_id), (restaurant_id, created_at), (restaurant_id, signature).
- `rec_events` — the trail: `event` ∈ shown · opened · evidence_viewed · accepted · dismissed · snoozed · completed · implemented · outcome · abandoned · checkin · superseded · expired · reopened (memory re-audit 9/29/26: an answer taken back — "Use again" or a restored quiet kind, `rec_ledger.unsilence` / `unsilence_login`, with the authority of who took it back; kept for good like the answers it reverses, and read by `decisions.history`, `declined_subjects` and `rec_learning`'s `reopened` state), with `surface`, `user_id`, `role`, `meta` JSON, `at` (UTC) and, since 9/29/26, `authority` (`permissions.answer_authority` of whoever answered: principal / delegate / admin — derived from the request's login when a caller does not pass it, `rec_ledger.request_authority`, so NULL is an older answer or a system one, which every reader treats as the principal's). UNIQUE (rec_id, dedupe): a `shown` is one row per episode, surface and the restaurant's LOCAL day (`rec_ledger.local_day`, its timezone — re-audit B25); an answer carried from another table is recorded once per key across every episode (`dedupe = <event>:<source_ref>`). Meta worth knowing: `shown` carries `position` and the `dollar_value` it was shown with; `dismissed` carries `kind` (hide / not_for_us), `reason_code` (`rec_ledger.REASON_CODES`) and `reason`; `accepted` from Track carries `tracker_id`; `outcome` carries `{verdict: improved|worsened|no_clear_change|unknown, tracker_id}` and, recorded by key alone, lands on the key's latest TAKEN episode (`rec_ledger._latest_taken`, LOOPS-7 — `link_tracker` and `outcomes.autostart_implemented` likewise resolve the taken or implemented episode); a `shown` from a rank build carries `rank.arm` (the holdout arm); `abandoned` carries `tracker_id`; `superseded` carries `{why: figure_changed|target_changed|replaced, from, to, by}`; `checkin` carries `{did_it, conditions_changed, note, tracker_id, attribution: {implemented, confounded, discount}}` — `discount` true means a measured move on that tracker should not be read as the recommendation working (the owner did not do it, or something else changed). Indexes: (rec_id, event), (at), and — re-audit B21 — (restaurant_id, key, dedupe) for every answer's idempotency check and (restaurant_id, event, at) for decisions.history, shown_elsewhere_today and the timeline (both held by EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN in tests/test_reaudit_b.py).
- `rec_missed_detections` — a problem that surfaced (an alert, an issue, a close-out 86) with no recommendation covering its subject shown in the `lookback_days` (14) before: `source`, `subject_key`, `module`, `detail`, `day`. UNIQUE (restaurant_id, source, subject_key, day). Admin-only (`admin_ops.missed_detections`).
- `rec_silences` (9/29/26) — a silence for ONE login: a delegate's (manager's or teammate's) decline, or an admin's answer through view-as (`subject_id` is then the acting admin's own id, never the owner's). `subject_id`, `key`, `rec_id`, `event`, `authority`, `reason_code`, `until`; UNIQUE (restaurant_id, subject_id, key). The episode stays open for the owner; `rec_ledger.silenced_keys(viewer=)` adds a login's own silences for that login alone. At most `DELEGATE_SILENCE_MAX_DAYS` (90). Pruned 30 days past `until` (registry, floor 1).
- `rec_distrust` (9/29/26) — "don't trust the data", held per DATA SOURCE until it is re-verified: `source`, `kinds`, `first_reported_at` / `last_reported_at`, `reports`, `rec_key`, `reported_by`, `verified_at`, `verified_by`; one open row per restaurant and source. Every card resting on an open source is capped (`rec_trust`) and Data Health shows it; `rec_ledger.verify_source` closes it and releases the answers held on it. Kept forever.
- `rec_kind_states` (9/29/26) — a recommendation kind held as durable state with a review date: PK (restaurant_id, `family`, kind); `family` `home` (a kind the owner went quiet on — `QUIET_AFTER_EXPIRED` (4) left unanswered — `state` quiet, re-tested once `RETEST_AFTER_DAYS` (60) on: answered it is loud again, ignored it is quiet to the next review; `decisions.quiet_state`), `schedule` (suppressed, see `schedule_recommendation_events`) or `restore` (the owner asked for a kind back — `state` restored, `restored_at`); `reason`, `since`, `review_on`, `retests`, `last_dollars`. It replaced recounting from a 2,000-episode window and a log pruned at 365 days, which lifted a hold by accident and never gave a kind a second chance on purpose. Kept forever.
- `rec_rank_builds` (9/29/26) — what learning did to each ranking: one compact row per restaurant, surface and local day (PK), `version`, `shown` / `not_shown` (the candidates a build ranked, with the rank score, the learned weight and its rung), and since the re-audit fix round `arm` (`learned` / `holdout` — `rec_learning.apply_holdout`, LOOPS-3; boot ALTER). Only the owner's Home logs the `one_thing` row (`executive_brief(log_rank=True)` from `strategy_routes._do_cross_module` for a principal, CROSSMODULE-13). Read by the admin's rank-learning view (`admin_ops.rank_learning`). Kept 400 days (registry, floor 365).
- `automation_trust` (`automation_trust.py`, 9/29/26) — when each automation earned the owner's trust, on what, and when it lost it: PK (restaurant_id, `scope`, `subject`) — `scope` `reply_band`, `subject` "3" / "4" / "5", and since 10/10/26 `shift_request`, `subject` "drop" / "swap" (`request_autonomy`: earned by `EARN_APPROVALS` of the manager's own approvals of the kind since the last lapse, lapsed at once by a reversal of an automatic one) — `state` earned / lapsed, `earned_at`, `basis`, `lapsed_at`, `lapse_reason`. While a band is held earned, an auto-posted reply the owner neither retracted nor edited within `WEAK_CREDIT_DAYS` (7) counts `WEAK_CREDIT_WEIGHT` (0.5) of a person's approval toward KEEPING it — never toward earning it; a band that lapses is named in the trust payload so the owner is asked again. Kept forever.

**Lifecycle.** `open` → `accepted` | `completed` | `implemented` | `dismissed` | `expired` | `superseded`. A snooze keeps it open with `snoozed_until`. An episode unanswered 14 days after it was CREATED — after its chain began, for one that replaced an unanswered episode (`chain_started_at`) — closes `expired` (nightly `expire_stale`) — ignored, and in every acceptance denominator. A "Not today" on an episode the job had just expired reopens it, snoozed (B17); an open episode inside a snooze is not shown again (`present` returns None, the same rule `silenced_keys` reads). An open episode re-shown with a materially different figure (±$25 and ±25%) or target, once it is 20 hours old, closes `superseded` and a new one begins; so does every other open key of a one-live-at-a-time kind (`REPLACING_KINDS`) when a new one is shown, and every open line of an old model read when a new read is presented (`present_many(replaces=…)`) — superseded is neither answered nor ignored and counts in no rate. `implemented` is set where the change is actually made (a price applied, a supplier order sent, a reply posted to Google, a campaign or win-back sent, a post published, a schedule edited to carry the recommendation out, a week published); it moves open / expired / accepted to `implemented` and stamps `implemented_at` on any status (Done stays `completed`) — but only on an episode that is live or accepted, or ended (answered, superseded, or went stale) within `IMPLEMENT_ATTACH_DAYS` (28): a change made today is not the one a card ignored in March asked for (B15). A check-in's "yes" names its episode (`rec_id`) and always attaches. The nightly `sync_existing` carries older ledgers' answers in, links every tracker to its episode and copies its verdict and abandonment — not windowed.

**What an answer holds (memory audit, 9/29/26).** Every answer is kept in `rec_events` for good; what `rec_ledger.answer_silence(key, event, kind, reason_code, reason)` decides is only how long it silences the key everywhere (`rec_instances.silenced_until`, with `silence_rule` and `silenced_at`) — one Done or one "Pass" used to hold 3,650 days on every key. By kind and by the owner's reason (`REASON_EFFECT`: already_doing → taken, bad_timing → defer, too_costly / doesnt_fit / other → decline, dont_trust_data → distrust; a free reason that names a time — "not now", "after the holidays" — defers like bad timing):
- Done (or a "taken" reason): a situational kind (`SITUATIONAL_KINDS`, and every `diag_` / `schedule_` / `insight_` key) `SITUATIONAL_DONE_DAYS` (60) at most; a recurring kind until its next occurrence (`RECURRING_DONE_DAYS`: slow_day 6, list_idle 30, category_dip 28, post_this_week 7); any other `DONE_DAYS` (365).
- "Pass": `DECLINE_DAYS` (365), a recurring kind a season (`RECURRING_DECLINE_DAYS`, 90). Hide: `SILENCE_DAYS["hide"]` (14). Accepted or implemented: `ACCEPTED_QUIET_DAYS` (14) while its outcome is measured.
- Bad timing: a snooze of `BAD_TIMING_DAYS` (28), in no acceptance denominator. Don't trust the data: held until the source is re-verified (`rec_distrust`), `DISTRUST_HOLD_DAYS` (90) at most.
- **Safety cycle:** `stock_low` / `critical_low` never past the current stock-out — `SAFETY_CYCLE_DAYS` (7) at most, and lifted the moment a count or a delivery of the item is recorded after the answer (`silenced_keys`).
- **Re-arming:** a situational Done that a build which evaluated the kind saw CLEAR (stamping `trigger_clear_at`) and then sees firing again is re-armed, once the answer is `REARM_MIN_DAYS` (14) old (`rec_ledger.reconsider`; `silence_rule` `rearmed`).
- **Reopening:** an answered, still-silenced key reopens as a new episode that names the one it follows (`reopened_from`, `previous_answers`) when its figure is at least `REOPEN_FACTOR` (2×) the answered one and $25 more — or, for a taken answer, its target moved; never for a bad-timing, safety or snooze silence (`reopen_change`).
- **Per-login silences:** a delegate's decline and an admin's view-as answer silence for that login only (`rec_silences`) and never train the owner's preferences.

Answers from before these rules were brought back to what each holds now, counted from the answer, once at boot (`cap_answer_silences`, `cap_recurring_silences` — on the ledger and on Home's own `home_dismissals` rows).

## Strategic foundations

- `recommendation_outcomes` — one tracked change: metric, baseline window/value/detail, `evaluate_on`, verdict. Partial unique index on (restaurant_id, source_key) while `status='tracking'`. The episode it measures holds its id in `rec_instances.tracker_id`. Rec-ROI columns (boot ALTERs after the CREATE; `outcomes.init_outcomes` fills older rows at boot): `module` (who is credited — the recommendation's own module in the value vocabulary `labor|inventory|reviews|marketing|intel|other`, not the metric's), `baseline_kind` (`prior window` | `matched weekdays` | `same weeks last year`; every row written before it was a prior window), `baseline_raw` (the before-window reading before any seasonal adjustment; `baseline_value` is what the after-window is compared against), `after_detail`, `delta_pct`, `attribution` (`none|associated|consistent|held`), `concurrent` (JSON `[{kind, label, date}]`; **NULL means never checked** — a row evaluated before it existed, graded at most `associated` until its re-check reads the window), `recheck_on` / `recheck_value` / `recheck_verdict` (`held|faded|reversed|unknown`) / `rechecked_at`, `accrued_through` (the accrual cursor: the last day `outcome_value_days` has read for this row). Invariants: **one tracker in flight per metric** (`outcomes.record`'s gate, checked again inside `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; per metric *family* for automatic starts; `metric` stored normalised by `metrics.normalize`) — enforced in code, not by an index, because a family spans metric keys; `started_on` / `evaluate_on` are the restaurant's own calendar dates; an observed action's `source_key` (`observed:<action>:<YYYY-MM>`) appears at most once in any status; a row whose re-check `faded` or `reversed` no longer counts in Delivered and accrues nothing after `recheck_on`. `owner_checkin` is JSON `{did_it, conditions_changed, at}` (older rows may also carry `attribution_before`, no longer read: the grade is recomputed from the stored result by `outcomes.regrade`). Calibration columns (confidence audit 9/24/26 — boot ALTERs from `outcomes._ADDED_COLUMNS` in `init_outcomes`, never on a request path): `trigger_start` / `trigger_end` / `trigger_value` (the window that fired a triggered tracker's recommendation and the metric over it; NULL = not triggered), `baseline_overlaps_trigger` (INTEGER, default 0; 1 = the baseline overlapped the trigger window — the result is shown and **never counted**, in learning or in value), `noise_band` (this restaurant's own band for the comparison, in the metric's unit; NULL = the stated band only), `noise_sigma`, `false_alarm_rate` (two-sided, with no real change), `band_basis` (words). Invariants: a triggered tracker's baseline never overlaps its trigger window unless `baseline_overlaps_trigger = 1`; a result with a "something else changed" check-in stops counting and accruing exactly as a "no" does; `observed:supplier_order_sent:*` and `observed:untaken:<rec_id>` trackers are informational (like `observed:alert_*`) — shown, never counted, never blocking a real change. Memory audit (9/29/26): `measures_key` (from `outcomes._ADDED_COLUMNS`) is the recommendation a tracker measures when its own `source_key` is not that recommendation's — a fill-a-night text's `campaign:<Day>:<date>` measures `slow_day:<Day>` (or the feed card it began on), Ask's `ask:<title>` the recommendation it followed; the module credited, the trigger window and the episode it links to at start are read from it, and the nightly and platform syncs read the verdict under it, while `source_key` stays the tracker's own identity. `changed_at` (millisecond UTC, `idx_outcomes_changed` `(changed_at, id)`) is kept by two triggers `models.ensure_tracker_change_stamps` re-creates each boot: `trg_outcomes_changed_ins` stamps every insert, `trg_outcomes_changed_upd` stamps an update only when one of `models.TRACKER_LEARNING_COLUMNS` changed (status, verdict, the windows, the re-check, the check-in, delta, baseline…) — the daily value accrual's writes are not a change the learning reads. `intelligence.feedback.sync` reads trackers forward from a `(changed_at, id)` cursor.
- `outcome_value_days` — measured dollars, one row per tracker per measured day, PK (outcome_id, day): `restaurant_id`, `day`, `module`, `metric`, `family`, `sign` (+1 a win, −1 a change that got worse), `dollars` (signed; 0 on a day the move was read and did not hold), `held`, `counted`, `basis` (`window` — the evaluated after-window; `daily` — a trailing re-read after it). Invariants: only days actually measured get a row (`metrics.accrual_days`: a per-day metric's day with no `labor_daily_history` row gets none, a comp/void day only when the POS was asked AND it traded, a food-cost day only when it traded); a sales-priced row's `dollars` is its monthly figure's share of one TRADING day (`metrics.days_per_month`); exactly one row per (restaurant, family, day) is `counted` — the broadest held reading of the family (`metrics.BREADTH`: labor % over overtime, food cost % over waste), then the largest — so waste and food cost over the same days are one saving and a labor % win with an overtime loss is the labor % reading. `outcomes.cumulative` elects the same row at read time in SQL (a window function), leaves out trackers the owner disowned, leaves out a labor/food-cost/comp/void row on a day a sales row moved the same way, and sums sales rows apart (`sales_lift`); it is never monthly × months. Written by `outcomes.accrue_window` (at evaluation) and `accrue_daily` (the `outcome_rechecks` job), never on a request.
- `owner_goals` — target per metric, one `active` per metric (older rows become `replaced`); also `abandoned`, `achieved`, and since 9/29/26 `proposed` / `declined`, and (re-audit R3) `missed` — an active goal whose deadline passed without reaching it, retired `goals.MISSED_GRACE_DAYS` (14) later by the 6am outcomes job (`goals.retire_missed`; `progress` already leaves it out past the grace): out of every prompt and target, kept in history, listed once in Goals to renew (a new active goal, the missed one `replaced`) or close (`abandoned`). `owner_memory.target_for` reads the newest active goal still in date. A goal is the target every module judges its figure against (`owner_memory.target_for`), so only an account holder's (or an internal caller's) goal takes effect: a delegate's or an admin's is stored `proposed` (`goals.propose_goal` — a newer proposal on the metric replaces the one waiting), read by nothing that judges a figure, until an account holder confirms it (`active`, replacing the live one) or declines it (`declined`). Columns (boot ALTERs): `authority` (`permissions.answer_authority`, `system` for a job), `source`, `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at`. A set, replace, confirm or end writes a `change_log` row; a proposal changes no target and writes none.
- `ops_issues` — issue, assignee contact, status open→acknowledged→resolved, `escalation_contact_id`, `escalated_at`, `resolution_note`, `notify_suppressed` (filed with notify=False — `issues.tick` never texts it; reassigning by name lifts it), `meta_json` (e.g. a coverage issue's people, suggested covers and who was asked — since 10/3/26 (schedule audit E-4, E-31) one coverage issue per business date and role family, `source_key` `coverage:<business date>:@<role>` (`#2`… when a manager closed one and someone else went missing later that night), meta `{business_date, role, family, scheduled_in_role, people: [{employee, role, shift_start, shift_end, minutes_late, status: missing | arrived | covered, hold, asked_off, notice_minutes, seen_at, arrived_at, covered_by}], covers: [{name, score, kind: stay | off, how, for, shift_start}], asked: [{name, via, at, offer_id, for, shift_start}]}`; an older issue is one person keyed `coverage:<calendar date>:<name key>` and `issues.coverage_people` reads both shapes; written whole by `issues.meta_text`, never cut mid-JSON (16,000-character cap); `modules` — the view modules an issue's content draws on, `permissions.MODULE_VIEW_PERMISSIONS` keys — which the Monday plan writes per item (`strategy_jobs.plan_item_modules`) and every viewer-facing list filters by (`issues.hidden_modules` / `viewer_sees_issue`: a login without Food Cost never reads a food-cost plan item — memory re-audit PEOPLE-14)). Timestamps UTC `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`. Since 9/29/26 an issue text is CLAIMED before it is sent (`UPDATE … WHERE notified_at IS NULL`) and retried: `notify_attempts`, `notify_next_at` (5, 15, then 45 minutes), `notify_error`, and `notify_failed_at` once it fails for good or reaches 4 attempts — then the owner is told by push and email instead (`issues._fall_back`). Escalation texts are claimed and capped the same way (`escalation_attempts`, `escalation_next_at`, `escalation_error`).
- `issue_links` — `token_hash` → (issue, contact, purpose). One link per person; tokens are never stored. Since 9/29/26 the token is DERIVED (an HMAC under the kept secret `issue_links`, `models.kept_secret`), so a retried text carries the same link and `issue_links` keeps one row.
- `issue_routing` — PK (restaurant_id, role ∈ manager|escalation) → consented contact, `escalate_after_minutes`.
- `pos_tickets`, `pos_ticket_lines`, `pos_punches` (`pos_archive.py`, 9/29/26) — the ticket-level POS archive, one row per POS ticket, sale line and time punch, PK (restaurant_id, provider, the POS's own id), indexed by business date. A ticket keeps its server, table, room (`is_bar`), covers, entrée and drink counts, tip, daypart (`mealtime`), revenue center (`profit_center`) and net sales (its sale and discount lines); a line its item, `kind` (sale / comp / void / refund / discount / nonsale / other), amount, reason and approver; a punch its hours, rates, pay, tips, breaks and any edit. Names are as the POS had them that night; ids are the join key. RPOWER's 2000-01-01 "no value" date is stored as NULL (no kitchen screens or "needed by" times at Simple EJ's). `pos_payments` holds each payment on a ticket (method, cash or card, amount, card tip) and `pos_payouts` each cash payout or pay-in (category, amount, the approving manager, reference). `pos_item_plus` (`plu_map.py`, 10/5/26) maps Back Office's PLU numbers to the POS's own items, because RPOWER's API carries no PLU for Simple EJ's items. It is keyed by (restaurant_id, plu, bo_name), since a PLU is not unique at EJ's. Each row keeps `item_id` (the POS item, NULL until matched) and `how` (name / name+qty / qty+sales / manual; a manual row is never overwritten by an import), plus Back Office's and the POS's quantities over the report's dates as the check. It is replaced in place and does not grow. `pos_menu_prices` is the POS's price per menu item and price level as of the last nightly read (no growth; the baseline a price change is found against — each move after the first read becomes a change_log "price" row with source "sync", only for levels rung in the last 60 days, and none when more than 50 move in one night). Job roles the POS lists are mirrored into `person_roles` with source "sync" (`pos_archive.sync_roles`) for people known by their POS id, never over an owner's role. `pos_archive_days` records each archived day (counts, net sales, the newest POS write, `restated` — how often the POS rewrote it after it was archived; food & bar audit 10/16/26: `links_ok` — 1 when the night's modifier links were read, 0 when the ticketitem read failed and the day is archived again by the backfill (POS-12), NULL before 10/16/26; `recon_gap` — our revenue lines minus RPOWER's own per-ticket totals of the same sales types, and `unknown_sales` — dollars on lines of a sales type the POS's list did not name, both NULL when not compared; past $1, or any unknown dollars, the night raises a `pos_reconcile` ops event (POS-11)); since 10/19/26 (scale audit A3-05/A3-06) `pos_archive_days.punches_read_at` — when the day's punches were last read from the POS (the archive, or the nightly labor read's refresh of a recent day); `pos_archive_state` also keeps the last change read every process shares (`change_read_at`, `change_read_since`, `change_read_dates` — JSON; A3-08) and `restate_pending` (JSON dates a failed night still owes a re-store, so `changes_checked_to` advances anyway; A3-11); `pos_archive_state.changes_checked_to` is how far the change read (ticketsales/getbytimestamp and, since 10/16/26, ticket/getbytimestamp — a cancellation or tip change that rewrites no sale line, POS-19; UTC) has looked. A day is replaced whole in one transaction, and every record the POS returned must land as a row or the day is rolled back (`ArchiveCountMismatch`, POS-10); a line's `line_id` is RPOWER's rid with its atom (`rpower.line_key`, the identity `_paged` keys on — rid alone lost lines that shared one). Lines and tickets also keep the raw references RPOWER sent (layout 3: `sales_type_id`, `voided`, `mgr_id`, `void_mgr_id`, `reason_id`, `shift`; the ticket `type_*` totals), so `rpower._day_rows` rebuilds a closed, archived day in RPOWER's own shape and the nightly report, stock depletion, comps and voids and daily net read it from here instead of each downloading it again (`pos_archive.ready_dates`: closed days only — yesterday's close checked as well as today's before it is stored (POS-16) — archived on first ask, the last 3 days re-checked against the change feed 30 minutes after storing, and not served as fresh when that read fails; a day stored under layout `READ_MIN_VERSION` (3) or newer is readable, so raising `ARCHIVE_VERSION` re-stores days through the bounded backfill without making them unreadable (POS-09); on a web request the archive only — no closed-day read, no change read, nothing archived on the spot, and a day it lacks is `rpower.RPowerArchiveMiss`, unknown to the page, never a live read (POS-13); `POS_ARCHIVE_READS=0` sends every reader back to RPOWER). A night re-read from the archive keeps the department and category it was archived under (POS-20). Written nightly by the `pos_archive` job — since 10/15/26 one `pos_archive` queue row per restaurant (`strategy_jobs.run_pos_archive` fans out; R2): the last complete business date (`pos.complete_through`; the row waits, `not_before`, for the restaurant's 5am local boundary when the fan-out runs before it — POS-08) once the POS has closed it, restated days, and a backfill toward 90 days, a week per night. Layout 4 (10/15/26, bar audit F5) adds to each line `department` and `sales_category` (as RPOWER filed it that night; `category_id`), RPOWER's `cost`, `discounted`, `aqu_count`, `fraction` and `atom`, and the modifier links read from ticketitem (`parent_atom`, `mod_level`, `parent_line_id` — never summed). `pos_item_daily` (scale audit 10/19/26 DB-11 / DB-02) — each archived day's lines grouped by provider, item, name, raw and effective kind, department, sales category, category, and the per-line facts the Bar's bought-vs-sold walk needs (`is_mod`, `mult` — a "double" modifier's 2x on its line, `pure_mod`, `in_bucket`, `qty_zero`, `has_aqu`), with `lines`, `units` (raw qty), `qty_eff` (a modifier's own or its line's, absolute for comps and voids), `sales`, `aqu`, `cost_pos` / `cost_lines` (positive RPOWER costs); written by `archive_day` in the day's own transaction (`bar.variance.resolve_lines`) and for older days by `pos_archive.roll_item_days`, the nightly prune's rollup for `pos_ticket_lines` (oldest first, bounded; it refuses — lines kept — while a day past the line window is unrolled). `pos_archive_days.item_rollup` is the version the day was rolled at (`ITEM_ROLLUP_VERSION`; NULL = not rolled, reset whenever the day is stored again). The Bar's walk and item names and the recipes' sold-by-name read take it for a window whose every archived day is rolled (`pos_archive.rolled_window`), else the lines. About a tenth of the lines' rows; its own window outlives the lines', so `RETAIN_POS_LINES_DAYS` can later be set under the archive's. `pos_sales_categories` (every POS sales category with its department, `is_bar`, `alt_qty_unit`, `alt_rpt_unit`, `aqu_in_aru`) and `pos_menu_catalog` (every menu item: `category_id`, `is_mod`, `last_cost`, `plu`, `upc`, `aqu_count`) are the POS's catalog as of the last nightly read (`pos_archive.sync_catalog`; replaced per store, no growth). `rpower_rate_bucket` — one row per RPOWER token (`bucket`, a hash of it, never the token): `tokens`, `updated_at` (epoch seconds), the request budget every thread and process shares (`rpower._sleep_for_spacing`; no growth). Since 10/19/26 (scale audit A3-14) a second row, `rpower:fleet`, is the fleet-wide ceiling (`RPOWER_FLEET_RPS`), and with `RPOWER_BUDGET_PER_STORE=1` the per-token row is per (token, store).
- `pos_request_ledger`, `pos_breakers`, `pos_catalogs`, `pos_live_snapshot` (`pos_io.py`, scale audit 10/19/26 A3; created at boot by `pos_io.init_pos_io` from `init_db`). `pos_request_ledger` — every request a POS client sent, aggregated per UTC `hour` ('YYYY-MM-DD HH:00:00'), `provider`, `restaurant_id` (0 = no store: a token check or the probe) and `endpoint`: `n` (sent), `ok`, `rows_out`, `s4xx`, `s429`, `s5xx`, `transport` (timeouts, refused connections), `skipped` (refused before sending: the breaker or the budget), `ms_total` / `ms_max` and a latency histogram (`b250`, `b1000`, `b3000`, `b10000`, `bslow`); written from each process's in-memory aggregate at most every `POS_REQUEST_LEDGER_FLUSH_SECONDS` (30) as one upsert batch; the demo store never reaches it; read by the console's Operations → Integrations "RPOWER traffic" card (`/admin/api/pos-traffic`); 90 days (`RETAIN_POS_REQUEST_LEDGER_DAYS`, floor 30). `pos_breakers` — one row per provider: `open_until`, `opened_at`, `cooldown` (doubles on each failed probe to 30 minutes), `probe_at` (the half-open probe's claim), `failures`, `reason`, `paged_for` (the opening the operator was paged for); no growth. `pos_catalogs` — a provider's configuration lists kept per (`provider`, `scope` — the consolidation group, or group:store for a per-store list, `path`): `payload` (the rows as JSON), `rows_n`, `fetched_at`; replaced in place, read by every store of the group and every process (A3-07). `pos_live_snapshot` — per (`restaurant_id`, `kind` sales | clock_ins): the last read during service, `business_date`, `provider`, `value_json`, `state` (ok | not_yet), `read_at`; one row per store and kind, replaced in place (A3-02). None of the four holds a guest's or an employee's personal details beyond the names a punch list carries in `pos_live_snapshot` (replaced every 15 minutes).

`rate_buckets` (`rate_bucket.py`, scale fix 10/19/26 SEC-03) — the general form: one row per shared budget (`bucket` PK, `credit`, `updated_at` epoch seconds), taken by compare-and-swap (`rate_bucket.take`). Today `guest_sms:r<restaurant_id>` (each restaurant's campaign texts, `GUEST_SMS_PER_SECOND`) and `guest_sms:fleet` (every campaign text, `GUEST_SMS_FLEET_PER_SECOND`). A few rows, no growth, no retention, copied as is (no personal data).

The Bar's sales side (`bar/mapping.py`, 10/15/26). `bar_sales_map` — PK (restaurant_id, kind, pos_name): the account holder's choice of where a POS name's sales count (`kind` item_category — "Beer › 56. Brunch Drinks" — | department | report_category; `bar_category` beer | liquor | wine | na | not_bar), the `suggested` kind and `suggested_why` it replaced, `confirmed_at` / `confirmed_by`. A name with no row reads its suggestion and is unconfirmed (no growth beyond the POS's names). `bar_sales_nights` — PK (restaurant_id, business_date, department, sales_category): each archived night's sale lines by POS department and item category (`sales`), the cell's own discount lines (`discount_lines`), comp-type lines' value (`comp_lines`), and the check discounts allocated to it per ticket (`discounts`, of which `comps100` were 100% comps), `basis` (rpower_discounted | pro_rata | mixed | no_discounts); mapping-independent, written by `bar.sales.derive_nights` whenever `pos_archive.archive_day` stores a day.
- `pos_loss_daily` — PK (restaurant_id, business_date, kind); zero rows are written for every day×kind asked about, so absence means "never asked". `by_approver` is {POS id: {amount, events, name}}; `by_reason` (9/29/26) is {the POS's reason name: {amount, events}}. From RPOWER a void is a `voided` line or an Error Correct (type_error) line, and an amount falls back to |sales| when the prices are 0. A row with `by_reason` NULL was written under the old rules, so `loss_detection.sync` re-reads the whole nine-week baseline window once when it finds one.
- `invoice_imports` — one scanned invoice: `image_sha` (dedupe), `lines_json` (proposal), `applied_json` (old→new costs, each line `by` rule/owner, each line applied once), `applied_at` (the first apply), `auto_applied` (the trusted-supplier rule applied it — never the owner's evidence in `ordering.invoice_trust`; the lines it skipped stay open for the owner). The rule applies only lines matched by the owner's own alias (`matched_by` 'your_match'), verified and unflagged — never a name match (FC/Bar audit 10/16/26, AI-10). A proposal line carries `partial` (a one-word ingredient inside a longer product name — "Cream" on SOUR CREAM: offered, never preselected or verified, ING-09) and `price_anomaly` ({median, z} when the read price sits more than 3.5 median absolute deviations from the ingredient's own applied prices — `invoices.price_anomaly`; not preselected). The applied costs in `applied_json` are that price history.
- `permission_grants` (auth schema) — PK (user_id, restaurant_id, permission); owner-granted extras beyond a role, `permissions.GRANTABLE` only (foodcost.view, loss.view, and since 9/29/26 reviews.voice — "Writes replies in our voice": that login's approved replies teach the drafter, read at the time of use so a revoke takes them back out; see `reviews` — and marketing.voice, "Writes posts in our voice" (re-audit, LOOPS-15): that login's sent pieces in `marketing_edits` teach the marketing voice block, read the same way by `marketing_voice._voice_sql`).
- `login_prefs` (auth schema) — PK (user_id, restaurant_id); `morning_brief` NULL = role default (owners and managers on, teammates off).
- `restaurants` columns: `morning_brief_enabled`, `morning_brief_hour`, `auto_draft_schedule`, `external_scheduling_tool` (all four touch points). `auto_draft_schedule` defaults to 0 in the column, but since 10/7/26 (AI cost audit #73, narrowed by the owner in re-audit 10/7/26 #2) a restaurant that pays for Labor from `models.AUTO_DRAFT_DEFAULT_SINCE` has it on: `create_restaurant` sets it for a new row with `module_labor` on and a paying `billing_status` (`models.is_paying` — never the dataclass's `trial`, so never a self-signup, an admin-made client or a demo — `labor_auto_draft_default`), and `update_restaurant` sets it when a write grants `module_labor` (a checkout's entitlement, provisioning, an admin's grant — Labor already on by the dataclass default counts, the grant is the purchase) to a restaurant that is paying once the write lands, made since that date, whose setting nobody ever changed (no `change_log` row for the field) and that is no demo (`_auto_draft_on_labor_switch`). An existing restaurant's setting is never flipped; a write naming `auto_draft_schedule` wins.
- `restaurants.auto_draft_weekday` (INTEGER DEFAULT 3, Thursday) and `restaurants.auto_order_weekday` (INTEGER DEFAULT 0, Monday) — the owner's days, 0 = Monday as `datetime.weekday()`, in the restaurant's own calendar (9/27/26; `ensure_columns`, all four touch points). `restaurants.auto_draft_weekday_chosen` (INTEGER DEFAULT 0; AI cost audit 10/7/26 #5) says the draft day is somebody's choice — `update_restaurant` stamps it on any write of `auto_draft_weekday`, and a once-only boot migration (`models.mark_chosen_draft_days`, `data_migrations` `auto_draft_weekday_chosen_v1`) set it where the day was already in force: opted into the auto-draft or auto-publish, a stored day other than 3, or an `auto_draft_day_changed` event — and a second once-only migration (`models.keep_preexisting_draft_days`, `auto_draft_weekday_chosen_v2`; re-audit 10/7/26 #6, the owner's decision) marks every restaurant made before `AUTO_DRAFT_DEFAULT_SINCE` chosen at its stored day, so no existing restaurant's day moved — and a third (`models.keep_four_day_spread_days`, `auto_draft_weekday_spread6_v1`; scale audit 10/19/26 A5-F4) marks every restaurant still unchosen when the spread widened chosen at the four-day spread's day it read; 0 reads the spread default, Monday–Saturday by restaurant id (`AUTO_DRAFT_SPREAD_WEEKDAYS`; Monday–Thursday, `AUTO_DRAFT_SPREAD_WEEKDAYS_V1`, before). Read through `models.auto_draft_weekday` (the chosen day, else the spread; `effective_auto_draft_weekday` for a reader of the raw columns) / `auto_publish_weekday` (the draft day + 1) / `auto_order_weekday`, which fall back to the default for a value out of range: the draft may be Monday–Saturday (it drafts the week starting the next Monday, and the auto-publish needs a day before that week), orders any day.

## Intel

`competitor_snapshots` (week-over-week diffed), `ai_visibility_runs` (one row per run, with `answered`/`appeared` totals, the `city_basis` it was measured against — runs on different bases are never compared — and the `payload_json` the Intel tab serves after a redeploy; `partial` / `failed_queries` — AI cost audit 10/7/26 #10: a run some questions did not come back for is stored with `partial=1`, the missing questions, its payload, and `ai_score` NULL, so every trend, comparison and alert — all read `ai_score IS NOT NULL` — leaves it out; the next live run in the same week re-asks only `failed_queries` and completes the row in place, writing its question rows then) / `ai_visibility_query_runs` (per query: `appeared` and the `answer` text; complete runs only).

## Events and public history (`event_memory.py`, memory audit 9/29/26)

What each night teaches, measured after it is final, and the restaurant's public history. All six tables are created at boot, are small, and are **kept forever** (only valuable old; tests pin them as not pruned). Nothing here calls a model.
- `event_outcomes` — one row per night and label, written by `event_memory.record_night` once the night is final (the nightly report's hook, and the 5am `event_memory` job re-reading recent nights and backfilling history — cursors `job_cursors` `event_memory_cursor` and `event_memory_backfill:<rid>`, and `event_memory_rerecord:<rid>`, the marker `queue_rerecord` sets when a label turns loud or quiet, so the history is recorded again through the backfill cursor): `business_date`, `weekday`, `kind` (`event_memory.KINDS`: event — what the owner listed, a campaign-fed one being a campaign; holiday — never one whose date is only approximated; influence — the closer's own words; rain — OBSERVED rain during service, never a forecast; campaign; payday), `label` (normalised: "Cubs home game" and "cubs game" are one; a holiday is keyed by its calendar identity, `holiday:new_years_day` — re-audit 9/29/26, QUALITY-5, re-keyed at boot by `event_memory.rekey_record`, which since event re-audit 2 reads only the rows above its marker, `job_cursors` `event_memory_rekeyed_through` (the highest id it has brought to the rules); a catalog game's label carries its season class word — preseason, playoff, special — and `event_memory.label_counts` never counts a night carrying a class word the asked label lacks) and `raw_label`, `source`, `net`, `baseline` / `baseline_n` (the median of the same weekday over `BASELINE_WEEKS` (8) before it, other flagged nights left out), `lift_pct`, `basis` and `net_source` (ONE basis, `canonical_facts.net_series`), `covers`, `labor_pct`, `owner_lift_pct` (the owner's own guess, beside), `figure_kind` (`measured`), `confounded` (1 when the night carried more than one thing — its one lift belongs to no single label; since the event re-audit (10/1/26) a followed game's own row is confounded by anything else on its night, quiet or loud, and any other label only by loud things, so a quiet game confounds no label but another game's; the boot pass `rekey_record` re-marks only nights recorded before `CONFOUNDING_MARKED_FROM`, 9/30/26) and `co_labels` (JSON, what else was on it) — re-audit 9/29/26, QUALITY-4, boot ALTERs; UNIQUE per restaurant, date, kind and label. A night re-recorded replaces its rows; a night carrying an if-necessary game with no result yet (`event_intel.store.unresolved_refs`) has none until a result or a cancellation is entered. Since 9/30/26 each row also keeps who worked the night — `headcount` (people punched in), `headcount_json` (`{role: people}`) and `labor_hours`, from `shift_facts` (`event_memory._night_staff`) — and `measured_effect` carries a label's `staffing` (median people by role and labor % on its nights).
- `event_effects` — one row per label (PK restaurant, label): `n`, `median_lift_pct`, `low_lift_pct` / `high_lift_pct`, `direction`, first and last date, weekdays, `owner_guesses` / `owner_median_pct`, `confounded` (the summary rests on shared nights: a label is measured on its own nights when those alone clear the floor) and `drift_json` (`{was, recent, n_recent, since}` when the last 3 nights all sit outside the older nights' spread — the recent median is then the figure; QUALITY-21). The median is age-weighted: a night counts half for every 2 years it is older than the label's newest night, in whole years. Read by `demand_signals.by_date` and `demand.forecast_day` (behind `EFFECT_MIN_N`, 3 nights), `dsr.predictions` (a rain or event effect is stated only in this restaurant's measured direction) and `forecast_log.explained_by`.
- `weather_daily` — the weather that HAPPENED per local day (PK restaurant, date), from the nearest National Weather Service station's observations: `high_f`, `low_f`, `precip_in`, `rain`, `wet_hours`, `conditions`, `station`, `n_obs`, `source` (`nws_observed`), `kind` always `measured` — a forecast is never stored here. Scheduler audit 10/10/26 LEARNING-21 (boot ALTERs in `init_event_memory`): `lunch_wet` / `dinner_wet` (an observation during service before / after the restaurant's lunch-dinner boundary was wet), `lunch_temp_f` / `dinner_temp_f` (the mean of each meal's readings), `snow` (snow, sleet, ice pellets or freezing rain during service) — NULL on rows captured before, which are read by the day's own figures; `demand_drivers.weather_effects` learns rain, snow, cold and heat per meal from them. The nightly report shows it beside its forecast (`dsr.access._live_weather`).
- `own_rating_history` — the restaurant's OWN public Google rating, one row per ISO week (PK restaurant, week; the week's latest reading): `rating`, `review_count`, `source` (places / business_profile / on_file — the boot backfill's first week). `restaurants.gbp_rating` is overwritten in place; this is what proves "4.3 to 4.6 since you joined".
- `market_events` — a competitor arriving, leaving or moving: `place_id`, `name`, `kind` (`arrived`, `gone`, `rating_up`, `rating_down` — a move of `MARKET_MOVE_STARS` (0.2★) since its last marked reading — and `tracked`, a row that only holds the reading a move is measured from: an anchor, never listed), `from_rating`, `to_rating`, `review_count`, `observed_on`; UNIQUE per restaurant, place, kind and day.
- `competitor_rating_monthly` — each competitor's rating per month (PK restaurant, place, month): `name`, `rating`, `review_count`, `captured_at`. `competitor_snapshots` is pruned at 365 days; this outlives it.

At boot, once per restaurant with no history yet, the public history is replayed from the `competitor_snapshots` still on file and the current Google rating becomes the first rating week.

## Billing / Contracts / Webhooks

- `docusign_events_seen`, `stripe_events_seen` — inbound-webhook idempotency, event id de-dup before acting. `stripe_events_seen` is kept 90 days (Stripe retries an event for three).
- `webhooks` — one row per client-configured outbound webhook (`url`, `secret`, `events` JSON list, `is_active`, `consecutive_failures`/`disabled_reason` for auto-disable after repeated failures, and since 9/29/26 `last_success_at`).
- `webhook_deliveries` — attempt log (`status`, `ok`, `attempts`, `error`) for each outbound fire; `event_id` (9/29/26) is the id the payload and its `X-Cavnar-Event-Id` header carry, so a receiver can drop a redelivery.
- `webhook_outbox` (webhooks.py, 9/29/26) — every outbound event, written before it is handed to the bounded pool: `event_id` UNIQUE, `restaurant_id`, `event_type`, `payload_json`, `state` queued → delivering → delivered | failed (`expired` when a restart left it behind too long), `attempts`, `last_error`, `done_at`. `webhooks.reap_webhook_outbox` re-drives what a restart left queued.
- `idempotent_responses` (`strategy_routes._idempotent`, CLIENT-21) — answers to retryable requests, PK (`restaurant_id`, `route`, `idem_key` — the client's Idempotency-Key), `status`, `payload_json`, `created_at`: a retry after a timeout is answered from the first result. The paid scans (invoice and recipe, `invoice_scan` / `recipe_scan`: no second model call), the offline queue's waste lines and deliveries, and since 10/10/26 a schedule save (`schedule_save:<history_id>`, `mobile_api._idempotent_save` — BUGSLIFE-22: a retried save wrote a second `edited` version and ran the learning capture twice). A row with no payload is a first request still in flight — a repeat is answered 409 `{in_progress: true}` for 10 minutes (a scan) or 2 (a save, `mobile_api.SAVE_PENDING_MINUTES`); a 5xx is never stored. Rows older than 7 days are deleted on the next request that reads the table.
- `stripe_subscriptions` — the subscription MIRROR (one row per restaurant, `restaurant_id` PK; 9/29/26): `subscription_id`, `session_id`, `customer_id`, `status`, `interval`, `interval_count`, `amount_cents` (the unit amount), `currency`, `quantity`, `discount_pct`, `trial_end`, `current_period_end`, `cancel_at_period_end`, `canceled_at`, `ended_at`, `cancellation_reason`, `price_id`, `module_keys`, `module_mismatch` (what Stripe's plan and the local module flags disagree about), `last_event_id`, `updated_at`. Filled from checkout (one Stripe read), from subscription created/updated/deleted events and nightly by `billing_jobs.reconcile_stripe`. A NULL `status` is a legacy row and is read as live; an ended subscription keeps its row with its terminal status (never counted live) until the next checkout replaces it. MRR is `billing_jobs.billed_mrr` over this table, each subscription counted once however many locations it covers.
- `stripe_billing_clock` (`webhook_routes._stale_billing_event`) — the newest billing-state event applied per restaurant (`restaurant_id` PK, `last_event_created` Stripe's epoch, `last_event_id`, `updated_at`): Stripe delivers out of order, so an older subscription event that arrives after a newer one is recognised as stale and never rolls the billing status back. One row per restaurant, so no retention entry.
- `stripe_invoices` (billing_jobs.py, 9/29/26) — one row per Stripe invoice (`invoice_id` PK): the restaurant and customer, `status`, `billing_reason`, `kind`, subtotal / discount / tax / total / due / paid / remaining cents, `recurring_cents` apart from `setup_cents`, `attempt_count`, `next_payment_attempt`, `hosted_invoice_url`, `invoice_pdf`, `charge_id` (so a dispute finds its restaurant without a Stripe call), `payment_intent_id`, the period, `paid_at`, `last_failed_at` (the past-due age the console reads).
- `stripe_duplicate_subscriptions` — a second checkout's subscription, cancelled against the first: its refund, invoices and cancellation are recognised and change nothing on the paying account.
- `billing_status_history` (DDL in billing_jobs.py, written by `models.update_restaurant`) — `restaurant_id`, `field` (one of `models.BILLING_HISTORY_FIELDS`), `old_value`, `new_value`, `source` (`stripe`, `docusign`, `admin_override`, `request:<endpoint>`, `system`…), `actor`, `stripe_event_id`, `reason`, `created_at`. Written in the same transaction as the change; kept when the restaurant is deleted.
- `billing_reconcile` — one row per restaurant: what the nightly reconcile found wrong against Stripe (`local_status`, `stripe_status`, `subscription_id`, `mismatches`, `first_seen_at`). The reconcile never changes billing status itself.
- `owed_sends` (billing_jobs.py, 9/29/26) — the billing email outbox. A billing email (the post-signing payment link and welcome, receipts, dunning, the pay-link chase, the provisioning welcome) is a row first, written in the step that owes it: `kind`, `dedupe_key` UNIQUE, `to_email`, `payload_json`, `status` pending → sending (claimed, `locked_until`; a claim whose lock ran out is taken again) → sent (or `sent_unverified` when an old-shape sender returned None, so it cannot loop) | pending again with a later `next_attempt_at` | failed; `cancelled` (dunning for an invoice since paid, voided or written off), `attempts` (6 tries over about two days, with backoff), `next_attempt_at`, `locked_until`, `last_error`, `last_status_code`, `message_id`, `source`, `actor`, `sent_at`. A permanent failure is `failed` plus an `ops.capture` (job `owed_send:<kind>`). Anything a Stripe event owes is sent only by the billing jobs; DocuSign signing and admin actions drain their own rows at once.
- `webhook_verifications` (9/29/26) — per inbound provider (Stripe, DocuSign): `last_verified_at`, `last_verified_event`, `last_failure_at`, `last_failure_error`, `failures_total`, `failures_since_verified`, `last_captured_at` (a failure reaches `ops.capture` at most once an hour per provider). Written by `webhook_routes._webhook_seen`, which since the integration wave also writes the Stripe and DocuSign verdicts to `inbound_webhook_health` (`models.record_inbound_webhook`); still read by the billing health panel.
- `inbound_webhook_health` (9/29/26) — the same for Resend and Twilio (inbound and status callbacks): the last verified event and its type, verified and failed counts, `failed_since_verified`, `last_failure_reason`, `last_capture_at`, and Resend's bounce and complaint counts and last times. Since the integration wave it holds every provider, Stripe and DocuSign included, and the console's webhook health reads it first (`admin_ops._webhook_health`), falling back to `webhook_verifications` only for a Stripe or DocuSign row written before it. `webhook_verifications` as a second table is a candidate for future cleanup after additional verification.
- `docusign_envelopes` gains `status` / `status_at` / `status_reason` (the envelope's own DocuSign state), `module_count` / `modules_list` (the terms it was sent with, so a resend knows whether the module list changed), `resend_count` / `last_resent_at`.
- `app_secrets` — this install's own signing secrets, never `SECRET_KEY`: `email_links` (unsubscribe links) and, since 9/29/26, VERSIONED kept secrets `join_links:vN`, `pay_links:vN`, `issue_links:vN`, `intel_org_hash:vN` (the key of the benchmark bands' organisation hashes, `intelligence.privacy.org_hash`) (`models.kept_secret(name, version)`; `rotate_kept_secret` mints the next version and every older one keeps verifying, because the version rides in the token). Emptied from every off-site backup copy.

## Admin / Ops

`job_runs` also carries `result_json` (a sweep's `{attempted, ok, failed, skipped, hit_bound}`) and `claim_job` (the `claim_period` key it ran under, so `ops._reclaim_dead_run` finds a run whose claim key and job name differ — `ops_digest` runs `ops_failure_digest`), added at boot by `ops.init_ops`; `ok` is 1 clean, 0 failed (raised, or every restaurant attempted failed), 2 partial (some failed, or the time bound cut the pass short) — `ops.run_outcome`. `source_health` (the Data Health ledger) is written by every ingest point: `pos` (nightly, retry, Sync now — `next_retry_at` +1h/+3h/+6h, never on auth), `reviews` (provider = the fetch mode: `gbp`, `places_fallback`, `places`), `gbp` (a Business Profile connection's own attempt — its consecutive failures are the fallback slots), `depletion` (`data_through` = last complete business day depleted), `loss`, `marketing` (beside `job_cursors metrics_sync:<rid>`), `competitor`, `visibility`, `weather`, `dsr` (the nightly report's sales collection) and `pos_intraday`. `data_health_daily` is written by the daily `scheduler.run_data_health_daily` sweep and read by the admin rollup.

`admin_events` event types an admin's own tools write since 9/29/26 (beside the request hook's rows): `client_settings.update` (a diff of the touched fields), `billing_status.override` (with the required reason), `staff_note.saved` / `staff_note.removed`, `brand.update`, `reviews.import`, `client_data.upload`, `alert_contact.added` / `alert_contact.removed`, `job.run_now`, `vendor_cost.set`, `email.unsuppressed`, `email.value_recap`, `alert_storm_cap.lifted`, `ai.breaker_reset`, `ai.retry_stalled`, the billing actions and the offboarding steps; the settings page's `admin_settings_update` activity row carries the diff.

`activity_log` event `target_change` (9/24/26): written by `models.update_restaurant` inside the same transaction when one of `models.OWNER_TARGET_FIELDS` (`labor_target_pct`, `food_cost_target`, `waste_target_pct`, `monthly_revenue_target`, and since 10/14/26 `bar_cost_target`, `beer_cost_target`, `liquor_cost_target`, `wine_cost_target`) actually changes value — `event_data` `{"field", "from", "to"}`, `created_at` UTC. Re-saving the same value writes nothing. Read by `rec_trust.owner_changes` (with `schedule_history.published_at`, `reprice_decisions` and `rec_events` completed/done) for the `changed_since` confidence flag; not one of the event types the owner's Account activity list shows.

`admin_events` (the audit trail — see *Operations, audit and billing ledgers* for its 9/29/26 columns), `admin_issue_resolutions` (+ `occurrence_at`, 9/29/26), `job_runs`, `job_failures`, `job_period_claims`, `job_cursors` (where a bounded pass stopped; also per-restaurant source state — `places_total:<rid>` the last Google total seen, `places_coverage:<rid>` JSON {since, google_growth, stored, fetches} read by `fetcher.places_coverage`, `places_history:<rid>` JSON {ISO date: Google's total that day} for the last 21 days (`fetcher.record_places_total`, read by `fetcher.places_growth_per_day`), `review_fetch_quiet:<rid>` "1"/"0" — whether the restaurant is on the quiet Places-only fetch cadence, decided at each review-fetch slot (`fetcher.decide_quiet`, read by `fetcher.review_fetch_slots`; AI cost audit 10/7/26 #45), `review_diagnoses_batch_cursor` / `food_cost_diagnoses_batch_cursor` / `labor_prewarm_cursor` (the 10/7/26 #58 and #100 passes'), `metrics_sync:<rid>` JSON {last_attempt_at, last_ok_at, error} read by `scheduler.metrics_sync_state`, `weekly_digest_cursor` the weekly digest pass's (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #52), `event_memory_rekeyed_through` the highest `event_outcomes` id `event_memory.rekey_record` has read), `scheduler_lease`, `milestones` (firsts an owner is told about once), `activity_log`, `async_jobs` (`kind` includes the admin work since 9/29/26: `menu_extract`, `client_setup`, `admin_seed_drafts`, `admin_redraft`, `admin_ig_refresh`, `admin_review_account`, `review_fetch_one`, `pos_sync_one`, `event_sync_one` (an owner's follow or Put back re-sync, 10/1/26), `event_catalog_resync`, `competitor_intel`, beside the owner's `schedule`; and `ai:insight_<labor|reviews|marketing>:<hash>` — a module read written off the request, stale-while-refresh, iOS parity #37: `insight_refresh`, result `{payload: {ok, error?, stale?}, http_status}`), `ai_usage` (every AI and data-API call's outcome, cost and tokens — see *AI reads*), `changelog_entries` (release notes; `whats_new.ENTRIES` synced at boot) / `changelog_seen` (`user_id` PK, `seen_at` 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS' — What's new seen per login since 10/12/26; a login with no row falls back to `restaurants.changelog_seen_at`), `status_incidents` / `status_incident_updates` / `service_status` (the public status page; an open incident holds the services it names at its severity), `sales_audits` (`linked_restaurant_id` links an audit to the client it became, audited) / `sales_audit_shares` (the in-person sales tool; since 9/29/26 a share token is stored as `sha256:<hash>` in `token`, with `expires_at` (90 days — links from before the change got at least 30 more days at the boot migration), `token_enc` (the link encrypted under `CREDENTIAL_KEY`, so it can be copied again; NULL without the key, and scrubbed from off-site copies) and `token_hint` (its last four characters)), `value_snapshots` (daily "value delivered" figure, populated opportunistically on first Home-tab load of the day — not a scheduled job).
