## Email

`email_log` (every send, for the Account "email history" view and debugging), `email_suppressions` (bounces/opt-outs), `onboarding_emails` (day-2/7/30 sequence state), `weekly_reports`, `login_reports`, `client_data` (miscellaneous cached client-facing snapshots).

Received mail (`email_intake.py`, 10/14/26; bar fix round 10/15/26): `email_intake_routes` — an intake address (PRIMARY KEY, lowercased, `<kind>-<random>@<domain>` from `email_intake.new_route`, 64 random bits since 10/15/26), its `restaurant_id`, `kind` (fintech), `active` (0 once retired — `rotate_route` retires the old address and issues a new one with the same senders), `note`, and (boot ALTERs, 10/15/26) `allowed_senders` (newline-separated whole addresses or `@domain` entries; mail from anyone else is held), `created_by` (the admin), `retired_at`; index `(restaurant_id, kind, active)`. `email_intake` — one row per received email, UNIQUE `email_id` (Resend's id; a retried webhook is the same row): `address`, `restaurant_id`, `kind`, `sender`, `subject`, `status` (queued | no_route — an address with no route or a retired one, never fetched | held_sender — the sender is not on the route's list | held_auth — Resend's SPF/DKIM/DMARC verdict did not pass (`email_intake.auth_verdict`: DMARC pass, or DKIM pass where the domain publishes no DMARC policy); both never read until an admin releases them | done — a file wrote invoices | ignored — files arrived, none wrote invoices | no_files — no attachment (an email's own text is never read as a file) | gmail_confirmation — Gmail asking the address to confirm a forward | failed — retried by the queue, or given up by `intake_reconcile` after three jobs), `detail` (each file's result, or why it was held), `files` (files that wrote invoices), `received_at`, `processed_at`, and (boot ALTERs) `auth` (Resend's `authentication` JSON: spf, dkim, dmarc), `released_by` / `released_at` (the admin who released a held email). Indexes `(restaurant_id, received_at)`, `(received_at)` (retention), `(status, received_at)` (the reconcile). Retention 180 days (`RETAIN_EMAIL_INTAKE_DAYS`), `no_route` rows 30 (`ops._special_retention` "email_intake_unrouted"); both address columns are scrubbed from off-site copies.

Invariants since the fix round (9/29/26):
- **`email_log.sent_at` is UTC** (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`). It was America/Chicago wall time in the same form, which every reader treated as UTC; a one-time, DST-aware boot migration converted the old rows and recorded itself in `data_migrations` (`email_log_sent_at_utc`). Readers compare in UTC; `get_email_log_for_client` adds `sent_at_local` for display. Every row is written by `emails.deliver` against its restaurant — no hand-written "sent" rows for payment, welcome or contract any more.
- **`email_suppressions.scope`** is `all` (NULL and '' read as all), `marketing` (Cavnar AI's own promotional mail) or `guest` (a restaurant's guest mail) — a comma list may hold two. Scopes only WIDEN: a later hard bounce widens a narrower row, a narrower signal never narrows a wider one. A complaint about owner mail suppresses marketing only; a bounce on an unidentified send suppresses all. The operator's own addresses (`models.operator_addresses`) are never written here — a bounce on one is captured for the operator instead — and operator MAIL is never suppressed whatever its address: every type in `emails.OPERATOR_EMAIL_TYPES` and every type starting `ops_` (`emails.is_operator_mail`), so a new ops sender such as `ops_weekly_digest` cannot forget to join the list. `models.unsuppress_email(email, actor, reason)` lifts one and is audited; SQL readers use `models.suppressed_scope_sql`.
- **`onboarding_emails.status`** is `sent` (Resend accepted it), `failed` (refused for good — not retried, raised to the operator), `covered` (another location of the same owner got it) or `skipped` (deliberately not sent); `error` says why. A row used to be written after every attempt, delivered or not.

## AI reads

`insight_cache` (`insight_store.py`): one stored model read per `(restaurant_id, kind)` — reviews, food, marketing, labor — with the `fingerprint` of the data it was written from. A read is reused while the fingerprint matches (and for at most a week), so web and iOS show the same words and a new read is written only when the data changes. The Reviews, Food and Labor fingerprints are `insight_store.read_fingerprint` (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #11, #26, #27): the prompt less the date it names, every "N days ago" and the DATA STATE block (its states, dates and decision go in instead), plus the ISO week (Reviews, Food) or the local day (Labor); the Labor key also leaves out the read's own last claim, which every read rewrote. Because the two weekly reads are served again on later days, their prompts give dates, never ages (the Food data window's counts by M/D/YY, and `insight_store.DATED_NOT_AGED` told to both — context re-audit 10/7/26 #8). Kind `reviews:locations` (re-audit 10/8/26 #1): the Reviews read written for a login shown its other locations (`client_api.review_store_kind`) — it carries their complaint themes, so it is its own row and its kept history is the owner-level surface `review_read_locations`; every read of the stored Reviews read (the stale-while-refresh answer, the refused-read and synchronous fallbacks) takes the kind matching `_review_sees_locations`, and a plain `reviews` row that still carries location themes is never served to a login that may not see them. Kind `<kind>:refused` (`hold_refusal` / `held_refusal`, #48): the fixed copy a refused read served, held against its prompt's fingerprint for `REFUSAL_HOLD_HOURS` (6) so the identical prompt is not sent again — never history, never the read's own row. A read written by a call site that runs the Response Validation Layer (workstream A) is stored wrapped — `payload` = `{"_rv": <engine version>, "raw": <the model's text before validation>, "out": <what the owner is shown>, "validation": {verdict, caveats, controls, codes, version}}` (`insight_store.put(..., raw=)`). When the engine's `VERSION` changes, `insight_store.get(..., revalidate=)` re-validates `raw` (no model call) and rewrites the row's payload, its `created_at` unchanged; a pre-engine row (a bare string) is re-validated from its own text, its old `UNVERIFIED:` line removed first. No column was added. The same rule holds for two reads stored elsewhere, also without a new column: `restaurants.competitor_intel` (a JSON blob, `competitor.intel_blob`) now carries `validation` (with its `version`) beside the insight, and `competitor.current_intel` re-checks a blob from an older version or from before the engine on read (never storing the model's raw text, since the blob is served to the browser whole); `review_diagnoses` / `food_cost_diagnoses` rows have no room for a version, so `get_diagnosis` / `get_diagnoses` re-validate each row's text on every read and do not serve one that no longer stands.

`ai_validation_log` (models.init_db; written by `ai_utils.log_validation` from `response_validation.log`; `call_id` since 9/29/26, the traced call it checked): one row per model output the Response Validation Layer checked — `restaurant_id`, `surface` (the engine's closed list), `action` (the `ai_usage` action key, so rates join on it), `verdict` (pass | caveat | withhold | refuse), `rules` (JSON list of rule codes), `tokens` (JSON list of each finding's offending token, each ≤ 60 chars, at most 20), `n_rewrites`, `n_drops`, `n_caveats`, `text_hash` (sha256 prefix of the original output), `mode` (shadow | enforce), `version`, `created_at`. Invariant: **no answer text and no guest text** — only codes, counts, the hash and short tokens from the model's own words (an echo finding stores no token). Pruned at 120 days (`prune_operational_logs`). Read only by `admin_ops.validation_rates` (`GET /admin/api/validation`). **A re-check of an unchanged stored text is not written again** (AI orchestration design W4, 10/7/26): a check with no model call behind it (`call_id` NULL) whose restaurant, surface, `text_hash`, mode, verdict and version already have a row inside `VALIDATION_RECHECK_DEDUPE_DAYS` (7) is skipped — `drafter.recheck_draft_flags` re-reads every flagged reply draft at each boot (action `recheck_flag`, regex only) and had made `reply_public` read 310 checks of 27 texts. A changed verdict or rules version is written. Indexed on (`text_hash`, `surface`) for the probe.

### The Message Batches queue (`ai_batches.py`, AI cost audit 10/7/26 #19)
Model calls nobody waits on (the nightly DSR narrative) go through Anthropic's Message Batches API at half the list price; created at boot by `ai_batches.init_ai_batches` (from `init_db`), retention 30 days (*Retention*).
- `ai_batch_jobs` — PK `batch_id` (Anthropic's): `workflow`, `status` (`in_progress` → `canceling` → `collected`), `n_items` and the result counts (`n_succeeded`, `n_errored`, `n_expired`, `n_canceled`), `submitted_at`, `ended_at`, `checked_at` (the collector's last look), `collected_at`, `error` (a pass that could not read it), `cancel_requested_at` (scale audit 10/19/26 A4-07, a boot `ALTER` in `init_ai_batches`; naive UTC: the collector cancelled the batch because every item still out in it was past its cutoff — its items wait for it to end, answered ones used, cancelled ones falling back, for `AI_BATCH_CANCEL_GRACE_SECONDS`; NULL = not cancelled for its cutoffs). The collector (`ai_batch_collect`) reads every row still `in_progress` / `canceling`, oldest first.
- `ai_runs` shadow rows (AI orchestration phase 6, 10/7/26): a replay of a cheaper tier on a kept production request (`ai_learning.shadow_arms`, through Message Batches as workflow `shadow_arms`, ledger action `shadow_arms`, no restaurant) is an `ai_runs` row with `shadow_of` = the production run, `trigger` `shadow`, `final_tier` the candidate, `cost_usd` on production's pricing basis (the batch cost ÷ the batch multiplier for a synchronous production run; the batch cost for a batched one — both in `context_json`) and `reviewer_score` the candidate's score from the workflow's Haiku rubric. Since the re-audit (10/7/26 #2) both texts are judged the same way: the candidate cut and redacted as the trace keeps production's (`ai_learning._as_traced`), both first through the deterministic check on the workflow's surface (`ai_learning.SHADOW_SURFACES`; a refused candidate scores 0, a refused production text leaves the pair uncounted), production always re-scored with the candidate's context — its score in the shadow row's `context_json.production_score`, never written on the production row — and production's cost read as its FIRST call's (`context_json.production_first_cost`). Only requests kept on the first rung of the ladder in force (`ai_run_requests.tier`), from production runs that started there and never escalated, are replayed. A pair without both figures is not counted. Every statistic and outcome reads `shadow_of IS NULL`.
- `ai_runs.canary` (context re-audit 10/7/26 #3; a boot ALTER in `ai_orchestrator.init_ai_orchestration`): for a workflow whose policy carries `canary`, 1 when the run started on the cheap first rung (a restaurant in `AI_CANARY_RESTAURANTS`), 0 when it was held back a rung (every other restaurant); NULL for a workflow not canaried and for rows before 10/7/26.
- `ai_batch_items` — PK (`workflow`, `custom_id`; a custom_id is used once): `batch_id`, `restaurant_id`, `action` (the `ai_usage` action), `model`, `callback` (`"module:function"`, reached by name), `context_json` (handed back with the answer), `request_z` (the request as sent, zlib-compressed JSON — kept only until the answer is ledgered and traced, then NULL), `status` (`queued` → `submitted` → `collecting` → `done` | `failed`; `blocked` — a gate refused it, never sent; `submit_failed`; `cancelled` — its caller stopped waiting → `discarded` when the answer lands; `cut_off` — past its `cutoff_at`, cancelled with its callback owed → `cancelled` once told, or `discarded` when its answer landed meanwhile, platform re-audit 10/7/26 #1; a `collecting` or `cut_off` item claimed over 15 minutes ago whose callback is not running is told again once, `reason` `stale: …`, then closed, #7), `reason`, `result_type` (`succeeded` | `errored` | `expired` | `canceled`), `stop_reason`, `outcome`, `error`, `usage_json` (the token counts), `cost_usd` (at the batch rate), `call_id` / `usage_id` (its `ai_calls` trace and `ai_usage` row), the attribution it was submitted under (`trigger`, `actor_user_id`, `correlation_id` — the item's own when it carried one: a workflow run's id where several runs share a batch), `callback_error`, `created_at`, `submitted_at`, `claimed_at`, `collected_at`, `cutoff_at` (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #59–#62, a boot `ALTER` in `init_ai_batches`; naive UTC — still out past it, the collector cancels the item and its callback is told `BatchItemFailed("cutoff")`, so its caller writes it synchronously; NULL = no cutoff).
### The orchestration ledgers (`ai_orchestrator.py`, AI orchestration design 10/7/26)
Created at boot by `ai_orchestrator.init_ai_orchestration` (from `init_db`; later columns by its boot ALTERs).
- `ai_runs` — PK `run_id`: one row per workflow run (`workflow`, `restaurant_id`, `subject`, `policy_version`, `start_tier` — the rung a pre-router moved it to, NULL on the first — `first_tier` (re-audit 10/7/26 #9: the tier its first attempt ran on; the learner reads only runs whose `first_tier` is the first rung of the ladder in force, under today's `policy_version`, since the workflow's override), `final_tier` / `final_model`, `attempts`, `escalations`, `steps_json`, `status` (`ok` | `failed` | `capped` | `held` | `error` | `refused` | `off` — `AI_WORKFLOW_OFF`), `verdict` (`fallback` when the caller threw the answer away after the run — `ai_orchestrator.mark_fallback`, #11), the reviewer's score, cost and tokens from the ledger, and the owner's `outcome` / `outcome_quality` / `outcome_detail` / `outcome_at`). An outcome or a later reviewer verdict lands on the run the caller names, else the subject's newest SERVED run (`ok`, `failed`, `capped`; never a shadow) — #5.
- `ai_run_requests` — PK (`run_id`, `seq`): a sampled run's kept requests, redacted like the trace (the guest names collected from all of the request's text first — #1), compressed; `seq` is the rung's step + 1 (the first call of each rung only), `tier` the rung it was sent on (#6; a row with no tier is never replayed). A batch item that is a run's first rung keeps its request at submit (`ai_orchestrator.keep_batch_request`).
- `ai_gate_verdicts` — PK `text_hash` (sha256 of kind, restaurant and the exact text a guest reads): the send gate's verdict (`ok`, `score`, `reasons`) on a guest text or email (`ai_reviewer.gate_send`, re-audit 10/7/26 #8), so a gated draft sent as it was, or a resend, is not read again for `GATE_VERDICT_DAYS` (30). Only a verdict a reviewer actually gave is kept.
- `digest_summaries` — PK (`restaurant_id`, `fingerprint`; AI cost audit 10/7/26 #50, #61): the weekly digest's model answer per prompt (`reporter.digest_fingerprint` — the prompt without its DATA STATE block, plus the readiness verdict and data state it was built from): `raw_text` / `stop_reason` / `call_id` (an answer a Message Batches item brought back the night before the send — `reporter.on_digest_batch`), `parsed_json` (the checked summary the digest renders), `source` (`batch` | `sync`), `created_at`. Read only within 24 hours (`DIGEST_KEEP_HOURS`), so a preview, a resend or a retried send serves the same week's summary; pruned on every write past 3 days (`DIGEST_KEEP_PRUNE_DAYS`). **A batch answer's `ai_usage` row** is written like a synchronous call's, priced at `ai_utils.BATCH_PRICE_MULTIPLIER` (0.5) of list — cache write and read rates included — with `price_version` `ai_utils.BATCH_PRICE_VERSION` (`<PRICE_VERSION>+batch`); an errored, expired or canceled request is a zero-cost `error` row, `reason` `batch_<type>`.
- `ai_batch_items` for the morning diagnoses (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #58): custom ids `rd-<rid>-<YYYYMMDD>-<theme hash>` and `fd-<rid>-<YYYYMMDD>`, so a re-run of the same morning's batch is a duplicate, never a second request; their `context_json` carries the compressed state the answer is validated against.

### The Restaurant Context Manager's sections (`restaurant_context.py`, AI orchestration design Phase 2, 10/7/26)
`context_sections` — created at boot by `restaurant_context.init_restaurant_context` (from `init_db`): the L2 copy of each built context section. PK (`restaurant_id`, `section`, `viewer_scope`): `section` is a `restaurant_context.SECTIONS` name (profile, owner_rules, roster, findings, kpis, sales_trend, labor_trend, events, weather, memory, alerts, data_state); `viewer_scope` is who it was built for — `all` (the same for everyone), `principals`, `team[:grants]`, `login:<id>:<role hash>` (a changed role or grant is a new scope), `salaries` / `hourly` (the labor trend with or without the salaries) — with `;p<hash>` when a caller narrowed the section (`params`; a param that only moves with the day — the memory section's `now` and `subjects`, `restaurant_context.VERSIONED_PARAMS` — is in the version but not the scope); `version` (a hash of the section's change markers: source_health's last-success day and data-through, the newest row of the tables it reads, the restaurants row's fields — the day only where the text depends on it); `built_at`; `text` (what the model reads: owner-safe, M/D/YY dates); `facts_json` (the response_validation facts behind its figures); `data_json` (the structured values a caller reads beside the text); `tokens` (an estimate); `missing` (1: the section says what is NOT on file). Invariant: **a row is served only while its `version` equals the version computed now** — a row is replaced, never appended, so the table is bounded by restaurants × sections × scopes, and a scope never carries a date: the DSR narrative's nightly memory read replaces its one row (context re-audit 10/7/26 #10); no row holds another restaurant's data. Production readers today (#10): the four page reads (`profile`, `labor_trend`), Ask (`memory`, `findings`) and the DSR narrative (`memory`); the other sections are registered and tested but no caller renders them yet. Pruned at 30 days of no rebuild (*Retention*). The module reads (`insight_cache` above) add the fingerprint of the sections they render — a hash of their versions — to their stored key.

### Cavnar AI's own history (memory audit 9/29/26)
`insight_cache` and the two diagnosis tables are the "current" pointers the screens read and are overwritten; the history is kept beside them (`ai_reads.py`, boot DDL):
- `ai_reads` — append-only: one row per read that was WRITTEN (a module read, a diagnosis, the nightly report's narrative, a brief, a digest, the monthly review): `surface`, `kind`, `subject`, `fingerprint`, `text_hash`, `shown_text`, `raw_text`, `verdict` (the validation verdict), `facts_hash` (of the facts it was written from), `cited_ids`, `rec_keys`, `meta`, `call_id`, `created_at`, `superseded_at` / `superseded_by`. A read re-served unchanged is not a new row; a new read supersedes the previous one of its surface and subject.
- `ai_claims` — the checkable claims inside those reads (a diagnosis's cause and the outcome it expects, a nightly-report action): `read_id` / `last_read_id`, `surface`, `subject`, `signature`, `claim_type`, `text` / `last_text`, `action`, `expected`, `rec_key`, `metric`, `direction`, the baseline window and value, `after_start`, `horizon_date`, `model_band` beside `capped_band`, `restated_n` / `restated_at`, `verdict` / `verdict_value` / `verdict_basis` / `verdict_source` / `verdict_at`, `action_state`, `tracker_id`, `superseded_at`. Invariant: **one open claim per (surface, subject)** — a diagnosis restated daily is one claim until its horizon (`restated_n` counts it), forecast_log's insert-once rule. The nightly `learning_memory` pass scores each at its horizon: the days after the read against as many before, on this restaurant's own noise band, or the linked tracker's verdict; a claim whose advice was never taken is `untested`, never wrong.
- Both kept 400 days (registry, floor 365); each closed quarter is first summarised into `ai_read_summaries` (`ai_reads.rollup_quarters`) — PK (restaurant, `quarter`, `surface`, `subject`): `reads_n`, first and last, `said`, `done`, `happened`, `claims_n`, `held_n`, `not_held_n`, `untested_n`. Kept forever.

The per-restaurant learning record, refreshed nightly by the `learning_memory` pass and **kept forever** (each small and bounded):
- `rec_learning_summaries` (`rec_learning.py`) — the month's what-worked record, PK (restaurant, `month`, `scope`, `name` — by kind and by subject tag): `module`, `shown`, `settled`, `taken`, `dismissed`, `ignored`, `measured`, `improved`, `worsened`, `no_clear_change`. The month in progress is rewritten; a closed month stands.
- `restaurant_thresholds` (`restaurant_thresholds.py`) — the margins that trigger advice fitted to this restaurant's own noise, PK (restaurant, `name` — `labor_over_period`, `labor_over_day`): `value` is the LARGER of the stated margin (`stated`, `thresholds.LABOR_OVER_TARGET_PTS`) and k·σ of its own final-day history — its spread can only widen the floor, never narrow it — with `sigma`, `k`, `n`, `method`, `basis`, `as_of`, `version`. Nothing stored, or a fit older than `MAX_AGE_DAYS` (45), reads the stated margin.
- `learning_scorecards` (`learning_scorecard.py`) — is Cavnar AI getting better here: PK (restaurant, `month`), `metrics_json` (reply edit rate, acceptance, measured success, forecast error, draft acceptance, Ask helpful rate, fatigue), `flags_json` (worsening / flat), `fatigued`, `version`, and (re-audit 9/29/26, boot ALTER) `fatigue_now` / `fatigue_now_json` — the throttle's own reading, written nightly on the month in progress (`learning_scorecard.rolling_fatigue`: the episodes SETTLED in the last 30 days, or the newest 20 in 60 days; unseen, distrusted, deferred and reopened episodes in no denominator). Refreshed for the month in progress, frozen once it closes. A fatigued restaurant is shown fewer cards (`volume_limit`, reading `fatigue_now`, else the month's `fatigued`) — no longer released on the 1st.
- `bi_links` (`link_memory.py`) — every cross-module link a read finds, one row per restaurant and `link_key` (at most one write per link per day): `kind`, `subject`, `headline`, `modules`, `day`, `dish`, `detail`, `first_seen` / `last_seen`, `times_seen`, `week_streak`, `last_week`, `last_missed`, `resolved_at` / `resolved_by` (done / implemented / not_for_us / gone), `recurred_at`, `recurred_after` / `recurred_after_by`, `times_recurred`, `menu_item_id` (re-audit 9/29/26, boot ALTER in `init_link_memory`: the ONE `menu_items` row a `reviews_x_menu` link is about — `resolve_menu_item`: the driver's own dish, else the best seller the guests' words name; the do-not-promote list and the reprice guard match on it, so "the chicken" no longer blocks every chicken dish). `resolved_by` may also be `measured` / `window_closed` — a marketing x labor link whose campaign night has passed ends with what the night measured (`end`), and a new campaign for the night reopens it. A link declined `not_for_us` is never re-surfaced by a reader of it (`business_intelligence.unanswered_links` filters every reader of the brief's links) and its `week_streak` stops; a Done link found again `RECUR_AFTER_DAYS` later lifts the ledger's Done silence (`silence_rule` `link_recurred`); one found `ESCALATE_WEEKS` running, or come back, is recurring. Answers resolve on the restaurant's own day. Observed nightly by `learning_memory` (`observe_links`) as well as by every read.

## Daily Sales Report (dsr/)

- `dsr_reports` — one row per (restaurant, business date, **version**); `status` is the stage (scheduled → awaiting_close → collecting → writing → final | provisional | failed); `stages_json` holds when each stage was reached (the report's provenance, real UTC clock) plus progress notes (`dsr.store.NOTE_KEYS`): `blocks` {block: when it was collected — carried unchanged into a later version that reuses it}, `closed_by` (pos | close_time | manual | deadline), `narrative` {status: written | skipped, reason}, `failures` (pipeline errors), `crashes` {block: collector errors}, `supersedes` (the version this one completes), `rerun` {at, refused, why} (a forced re-run refused because the POS didn't return the night's sales — no version was made), `narrative_batch` {custom_id, status: pending → collecting → landed | failed → fallback (or not_sent), submitted_at, cutoff_at, claimed_at, landed_at, fell_back_at, reason} — the narrative sent through Message Batches (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #20): the version waits in `writing` until the answer lands or the cutoff passes, and every move of it is one compare-and-set UPDATE (`store.swap_narrative_batch`, `store.land_narrative_batch`), so the batch's answer and the sweep's synchronous fallback can never both be stored; `facts_json` the snapshot every view renders from; `narrative_json` the AI read (NULL when skipped); `trigger` what started the version (sweep | manual | late_data); `attempts` counts block re-collections (the 10/20/40-minute backoff reads it; the close-day poll and late-data checks don't count); `next_attempt_at` (UTC) is when the sweep looks again — NULL once nothing is left to do. `provisional` = sales were still missing at the deadline (the only required block; any other block still awaiting leaves the night final and is named in `facts.missing`). A night completed after a provisional finish is a new version, never an edit. Each version is claimed in `job_period_claims` as `dsr:<restaurant_id>:<date>:v<version>` (kept once the version is terminal); the sweep's cursor is `job_cursors.dsr_sweep_cursor`.
- `dsr_metrics` — PK (restaurant_id, business_date, metric); `metric` is `<block>.<key>`; only measured values are stored (NULL = not measured, never 0). The searchable history. Only a READY block writes rows (`status` is therefore the block's, `ready` — the report's own status is on `dsr_reports`), and it replaces its block's keys whole: a key the new version dropped (`sales.cat:Unmapped` after the department was mapped) is deleted. When a version finishes (final | provisional) `store.sync_metrics` makes the night's rows exactly that version's READY blocks; a failed re-run restores the finished version's. Since 9/28/26 the Sales block also stores each department's net (`sales.dep:<department>`, and `detail.by_department`): categories are derived from them under the category map as it stands when a night is shown (`block_sales.live_categories`, `access._live_categories`, `rollup._day_categories`), so a department mapped after a report counts toward its category. Rows with `report_id` NULL and `source` `<provider>_backfill` are the nights before the report started, read from the POS by `dsr.backfill` — its own nightly job `dsr_backfill` since 10/15/26 (figures only — no report, email or model; department and item-category dollars, `sales.depcat:` too; a night the archive restates is read again, `dsr.backfill.restate`); `source` `archive_repair` is a night's department dollars re-derived from the ticket archive for a night that had none (`repair_department_metrics`, Simple EJ's 9/27/26); `source` `pos_wages_after_report` is labor a report withheld for want of a wage rate, costed at the POS's pay. A report for that night replaces them. Since 9/29/26: `sales.forecast_same_basis` is 1 on a night whose forecast (`sales.forecast_net`) rested on the report's own basis (`demand.forecast_net`) — only then is a forecast set beside the night's net — and `demand.demand_accuracy` scores only those nights (a night from before the key counts unless its POS is known to build a different figure; the rest are reported as `excluded_other_basis`). The Intel block's `intel.weather_high_f` / `weather_low_f` / `weather_precip_pct` are the National Weather Service FORECAST for the date, never a measurement (`dsr.narrative.FORECAST_FACT_KEYS`); what the weather did is `weather_daily`.
- `dsr_coverage_runs` — PK (restaurant_id, business_date), `first_at` / `last_at` (UTC): the business dates the live clock-in check (`strategy_jobs.run_coverage_check`) actually read the POS's clock-ins. The Labor block states "0 no-shows" only for a night on file here (`store.coverage_ran`); otherwise no-shows are unmeasured.
- `dsr_budgets` — PK (restaurant_id, business_date); gross / net budget per night.
- `dsr_category_map` — PK (restaurant_id, pos_name) → category; an unmapped POS department is shown as Unmapped, never guessed. `pos_name` is a department, or a POS item category inside one ("Other › Pool", `store.sub_name`), which wins over its department's mapping. `held_json` (10/5/26): the POS item categories the department had SOLD when the owner mapped it — the ticket archive's last 90 days with the latest night (`rpower.department_sold`; never the POS's catalog, which files Gratuity and Tax Exempt beside Darts under EJ's "Other"), set at mapping time or by the first night that finds it unknown (`store.note_held`, never widened); something new inside a mapped department is listed unmapped, named and marked `new_in`, until the owner places it (`block_sales.categorize`). NULL: not known yet. The nightly metrics carry `sales.depcat:<department › category>` beside `sales.dep:<department>` so a week or period sees the same.
- `dsr_history_import` — PK (restaurant_id, business_date): gross, net, categories_json, source_file — last year from the owner's old DSR workbooks; an empty cell is skipped, never stored as $0.
- `dsr_predictions` — UNIQUE (restaurant_id, for_date, key): what a night's report predicted about the NEXT night (`dsr.predictions`, "How did yesterday turn out?", 9/25/26). `key` sales_range | sales_budget | rain | event; `metric` the fact it is graded on (sales.net); `op` between | gt | lt with `value` or `low`/`high`; `text` the claim as the owner reads it; `basis` what it rests on; `made_on` the report's night; `created_at` when the row was really written (UTC, the pipeline's clock). Written ONCE, strictly before its night's service opens (`pipeline.prediction_cutoff_utc`; INSERT OR IGNORE — a re-run never rewrites a prediction after the fact); `outcome` correct | incorrect | void (written at or after the night opened — never graded, listed or counted) | NULL (ungraded — a night with no sales is never "wrong"), `actual`, `graded_at` set from that night's Sales block, re-graded by each version. `prob` (10/19/26) how likely the report said the claim was, 0-1, from the forecast's log spread (NULL on older rows and below 5 nights of spread; a call under 65% is not made). `accuracy()` reads the last 90 days' correct/incorrect rows (optionally without the kinds a view may not see), the range and the calls counted apart, and the stated `prob` set against the share that came true.
- `dsr_deliveries` — UNIQUE (restaurant_id, business_date, user_id, channel, kind): who was told about a night (`dsr.deliver`). `channel` email | push | history (user_id 0: the restaurant's one `alert_log` row for the night and kind, its id in `detail`); `kind` first | updated; `status` sending | sent | held | failed | skipped | expired; `hold_until` (UTC) when a held push may go; `report_id` / `version` / `provisional` the version it was rendered from (a released held push records the version it actually carried); `view` owner | manager; `detail` the error or the reason it was skipped. The row is written BEFORE the send — the UNIQUE constraint is the claim, so a retry, a second process or a re-run never sends twice; a row left `sending` by a killed process is never retried (one missed notice, never two).
- `restaurants`: `fiscal_week_start_dow`, `fiscal_year_start`, `fiscal_period_scheme` (4x13 | 445 | 454 | 544 | the exact period lengths, e.g. "4,4,5,4,4,5,4,4,5,4,4,5"), `fiscal_years_json` (NULL, or a JSON list of the years that run differently — `[{"start": "2026-12-30", "lengths": [4,4,5,4,4,5,4,4,5,4,5,5]}]`, a 53-week year; sorted, non-overlapping, each start on the week's first day, validated by `dsr.fiscal.parse_years`. A day inside a listed year is placed by that year's lengths; any other day walks whole default-scheme years from the latest anchor at or before it — a listed year's end or `fiscal_year_start` — so the year after a 53-week year starts a week later instead of drifting. A value that no longer parses is ignored, never guessed around), `dsr_enabled`, `dsr_deadline_hour`, `dsr_gross_basis` (`items` | `all` — what the DSR calls gross: items only, or everything on the POS incl. tax and voids, Erik's; net is the same under both), `dsr_notify` (0 by default: the report builds and shows in the app; email and push start only when the owner turns this on — and the old closing-summary push stays until then), `dsr_budget_mode` (`day` | `week`, NULL reads as day — how the owner budgets on the Budget tab, 10/9/26; a weekly figure is split into each night's `dsr_budgets` row by the restaurant's weekday mix, `dsr.budget_plan.save_week`).

## Operations, audit and billing ledgers (admin-console fix round, 9/29/26)

All created at boot (`init_db` and the `init_*` it calls, or the boot block for the web process's own telemetry). Retention for each is in *Retention* below.

### The admin audit trail and support records (`admin_events.py`)
- `admin_events` — the one audit trail. Base columns `source` (`admin`, `request`, `stripe`, `docusign`, `audit`…), `event_type`, `restaurant_id`, `customer_id`, `email`, `amount`, `summary`, `payload`; since 9/29/26 `actor`, `actor_id`, `target`, `before_json`, `after_json`, `result` (ok / failed / refused / error / partial), `ip`, `request_id` and `external_id` (a provider's own event id). Written by `admin_events.record_admin_action` — the one call every admin action that records itself makes (a typed action with before/after, secrets redacted by key name — `_SENSITIVE_PARTS` / `_SENSITIVE_KEYS` — never a credential value; a value under a key naming a phone keeps only its last four digits, `_mask_phone`) and by the request hook `audit_admin_write`, registered on every blueprint that carries `/admin` writes (admin, status, the POS blueprints, the sales-audit tool): after the response, and only for a console login (admin or support) that passed CSRF, with the redacted body and the response status. Stripe events are one row per event id however often Stripe redelivers (the partial UNIQUE index `idx_admin_events_external` on `(source, external_id) WHERE external_id IS NOT NULL`, created by `billing_jobs.init_billing`). The sales-audit autosave is coalesced to one row per actor and audit per 10 minutes. Indexes: `idx_admin_events_rid` `(restaurant_id, id)` and `idx_admin_events_created_at` `(created_at)` — one definition each, the same statements `init_admin_events` and `ops._ensure_retention_indexes` both issue, so whichever boot step runs first makes them (the briefly-created duplicates `idx_admin_events_rest` / `idx_admin_events_created` are dropped at boot) — plus `idx_admin_events_source` `(source, id)`, `idx_admin_events_actor` `(actor, id)` and `idx_admin_events_request` `(request_id)`. Kept when the restaurant is deleted.
- `admin_audit_refused` — attempts on `/admin` writes the trail does not hold: anonymous, not an internal login, or failed CSRF (`reason`), with method, path, endpoint, ip, user and status. Capped at `REFUSED_CAP_ROWS` (5,000) and `REFUSED_KEEP_DAYS` (30), and at 20 rows per IP per 10 minutes (`dropped` counts the rest) — pruned by its own writer, so nobody outside can flood the trail an admin reads.
- `support_notes` — authored, dated, append-only notes on a client (`restaurant_id`, `author`, `author_id`, `body`); there is no edit or delete path. `restaurants.internal_notes` is returned beside them read-only (`legacy_internal_notes`).
- `bug_reports` — the in-app bug report, stored BEFORE its email goes: `restaurant_id`, `user_id`, `username`, `email`, `message`, `meta_json`, `source` (ios), `status` open / closed, `notified` + `notify_error` (whether the email to Will went), `closed_at` / `closed_by` / `close_note`.
- `offboarding_steps` (`offboarding.py`) — the account-closing checklist: one row per `(restaurant_id, cycle, step)` UNIQUE, `step` stripe / docusign / integrations / export, `status` done / skipped / pending (a skip needs a note), `actor`, `note`, `detail_json`. `cycle` is the `deletion_requested_at` the checklist was worked under ('' when an admin offboards with no request), so a withdrawn request's ticks never carry into a later one. Kept when the restaurant is deleted.
- `admin_issue_resolutions.occurrence_at` — the occurrence a Resolve covered (an issue's start, its newest evidence, or when it escalated): a newer occurrence reopens the issue. A row with no occurrence (written before 9/29/26) covers only occurrences that began before it was resolved. Condition-level issues stay resolved while the condition lasts and lapse after 30 days (`ADMIN_RESOLUTION_MAX_DAYS`). `admin_issue_resolution_history` keeps every resolve, reopen and automatic clear (`key`, `action`, `occurrence_at`, `note`, `actor`).

### The scheduler and the operator (`ops.py`)
- `job_runs` gains `restaurant_id`, `owner` (the process that ran it), `pulse_at` (stamped while it runs; a run whose pulse stopped is an orphan a deploy killed) and `request_id` (the console request a manual run came from). `ok` is 1 ok, 0 failed, 2 partial (`ops.run_outcome`: a result with `failed > 0` and no `attempted` is partial). Orphaned runs are closed at boot and on lease takeover (`ops.close_orphaned_runs`).
- `job_failures` gains `restaurant_id` (exact — `ops.capture` reads `restaurant_id=N` or `rid=N` from the context as a whole number, so rid 5 never matches 50) and `kind` (`job` / `request` / `ai_quality` / `audit`, default `job`); both indexed. AI output findings no longer land here — they are `ai_quality_events`.
- `scheduler_lease.kept` — the holder runs a lease keeper that renews every `LEASE_RENEW_SECONDS` (60); a KEPT lease silent for `LEASE_OWNER_GONE_SECONDS` (240) is taken over, an unkept one keeps the 30-minute `SCHEDULER_LEASE_STALE_SECONDS` window.
- `scheduler_heartbeat` — one row (`id = 1`), written only by the loop: `beat_at` (the pulse may stamp it while a job is inside its own bound), `loop_completed_at` (only at the end of a tick), `running_job` / `running_since`. `status_manager.scheduler_heartbeat_age_minutes()` and `scheduler_state()` read it; nothing else writes it. It replaced `service_status.updated_at`, which the liveness check and the console's status "Change" both reset.
- `job_run_requests` — "Run now" from the console: `job`, `requested_by`, `status` pending → running → done / failed, or `expired` (`taken_at`, `taken_by`, `finished_at`, `ok`, `error`). The loop takes a request under the lease; one not taken within `RUN_REQUEST_TTL_MINUTES` (30) is never run late.
- `job_expected_since` — when this database first expected each registry job (written at boot, never updated), so a job a release adds is not read as weeks overdue on the first `/health` after the deploy.
- `ops_markers` — once-ever markers (`ops.claim_marker(job, value)`: "told this owner about this competitor at this level", "first saw this source connected"), never pruned. They lived in `job_period_claims`, which is pruned at 45 days, so each came back every 45 days; a boot migration copied the old ones across.
- `operator_alerts` — every operator page: `key`, `subject`, `sent`, `channels` (JSON: sms / email / push), `error`. The page's cooldown is a `job_period_claims` row `cooldown:<key>`, claimed only after a text or the email went (held in process memory when the database cannot be written).
- `missed_windows` — a window that closed with nothing sent (`job`, `restaurant_id`, `local_date`, `detail`; UNIQUE per job, restaurant and day), from `morning_brief.run_due` and `scheduler.local_due`.
- `backup_runs` — one row per nightly backup, failed or not: `local_ok`, `integrity_ok`, `size_bytes`, `local_path`, `offsite_ok`, `offsite_target` (`s3://bucket/key`, `email`), `offsite_error`, `sha256` (of the encrypted off-site file), `db_bytes`, `wal_bytes`, `backups_bytes`, `free_bytes`, `detail_json` (including any unclassified credential column the scrub caught). `ops.backup_status()` and `storage_trend()` read it.
- `async_jobs` — see *Admin / Ops*; admin work runs on the two bounded admin pools (`ops.run_admin_task` for fetch-now and manual POS syncs, `admin_routes._submit_admin_job` for every other admin job) and is tracked here. `deadline_at` (TEXT, UTC, 10/3/26): the job's own wall-clock limit, set by a schedule generation once its calls are planned (`ops.set_async_job_deadline`, schedule audit P-22); a poll past it (plus `ops.ASYNC_DEADLINE_GRACE_SECONDS`) writes the job failed, `finish_async_job` writes only a pending row, and a job past it is never joined. NULL: judged by `ops.JOB_MAX_MINUTES` as before. Added at boot by `ops.init_ops`. Since the 10/4/26 re-audit: a job with a deadline is judged by it alone (PIPE-9 — it used to be failed 45 minutes after its press, inside its own deadline, after queueing for a slot); `started_at` (TEXT, UTC) is when it began running (the generation pool's slot taken, `GenerationClock`), and a job with no deadline is aged from it (from `created_at` while still queued); `request_json` (TEXT) is what the press asked — a schedule's `{week_start, dates, history_id, instruction}` (`schedule_engine.generation_request`) — and `ops.claim_async_job(request=…)` joins only a job asked exactly that, raising `ops.JobBusy` for any other (UI-8). Both added at boot by `ops.init_ops`. `stop_requested_at` (TEXT, UTC) and `saved_ref` (INTEGER), 10/10/26 (schedule audit ARCH-2, BUGSLIFE-26): `saved_ref` is the first draft the job saved, written in the same transaction as the draft (`models.save_schedule_history` `job_id`, which also refuses to write for a job no longer pending or stopped); `ops.stop_async_job` finishes a job with no `saved_ref` as stopped at once, and on one that saved sets `stop_requested_at` for the job to answer (`ops.job_stop_state`). Added at boot by `ops.init_ops`. `progress_json` (TEXT, AI cost audit 10/7/26 #36): how far a pending job has got — a schedule generation's `{days_total, days_drafted, dates_drafted}`, written by `ops.set_async_job_progress` from the generation's clock once per newly finished day (counted from the answer as it streams, `schedule_output.DayWatch`, and from the days the engine keeps); only while the job is pending; returned as `progress` by a pending poll (`/api/schedule-status`, `/mobile/api/labor/schedule-status`). Added at boot by `ops.init_ops`.

### The console's own data (`admin_ops.py`)
- `business_metrics_daily` — one row per day (`date` PK): list / billed / committed MRR and its source, subscriptions, paying / trial / past-due / paused / canceled / internal accounts, signups, activations, churns, active accounts and users, AI cost and cost by vendor, the month's vendor costs, and `state_json` (the flows). Written by `admin_ops.snapshot_business_metrics` (idempotent, set-based; refuses to write when the build had query errors). **Never pruned** — MRR and account history exist only from the first snapshot on.
- `account_risk_state` — each paying account's churn-risk `level` and `since` (with `points` and `reasons_json`), written by the snapshot, so "at risk for 7+ days" is a stored fact.
- `value_figures_daily` — the four value figures per restaurant per day (`delivered_monthly`, `avoided_to_date`, `surfaced_30d`, `opportunity_monthly` — stored side by side, never summed), written by `intelligence.dashboard.snapshot_value_figures` (a bounded, resumable sweep) so the admin Intelligence page sums stored rows.
- `ai_vendor_invoices` — the month's billed total per metered vendor (`month` YYYY-MM UTC, `vendor` anthropic / google_places / perplexity, `amount_usd`, `note`, `entered_by`, `entered_at`; PK month × vendor), entered in Engineering → AI costs (step-up, audited `ai_invoice.recorded`) and read against the ledger's own figure for the month — a ratio away from 1.00 is price drift (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #96). Created at boot by `admin_ops.init_admin_ops`; never pruned (a dozen rows a year). Not `vendor_costs`: those feed gross margin beside the metered usage, and an AI invoice there would count the same spend twice.
- `vendor_costs` — what each vendor cost in a month, entered by hand (`vendor`, `month`, `amount_usd`, `kind`, `source`, `note`, `updated_by`; UNIQUE per vendor and month; audited). Gross margin and ARPA read it.

### The web process's own telemetry (`platform_monitor.py`, `provider_health.py`)
- `boot_events` — one row per boot: start, ready and stop times, pid, host, deployment and replica ids, build, Python and SQLite versions, volume, database size, `boot_ms`, `ok` / `error`, and `previous_exit` (clean, unclean or boot failed — how the process before it ended). More than 3 boots of one deployment, or more than 3 that followed a crash, in an hour pages once.
- `tenant_request_rollups` — per hour and restaurant: customer requests, 5xx, total and max ms — each hour's busiest `http_layer.TENANT_ROLLUP_TOP` (50) restaurants by requests and by time, a sample (scale audit 10/19/26 OPS-9: the route rollups carry no restaurant). Persisted by the supervisor from `http_layer`'s per-hour buffer; `platform_monitor.tenant_load` reads it.
- `request_rollups` — per minute, per route rule, method and traffic class (`klass`, `http_layer.traffic_class`: admin, mobile, probe, static, webhook, web_api, web — customer latency leaves out admin, probe and static): requests, 4xx, 5xx, total and max ms, latency buckets, peak in-flight. Persisted by the supervisor from `http_layer`'s buffers.
- `http_5xx_log` — a sample of every 5xx, handled or not: route rule (never the raw path), status, redacted error, exception type, `unhandled`, `request_id`, `restaurant_id` when the URL has one, class, duration.
- `provider_health` — one row per credential probe (`provider`, `state` ok / failing / error — unreachable: a timeout or a 5xx / unconfigured, `detail`, `http_status`, `latency_ms`, `checked_at`); a provider in `PROVIDER_PROBES_SKIP` writes no row.

### Messaging ledgers (`models.py`, `push.py`, `webhooks.py`)
- **The guest list, host stand to next-day text** (`guest_hub.init_guest_hub`, called from `guest_marketing.init_guest_marketing` at boot; 10/20/26). `guest_contacts` is the list (every source adds a guest UNCONSENTED; boot ALTERs `tock_guest_id`, `last_party_size`, `tock_marketing_optin` — Tock's own flag, shown, never consent — and `first_seen_at`). `tock_events` — UNIQUE (restaurant_id, hook, event_key): every Tock webhook delivery raw (`hook` reservations | walkins | guests; `event_key` Tock's event id, else a hash of the event), `processed_at`/`error` per event, re-applied by `reprocess_tock`. `tock_bookings` — PK (restaurant_id, kind, tock_id): a reservation or walk-in as Tock last said it (`status` booked | seated | completed | cancelled | no_show, `party_size`, `booked_for` local, `business_date`, the guest's name/phone/email, `contact_id`, `visit_counted` — a seated or completed booking is one visit, once); a night's booked covers are the `demand_signals` "Reservations" row (source tock). `guest_followup_settings` — PK restaurant_id: the next-day thank-you (`enabled`, `message`, `review_link`). `guest_followups` — PK (restaurant_id, contact_id, visit_date): each thank-you claimed before it is sent (`status` sending | sent | failed). `guest_inbox` — guests' texts to the restaurant's own number and the owner's answers (`direction` in | out, `kind` reply | followup, `read_at`). `guest_sms_senders` — PK restaurant_id: the restaurant's own Twilio messaging service and number (`service_sid`, `phone_number`); notify sends every guest text through it when set, and an inbound text to it finds the restaurant. `guest_import_rows` — PK (restaurant_id, row_key): a spreadsheet row's visit counted once. `guest_pos_visits` — PK (restaurant_id, ticket_id): an RPOWER check carrying a real customer (`pos_tickets.customer_id`, not the placeholder) matched to a guest — one visit, and the items behind "your usual".
- `sms_log` — every SMS attempt, whatever happened to it: `restaurant_id`, `use_case` (alert, otp, staff, guest…), `to_hash` and `to_last4` (never the number), `status` (Twilio's own answer at send — `accepted`, `queued`… — moved forward to `delivered` / `undelivered` by the status callback; `failed`, `blocked` (consent or the platform STOP — never reached Twilio), `not_configured`), `error_code`, `error`, `provider_sid`, `cost_usd` (never filled — Twilio's status callbacks carry no price). `POST /webhooks/twilio/status` moves a row forward only.
- `push_outbox` — one row per device per push, written before it is handed to the pool: `state` queued → delivering → sent | failed (`expired` when a restart left it too long), `attempts`, `last_error`, `done_at`. `push_deliveries` stays the ledger of attempts made; this is the queue. `push.reap_push_outbox` re-drives what a restart left.
- `live_activity_tokens` — the phone's Live Activity push tokens (iOS parity audit 10/7/26 #38, #61, #94): `kind` `start` (a push-to-start token, one per device per `activity_type` — `pending_send`, `schedule_build`, `service`) or `update` (one running activity's token, keyed by `activity_key`: the delayed action, the generation's job id, the service's business date), `apns_token` UNIQUE, `environment`, and `session_hash` — the `sessions.token` of the sign-in that registered it: `push.live_activity_tokens` sends only while that session is live and its login active, so signing out ends every Live Activity push to the phone. Registered by `POST /mobile/api/live-activity-tokens`; a token Apple refuses as dead is deleted.
- `live_activity_runs` — one row per thing a Live Activity is about (UNIQUE restaurant × type × key): `started_at` (the push-to-start went out — claimed once), `ended_at` (claimed once), `state_json` (the last content sent; an update goes only when it changes) and, for a service, `coverage_json` — the coverage check's latest names and roles of who hasn't clocked in, which the "Tonight" route and the pushes read instead of calling the POS.
- `widget_waiting_counts` — the waiting count each login last read (PK restaurant × user); a web read that finds it changed sends that login's phones a silent push (`push.fire_silent`, "waiting") so the widget redraws. One row per login per location; not pruned.
- `morning_brief_deliveries` — one row per person per brief day (UNIQUE): `channel` push / email, `status` queued / sent / failed, `error` — a push that failed or could not be queued falls back to email, and anyone already reached today is skipped. A brief used to count as sent the moment a push was queued.
- `alert_storm_caps` — at `ALERT_STORM_PER_HOUR` (10) alerts in an hour a restaurant's alerts are capped until its next local midnight (`until_at`, UTC; `alerts_in_window`, `threshold`, `suppressed`), one per restaurant per local day; health alerts still go through; an admin can lift it early (`lifted_at`, `lifted_by`).
- `data_migrations` — one-time data migrations by `name` (the first: `email_log_sent_at_utc`).

### AI operations (`ai_utils.py`, `init_ai_ops`)

- `ai_usage` gains (boot ALTERs in `init_ai_ops`) `vendor` (anthropic / google_places / perplexity; older rows backfilled from the model string), `outcome` (ok / error / refused / truncated / unparseable / blocked — `status` stays the coarse legacy reading: ok, blocked or error), `stop_reason`, `attempts`, `request_id`, `trigger` (owner / scheduler / admin / system), `actor_user_id`, `correlation_id`, `price_version`, `call_id`, `effort` (the `output_config.effort` the call went out at, after the defaults; NULL is the model's own default — AI cost audit 10/7/26 #74, written by `create_with_retry` and the batch collector) and `reason` (why a call was blocked: budget, breaker, data_not_ready, no_key, deadline, busy — no interactive slot free on a request thread, 10/7/26 — the Places budget or breaker; and `stream_cut` on an `error` row — one attempt whose stream failed partway, a mid-stream overload or 5xx or a dropped connection, filed with the tokens the SDK's accumulated message carries before `create_with_retry` sends it again or gives up — AI cost audit 10/7/26 #72; it shares the call's `call_id`, `mark_outcome` never re-files it, and its output tokens are the stream's `message_start` figure, so it undercounted the output billed — since the platform re-audit, 10/7/26 #10, when the accumulated snapshot's text, thinking and tool input come to more (characters / 4) that estimate is filed instead, under reason `stream_cut_est`, which `mark_outcome` never re-files either). A call stopped before it reached the provider is a zero-cost `blocked` row; repeats are merged into one row per restaurant, action and reason per minute, `attempts` counting them (`ai_utils.log_blocked`). `refused` (a model refusal) and `truncated` (`max_tokens`) stay billed. A reply the caller could not use is re-filed `unparseable` (`ai_utils.mark_outcome`). A one-time reprice at boot corrected pre-9/22 Sonnet 5 rows and Perplexity rows (`price_version` `repriced-2026-09-22`).
- `ai_calls` — one row per provider call (`call_id` PK): action, vendor, model, restaurant, trigger, actor, correlation id, caller, `template_hash` (the calling function's source plus the static system block), `prompt_hash`, request id, stop reason, outcome, `output_hash`, tokens, latency, attempts, `usage_id`, `meta_json`, and the prompt and output text REDACTED (emails, phones, card-like numbers, labelled guest names, name fields, the drafter's fenced reviewer name) and compressed (`prompt_z`, `output_z`). Text is kept for the newest 10 calls per restaurant and action, for `AI_TRACE_DAYS` (30), and within `AI_TRACE_TEXT_MAX_MB` (512) in all — past it the nightly prune drops the oldest text first (scale audit 10/19/26 A4-14, `ai_utils._trim_trace_text`); rows for `AI_CALLS_RETAIN_DAYS` (120). Only full admins read the text (`/admin/api/ai/calls/<call_id>`). `ai_usage`, `ai_validation_log`, `insight_cache` and `ai_quality_events` carry `call_id`.
- `ai_quality_events` — findings about a model's OUTPUT (a validation refusal, a dropped line, a fallback, a safety disagreement): `surface`, `kind`, `action`, `detail`, `codes`, `n`, `call_id`. They used to be `job_failures` rows and Sentry events; the console reports them as a rate per 100 calls of the surface's own action.
- `ai_memory_sizes` — how much memory each prompt surface carries (memory re-audit 9/29/26, PROMPTS-2/-10/-16): one row per UTC `day`, `surface` and `section` (PK), with `calls`, `chars`, `max_chars`, `dropped` (lines cut for the budget or hidden from the viewer), `cut_calls` (calls whose budget had to leave lines of the section out) and `errors` (provider failures). `memory_context` aggregates in-process and adds its counts every `FLUSH_SECONDS` (300); the admin AI page reads it ("Memory in prompts"). No memory text. Pruned at `AI_MEMORY_SIZES_RETAIN_DAYS` (400) by `ai_utils.prune_ai_ops`.
- `ai_health_events` — breaker opens and closes, admin resets, budget warnings and stops, and auth / permission / credit / model-not-found / overload failures (`vendor`, `event`, `scope`, `restaurant_id`, `detail`, `paged`).
- `ai_usage_daily` — one row per UTC day × restaurant (`rid_key`, 0 for none) × vendor × action × model: calls, each outcome, attempts, tokens, cost, admin-triggered cost, latency count/sum and p50/p95/max, and `efforts_json` ({effort: calls}, "" for the model's default; #74 — a column, not part of the key: an action runs one effort on a model almost always, and a key change would rebuild a never-pruned table at boot). `ai_validation_daily` — the same for the validation log (per surface and mode, verdict counts, rules, and `n_texts`, the distinct texts checked — W4). Rebuilt idempotently by `ai_utils.rollup_usage` (a day is recomputed whole, its rows read by a `created_at` range the index serves) — nightly as the first step of `ops.prune_ledgers` (no longer at boot, AI cost audit 10/7/26 #91), before any raw AI row is deleted (a rollup that failed keeps that night's raw `ai_usage` and `ai_validation_log` rows); **never pruned**.
- `places_details_cache` — one Google Places Details answer per `place_id`, `day` (the requesting restaurant's calendar date), `fields` (sorted, comma-joined) and `variant` (JSON of the other answer-shaping parameters, e.g. `reviews_sort`), with `payload_json` (the response body) and `created_at` (AI cost audit 10/7/26 #13). `ai_utils.places_request` answers a Details request from it when the same place was fetched that day with a superset of the fields — no request, no ledger row, no cost; the review fetch (`PLACES_UNCACHED_ACTIONS`) never reads it. Public Places data for one place only, shared by every restaurant tracking that place. Created at boot by `init_ai_ops`.
- `ai_rate_events` — the AI rate limiter's window (`key`, `at`, `expires_at`), one short IMMEDIATE transaction per check, expired rows removed as it goes; `ai_utils._ai_call_log` is only the fallback when the table cannot be reached. `ai_rate_hits` counts refusals per day and bucket (the key's prefix, never the key — some keys hold an IP).

## Retention — the one registry

`ops._RETENTION_DAYS` / `ops._RETENTION_COLUMN` is the one registry (`models._LOG_RETENTION_DAYS` is a view of it), and since the memory audit (9/29/26) each table declares three more things: its **floor** (`_RETENTION_FLOOR_DAYS`), the **rollup** run before its rows go (`_RETENTION_ROLLUP`) and its **readers' windows** (`_RETENTION_READERS`). `ops.prune_ledgers` runs nightly straight after the backup (so the pruned rows are in it): first every declared rollup, once each (the AI daily rollup `ai_utils.rollup_usage`, which also prunes the AI-operations tables, then `history_rollups.roll_alerts`, `roll_engagement`, `stamp_newsletter_results`) — a rollup that fails keeps its tables' rows that night; then each table in chunks of `RETENTION_CHUNK_ROWS` (5,000) with a commit each, at most `RETENTION_PASS_MAX_ROWS` (200,000) rows per table per pass — or `RETENTION_INFLOW_FACTOR` (2) times the rows that aged out in the last day when that is more (`ops._inflow_cap`, scale audit 10/19/26 DB-03: a fixed cap under a table's daily inflow meant it only grew; a wrong clock or mis-stamped column still loses at most a couple of days of rows a night) — the rest goes on the following nights, reported as `capped` and captured, not paged. On Postgres a partitioned table's months wholly past its window are first detached and dropped (`pg_partitions.drop_expired`, one a table a night, `PARTITION_DROPS_PER_PASS`); the result lists them as `partitions_dropped`. Claims of a period under a day (`ops.SUBDAILY_CLAIM_JOBS`: `live_labor`, `intraday` and the loop's 5–20 minute ticks) go after `RETAIN_SUBDAILY_CLAIMS_DAYS` (2, floor 1; DB-14 / INF-16) — the rest of `job_period_claims` keeps its 45 days, stopping taking on tables after `RETENTION_MAX_SECONDS` (10 minutes); then a bounded `PRAGMA optimize`. Every registered column is indexed (`ops._ensure_retention_indexes`). Each window is an env override (`RETAIN_<TABLE>_DAYS`); 0 disables one (kept forever).

**Floors.** A window set under its table's floor — a typo such as `RETAIN_ALERT_LOG_DAYS=18`, which would have erased every restaurant's alert history overnight — is REFUSED: nothing is deleted from that table, the run is partial (`refused` in its result), a `job_failures` row is captured and the operator is paged (`ops.page_operator("retention_floor")`, hourly cooldown); `ops.retention_refusals()` lists them. Each floor is at least the longest window any declared reader reads; where the default IS the floor the window may be raised, never lowered. The same rule holds for the boot prune of `login_history` and for the special prunes below. `tests/test_mem_m7_retention.py` fails when a registered table has no floor, or a declared reader's window (days, or its own constant) does not fit inside it, or a lifetime reader does not read a summary; the readers that reach past their window today, each with its reason, are in `ops._RETENTION_KNOWN_GAPS` (a list that may only shrink).
