| `staff_consents`, `compliance_pay_ledger`, `good_faith_estimates`, `fww_requests`, `fww_access_offers` | 1095 (`RETAIN_COMPLIANCE_RECORDS_DAYS`; schedule audit 10/10/26 RM-15: the scheduling records a predictive-scheduling law asks be kept three years — staff's written consents, the predictability-pay and rest-premium ledger, good-faith estimates, the written answers to change requests, access-to-hours offers) | `created_at` | 1095 | — |

**The three-year records hold (schedule audit 10/10/26 MARKET-4).** A restaurant a predictive-scheduling law covers (`compliance_packs.records_hold_days` ≥ `ops.RECORDS_HOLD_DAYS`, 1095 — Chicago, New York City fast food, Seattle, San Francisco, Oregon, Evanston, Berkeley and Emeryville keep three years; Philadelphia two) keeps the rows of `ops._RETENTION_HOLD_TABLES` — `attendance_events`, `shift_offers`, `staff_announcements`, `staff_announcement_recipients`, `staff_thread_messages`, `staff_running_late` — and its intermediate `schedule_versions` (the covers, swaps and sent edits) for three years, whatever their table's window: `prune_ledgers` reads the held restaurants once a pass (`ops._records_held`) and adds `NOT (restaurant_id IN (held) AND <column> >= now - 1095 days)` to those deletes. Every other restaurant keeps the windows above.

Also in `prune_ledgers`, each with a floor (`ops._special_retention`): `inventory_history` — thinned to one snapshot a week past 56 days (floor 28), keeping every snapshot taken on a real count too (`waste_trend._counted` read from its items: food cost % is anchored only on counted snapshots — food & bar audit 10/16/26, FC-CALC-02), and past 395 days (floor 395: `food_cost_intelligence.seasonal_baseline` reads 392) dropped only once its week has its `inventory_weekly_summary` row (the week's value, waste and its top items, 7-day COGS and food cost %) and the month's unit cost per ingredient is in `ingredient_cost_monthly` (`history_rollups.summarise_inventory`; `waste_trend.load_waste_history` reads the summary for the weeks that are gone, so food cost % and the seasonal re-check reach past 395 days); the detail of superseded, never-published, never-shared drafts of weeks that are over, past `RETAIN_DRAFT_DETAIL_DAYS` (30, floor 14) — `quality_json` and `review_json` thinned to their headline, `what_if_json` cleared, `schedule_history.detail_thinned_at` stamped; the CSV, economics and score columns stay — and of intermediate `schedule_versions` past `RETAIN_VERSION_DETAIL_DAYS` (30, floor 30 — 90 / 60 until the scheduler audit of 10/10/26, SCALE-4; never the generated, a published or a week's newest): a version written since keeps its inline headline and loses its `quality_ref`, an older one is thinned to its headline as before; the same pass compacts versions still holding their whole evaluation inline into headline + `quality_blobs` and removes the blobs nothing points at; intermediate `schedule_versions` of weeks older than 180 days (floor 168: the learners read 24 weeks — `schedule_versions.LEARN_DAYS`, schedule audit 10/3/26 L-27) — never a published version, never the generated draft, never a week's newest; and superseded, never-published, never-shared drafts older than 365 days (floor 90); received intake mail to no address of ours (`email_intake` `no_route` rows — mostly spam) past `RETAIN_EMAIL_INTAKE_UNROUTED_DAYS` (30, floor 7: "email_intake_unrouted"); and a received invoice file's text (`fintech_files.content` cleared, its object-store copy deleted) past `RETAIN_FINTECH_FILE_CONTENT_DAYS` (365, floor 90: "fintech_file_content" — the invoice lines hold the history; the file can no longer be re-read).

Outside the registry, each with its own prune: `login_attempts` 2 days (daily job); `view_as_sessions` 2 days (when a view-as opens); the staff app's own ledgers (10/2/26) — `staff_notices` 30 days, never a held or sending row (`staff_reminders._prune`, every `staff_reminders` run), `staff_otp_sends` 1 day (as each new send is counted) and `staff_pin_resets` 15 minutes past their start (as the next reset starts; a used one at once); `admin_audit_refused` (its own cap); `request_rollups` 14, `tenant_request_rollups` 14 (`RETAIN_REQUEST_ROLLUPS_DAYS`), `http_5xx_log` 30, `boot_events` 180, `provider_health` 30 days (`platform_monitor.prune_platform_telemetry`, hourly on the supervisor thread); `ai_calls` 120 days (text 30, and at most `AI_TRACE_TEXT_MAX_MB` of it), `ai_quality_events` 180, `ai_health_events` 365, `ai_rate_hits` 180, `ai_memory_sizes` 400, expired `ai_rate_events` (`ai_utils.prune_ai_ops`, called by `rollup_usage` — nightly from `prune_ledgers`). `login_history` is pruned twice on purpose — at boot (`auth.prune_login_history`) and by the registry — to ONE window, the registry's. Owner-controlled: reviews older than `restaurants.data_retention_months` are soft-deleted nightly (`models.purge_expired_reviews`), and in the same transaction what they were — per month, no guest text — is added to `review_monthly_stats`. A removed review — by that retention, by the owner, or because Google no longer lists it — is hidden at once, and past `RETAIN_REVIEW_ERASE_DAYS` (30, floor 7; `ops._special_retention` "reviews_erase") its guest text (`history_rollups.REVIEW_GUEST_TEXT`: author, text, summary, the drafts, entities, the specific complaint, edit signals) is blanked and `reviews.erased_at` stamped (`history_rollups.erase_removed_reviews`, in `prune_ledgers`); the row and its platform key stay so a re-fetch cannot re-add it, and a guest's later edit never rewrites a removed row (`models._apply_review_edit`). The off-site copy blanks every removed review's guest text at once (`offsite_backup.SCRUB_ROWS`) (memory re-audit 9/29/26, FORGET-1). **Never pruned:** `ops_markers`, `business_metrics_daily`, `ai_usage_daily`, `ai_validation_daily`, `ai_vendor_invoices`, `rec_instances`, `value_snapshots`, `ai_read_summaries`, `rec_learning_summaries`, `restaurant_thresholds`, `learning_scorecards`, `bi_links` (one row per restaurant and cross-module link subject, `link_memory`), `change_log`, the summaries in *What outlives a prune* below, `learning_tombstones`, the recommendation memory's small state tables (9/29/26) — `rec_distrust`, `rec_kind_states`, `automation_trust` (a row per data source, kind or automation) — the people memory's (9/29/26): `people`, `person_aliases`, `person_questions`, `person_merges`, `person_roles`, `schedule_standing_patterns`, the scheduling memory (`schedule_memory`, one row per learned fact, and `schedule_memory_state`, one per restaurant — schedule audit 10/3/26 L-29) and `person_quarters` (each person's quarter — shifts, hours, roles, dayparts, attendance outcomes, covers taken, guest mentions — written by `shift_facts.roll_all_quarters` while the quarter's raw rows are whole; `shift_facts.tenure` reads it for what the raw rows no longer hold); the owner memory's `preferences` (bounded by its keys, kept until changed); the event memory's `event_outcomes`, `event_effects`, `weather_daily`, `own_rating_history`, `market_events` and `competitor_rating_monthly`; the owner's corrections and voice record, `marketing_edits`, `invoice_aliases` and `review_retags`; and the platform's `intel_pattern_history` and `schedule_experiment_verdicts`, and the Schedule Learning Scorecard's frozen rows (`schedule_learning_scores`, one per published week — 10/10/26), the weekly staffing estimates (`staffing_elasticity`, `staff_productivity` — bounded, replaced each Monday), the pre-go-live labor standards (`labor_standard_references`, a row per family x meal) and the weights' pooled cohort evidence (`schedule_calibration_cohort`, one row a week) (the intelligence tables are kept as before — `intel_benchmark_facts` alone is pruned, at 8 weeks, by its own pass). The raw nightly nets — `labor_daily_history.sales` and `dsr_metrics` `sales.net` — are in no registry either.

## What `delete_restaurant` keeps

`models.delete_restaurant` (run only by an admin: the demo delete, or the offboarding checklist's `delete_restaurant_now`, both behind the typed name) removes every row in every table with a `restaurant_id` column, the `restaurants` row, and then any row left pointing at a row it removed — except the tables in `models._KEEP_ON_RESTAURANT_DELETE`: `admin_events`, `offboarding_steps` and `billing_status_history`. The account is gone; the record of what was done to it, and that it was deleted and by whom, is not.

And its **anonymised learning** (owner decision, Will, 9/29/26): for a restaurant that could teach (`models.learning_exclusion` — never a demo, a test or an internal account), its `intel_rec_events` rows from its `learning_since` on are kept, re-keyed to `restaurant_id = -learning_tombstones.id` with every recommendation key's subject hashed (`models._anonymous_key`: a key can name a dish or a person), so cohort priors do not forget what a churned restaurant measured. `learning_tombstones` holds only the restaurant's type and whether it was confirmed, when, and how many rows were kept — no name, no id, no raw rows. Its `intel_features` weeks are deleted with it (Will, 9/29/26): a band reads only the last three weeks, so they taught nothing once the restaurant lapsed. A tombstoned row points at no restaurant by design (the delete's orphan sweep skips it); it passes `models.learning_filter_sql` and counts toward a cohort, and nothing can show a name for it. Everything else of the restaurant's own is deleted. What teaches (memory re-audit 9/29/26, FORGET-16): the kept `intel_rec_events` keep their stamped `cohort` and partition labels, so they go on teaching the priors. `learning_tombstones.category` has no reader — an intentional record, kept so a future cohort-history reader can place the kept outcomes. The `members_json` of the stored bands is handled separately: a restaurant alone in its organisation is removed from every list at the delete (*Intelligence engine*).

## The change log and what outlives a prune (memory audit, 9/29/26)

- `change_log` (`change_log.py`, at boot from `init_db`) — one append-only row per change to a setting, target, never-say list, hours, profile, automation, notification or staffing rule, pay rate (written by `models.update_restaurant` inside its own transaction, for every field `change_log.RESTAURANT_FIELD_KINDS` tracks; credentials, billing, caches and screen state never — `RESTAURANT_UNTRACKED`, each with its reason, and a test fails on a field in neither), and — through `change_log.record(…, subject=)` — prices, menu items, the roster, suppliers and goals: `kind`, `entity`, `field`, `subject`, `old_value` / `new_value` (JSON), `source` (owner | manager | admin | seeded | sync | import | system | legacy), `actor_user_id`, `actor_role` (`view-as` for an admin acting as a login), `via` (the endpoint or job), `changed_at` (UTC). Who: the login the auth decorator resolved (`auth._bind_log_context` keeps it on `flask.g`) through `permissions.answer_authority`, or a `change_log.attributed(...)` block. **Kept forever** — no registry entry. `activity_log`'s `target_change` / `profile_changed` rows were carried over once at boot (`source='legacy'`, `data_migrations` `change_log_from_activity_log_v1`). Read by `rec_trust.owner_changes` (the "changed since your data" caution, which now says who) and `outcomes.find_concurrent` (a price, menu, roster, pay, hours or supplier change is another change on the number); `change_log.value_as_of` answers which value held on a date (a bare date is the restaurant's local day, bounded in UTC) — read by Ask's `read_target_history` (`targets_as_of`, `target_changes`: the targets in force on a date and every change, each target shown only to a login that may see its module).
- `restaurants.target_setters_json` — who set each target stamped `set` ({field: principal | delegate | admin}); `thresholds.target_label` says "your target" only for a principal's ("the target Cavnar AI set", "the target a manager set" otherwise; a target set before 9/29/26 keeps "your target").
- `alert_monthly` (restaurant × month × alert type: fired, dollars, opened), `engagement_monthly` (login × restaurant × month: sign-ins, sign-in days, notification opens by type; the console's owner activity reads it for the last console sign-in month once `login_history`'s 90 days hold none — `admin_ops._load_with`, memory re-audit 9/29/26), `review_monthly_stats` (the reviews the owner's retention purge soft-deleted, per month: count, rating sum, responded, drafted, posted, the edit mix), `inventory_weekly_summary` and `ingredient_cost_monthly` (above), all in `history_rollups.py` — written before their raw rows go, no guest text, **kept forever**. A month is summarised only while its raw rows are whole and never rewritten after the prune has started on it. `value_delivered.ledger`'s lifetime counts read them (`alerts_lifetime`, `purged_review_totals`).
- `guest_newsletters.opens_recorded`, `clicks_recorded`, `tracked_recorded`, `results_stamped_at` — a newsletter's results stamped on its row 30 days after it went (`history_rollups.stamp_newsletter_results`, NULL opens when tracking was off); `guest_email.newsletter_history` reads them once stamped.
- `value_snapshots` — each restaurant's NET measured monthly figure per day, dated on its **local** day, written once a day for every restaurant in service, internal ones included (`value_delivered.run_value_snapshots`, the `value_snapshots` job — its only writer since the parity round, 10/7/26: Home's GET no longer writes it); kept forever.
- `restaurants.learning_since` / `learning_override` — learning eligibility (`models.learning_exclusion`, the one predicate every learner reads): rows recorded before `learning_since` (stamped when a demo becomes a real account) teach nothing; `learning_override` ('include' | 'exclude') is the admin's word over the automatic rule (internal billing, only internal logins, a test-pattern name). Only a CHANGE to 'include' stamps `learning_since` (unless the admin says the history counts, `learning_history`); a save that re-sends the override already stored leaves it alone, so the history it has taught from is never dropped (`admin_routes`). The demo seed's `labor_daily_history` rows carry `source='seed'` (older ones stamped once at boot, `labor_seed_provenance_v1`); a converted demo's own daily-history readers drop them (`models.own_history_sql`).

## Onboarding at scale (`onboarding.py`, scale audit 10/19/26)

Created at boot by `onboarding.init_onboarding` (from `models.init_db`).
- `pos_backfill_slots` `(restaurant_id, provider)` — a store that holds (or held) one of the fleet's POS history backfill slots (ONB-1): `admitted_at`, `finished_at` (NULL while active; set when its 90 days are archived), `held_reason` / `held_at` (no room for tonight's days and the backup after them). At most `POS_BACKFILL_MAX_STORES` active rows; one row per store, no growth.
- `onboarding_confirmations` `(restaurant_id, step)` — the setup judgments made once per store (ONB-11/12: `week_start`, `roles_rates`, `salaried`, `station_logins`, `floors_closers`, `availability`, `dsr_calendar`): `status` pending | confirmed, `suggested_json` (what the data suggests — RPOWER's `ot_dow` reading for the payroll week), `value_json`, `source`, `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at`. A suggestion never overwrites a confirmation.
- `provision_batches` / `provision_rows` `(batch_id, line)` — each applied provisioning list (ONB-2) and each of its lines: `store_key`, `restaurant_id`, `status` created | exists | error, `error`. An audit record of who created which restaurants; a few rows a batch, no retention.
- `partners` (`name` unique, `billing_mode` direct | partner, `master_agreement_signed_at`, `master_agreement_ref`, `contact_email`) and `partner_stores` `(restaurant_id` primary key, `partner_id)` — the reseller entity (ONB-10); read for billing only with `PARTNER_BILLING=1`.

## What leaves the server in a backup — the scrub registry

The LOCAL nightly snapshot is never scrubbed: it is the restore artifact, on the same volume as the database (a gzipped `VACUUM INTO` copy since 10/7/26, the newest `BACKUP_RETAIN_COUNT` kept). Every OFF-SITE copy (object storage and the emailed copy) is a scrubbed, Fernet-encrypted copy, and the scrub is one registry in `offsite_backup.py`:
- `SCRUB_TABLES` are emptied: `sessions`, `two_fa_backup_codes`, `trusted_devices`, `device_tokens`, `live_activity_tokens`, `login_reports`, `staff_portal_tokens`, `view_as_sessions`, `user_backup_codes` (an internal login's own backup codes), `user_totp` (an internal login's authenticator-app secret, R10) and `async_jobs` (a job's payload and result can carry what an admin typed or a model returned).
- `SCRUB_COLUMNS` are nulled (a NOT NULL column is blanked): reset tokens and recovery codes on `users`, `restaurants.temp_password` and `two_fa_device_token`, the sales-audit share link (`sales_audit_shares.token_enc`), and the intake ledger's copy of an address (`email_intake.address`).
- Sealed, not scrubbed (scale audit 10/19/26 OPS-1), when `CREDENTIAL_KEY` works at backup time: every `credentials.FIELDS` column (read at call time) stays the `enc:v1:` ciphertext it is at rest — a plaintext one is encrypted in the copy — and `SEAL_COLUMNS` (`restaurants.stripe_customer_id`, `webhooks.secret`, `email_intake_routes.address` — an address is a write capability for a restaurant's invoices) and `SEAL_TABLES` (`app_secrets.value`, the link-signing secrets) become `sealed:v1:` ciphertext under `CREDENTIAL_KEY`, opened on restore by `offsite_backup.unseal` (`db_restore` on the `RESTORE_FROM` file; once at boot for a database copied in another way). Without a working key every one of them is nulled and `app_secrets` emptied, as before.
- `KEEP_COLUMNS` are credential-LOOKING columns kept on purpose, each with its reason (`users.password_hash` — a salted hash; dropping it locks every user out of a restored copy; token-expiry dates; hashes of one-time links; public link ids already printed in guest texts and posts).
- `SCRUB_ROWS` (memory re-audit 9/29/26): rows whose words never leave the server — every removed review (`reviews.deleted_at IS NOT NULL`) has its guest text (`history_rollups.REVIEW_GUEST_TEXT`) blanked in the copy, the same night it is removed; the server itself erases it after 30 days (*Retention*).
- Any other column whose name matches `offsite_backup.CREDENTIAL_NAME` (token, secret, api key, password, passcode, private key, credential) is scrubbed anyway and reported in `backup_runs.detail_json`; `tests/test_fix_d_backup.py` fails until it is classified. The copy is `VACUUM`ed so the old pages go too.

## Conventions that apply across this schema

- **Additive-only migrations.** A new column is added via a guarded `ALTER TABLE` in `init_db()`'s list or `ensure_columns()`'s (both run at boot; 57 columns live only in the latter), never a destructive rewrite, so old rows keep working and a rollback of the code doesn't orphan data.
- **`restaurant_id` scopes every tenant table**, and the newer tables declare `NOT NULL REFERENCES restaurants(id)`. About thirty older tables (`alert_log`, `schedule_history`, `email_log`, `sessions`, `login_history`, `ai_usage`, `push_deliveries`, …) carry the column without the FK clause, so `PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON` does not protect them; the `WHERE ... AND restaurant_id=?` convention does. New tables declare the FK.
- **A restaurant's read of a growing table searches an index that leads with `restaurant_id`** (scheduler audit 10/10/26, SCALE-14/SCALE-30). The retention indexes lead with the prune column (`created_at`), so a per-restaurant read that also filtered by time rode them and scanned every restaurant's rows — `_WeekData`'s version query read the whole `schedule_versions` table on every learner call. `tests/test_query_plans.py` runs every literal `SELECT … restaurant_id=? …` in the app through `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` on the boot schema and fails on a SCAN of any retention-registry ledger or other growing table (`GROWING` there). Added 10/10/26: `idx_schedule_versions_rid_created` `(restaurant_id, created_at)`, `idx_login_history_rid`, `idx_users_restaurant`, `idx_webhook_deliveries_rid`, `idx_webhooks_rid`, `idx_ask_actions_rid`, `idx_alert_holds_rid`, `idx_recipe_drafts_rid`, `idx_schedule_shares_rid`, `idx_guest_newsletters_rid`, `idx_gor_rid`, `idx_async_jobs_rid`, `idx_notification_opens_alert` `(alert_log_id, user_id)`, and the small per-restaurant tables' (`idx_permission_grants_rid`, `idx_login_prefs_rid`, `idx_trusted_devices_rid`, `idx_two_fa_backup_codes_rid`, `idx_docusign_envelopes_rid`, `idx_task_lines_rid`).
- **Append-only tables stay append-only** (`login_history`, `ask_cavnar_actions`, `alert_log` — but for `notify.withdraw_notification`'s unopened row for a push that reached no device (*`alert_log`*) — `capability_changes`, and since 9/29/26 `admin_events`, `support_notes`, `billing_status_history`, `operator_alerts`, and from the memory round `change_log` and `intel_pattern_history`, whose trigger refuses an UPDATE) — they are audit trails; a "current state" view is always computed by querying the latest row, not by mutating history in place. Append-only is not unbounded: each has a retention window or a reason it has none (*Retention*).
- **Timestamps are UTC** in SQLite's `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` form unless a column says otherwise (a restaurant's local business date, a `local_day`). `email_log.sent_at` was the exception until 9/29/26 and was migrated.
- **JSON-in-a-column** (`*_json` fields on `restaurants`, `summary_json` on `schedule_history`) is used deliberately for structured settings that don't need their own table and are always read/written as a whole blob by one piece of code — not for anything queried by its internal fields.

## Intelligence engine

`restaurants.category` (the cohort; inferred when unset), `intel_features`
(one row per restaurant-week of ratios, rates and counts — the only table
cross-restaurant learning reads; no dollars, names or people. Since 9/29/26
`version` is the definition the row was computed under: readers take only
rows of the current `intelligence.features.FEATURES_VERSION` (3), NULL = a
row from before versioning, never read; the trigger `trg_intel_features_version`,
re-created each boot from the constant, stamps a row inserted without one;
`backfilled` marks a row computed for a PAST week from the raw tables by the
`intelligence_features_backfill` job rather than by that week's nightly pass;
`idx_intel_features_version` `(restaurant_id, version, week)`),
`intel_rec_events` (every recommendation's answered / measured events,
derived from rec_ledger's `rec_events` — every surface, read forward from
`job_cursors['intelligence_feedback_ledger']`, counting only answers to an
episode some surface showed — and, for what predates the ledger,
`home_dismissals`, `ask_cavnar_actions` (keyed `ask:<proposal id>`) and
`delayed_actions` forward from their own cursors (`intelligence_feedback_home`,
`_ask`, `_auto` — a low-water mark that never passes a pending row) and
`recommendation_outcomes` whole (its rows change after they are written),
each verdict read through `rec_learning.learned_verdict` (disowned or
conditions-changed → unknown, faded/reversed → no_clear_change); one
connection and one commit per pass; the one-off repair of old derived rows
runs once (`intelligence_feedback_repair:v2`). A `measured` row is one
per EPISODE, keyed `<source_key>#o<tracker id>` (a later tracker on the same
key is its own result, never an in-place overwrite), and on one number only
the earliest of overlapping after-windows carries its verdict (the others
are `unknown`); rows written under the bare key were dropped once
(`intelligence_feedback_repair:episodes_v1`) and re-derived. Since the
memory audit (9/29/26) an ANSWER is one row per episode too, keyed
`<key>#e<rec_id>` (`feedback.EPISODE_KEY_SEP`; an answer older than the
ledger, with no episode, keeps the bare key) — the bare-key row used to keep
only the first answer a key ever had; the bare-key answer rows were dropped
once where the ledger still holds the answer
(`intelligence_feedback_repair:per_episode_v1`) and re-derived. UNIQUE
(restaurant, key, action) therefore holds one row per answer per episode,
whichever path reaches it; actions
include `snoozed`, `accepted`, `implemented` and `ignored`; scoring puts
each recommendation in ONE bucket, taken > declined > hidden > ignored.
Columns added by the memory audit (boot ALTERs in `init_db`): `rec_id` (the
episode), `partition_key` (the restaurant's confirmed peer partition for the
row's metric family — the prior ladder's second rung, beside `cohort`),
`review_derived` (the row rests on review data), `google_data` (a
review-derived row of a restaurant whose reviews come through the owner's
Google connection: Google user data, never read by a pooled reader —
`intelligence.provenance`) and `trust_version` (the meaning of the %
`confidence_at` was read from); and `authority` (memory re-audit 9/29/26,
PLATFORM-4: whose answer the row carries — only the restaurant's own answers
are filed, `rec_ledger.counts_for_restaurant`, so 'principal', a delegate's
accept or Done, or NULL for a system answer); labels (`cohort`, `partition_key`,
`google_data`) are re-stamped whole whenever a restaurant's change, NULL
included; `idx_intel_rec_events_created` serves the bounded confidence fill),
`intel_patterns` (discovered
patterns with n, effect, p, q, confidence, status active|retired),
`intel_pattern_history` (9/29/26 — what the platform believed, week by week:
one row per ISO `week`, pattern `key` and `status` reached (UNIQUE), with
`cohort`, `hypothesis`, `n_with` / `n_without`, `effect` / `effect_unit`,
`cohen_d`, `p_value`, `q_value`, `prospective`; written once, never
re-figured — the trigger `intel_pattern_history_append_only` refuses an
UPDATE; aggregates only, never an id; kept forever),
`intel_benchmarks` (cohort × metric × week percentiles, n ≥ 5, plus `vals_json` — the member values, sorted and unlabelled, never selected into a payload, so the band shown to a member leaves its own row out; shown only at ≥ 8 others, coarse-rounded, ≤ 8 weeks old — `intelligence.benchmarks.published`),
`intel_confidence_log` (weekly acceptance and success by kind; since
9/29/26 per `trust_version` — only support-score snapshots of one meaning
are averaged — with `orgs`, the organisations behind each figure). Invariant:
no row in `intel_patterns`, `intel_benchmarks` or `intel_confidence_log`
describes fewer than `privacy.MIN_COHORT` restaurants.

Benchmarking audit (9/24/26, workstream P), all DDL at boot:
- `restaurants` gains the owner-confirmed profile — `service_model`
  (counter | full_service | bar_led | daytime), `concept` (a
  `categories.TAXONOMY` value), `bar_led`, `ownership` (independent |
  franchise | corporate), `opened_year`, `profile_source` (set | inferred),
  `profile_confirmed_at` — plus `exclude_from_learning` (a test or internal
  account: out of every cross-restaurant figure), `labor_target_source` /
  `food_cost_target_source` (set | seeded | default; NULL on an old row reads
  "default" at the 30% default, else "set"; a seed that no longer qualifies
  is reset to the default value and source at boot by
  `models.backfill_seeded_targets`), `hourly_rate_source` (set | NULL: an
  owner-entered rate is theirs even at exactly $26, so
  `thresholds.labor_cost_basis` reads `owner_blended`) and `google_types` /
  `google_price_level` (the restaurant's own Google listing). Each has the
  four touch points (dataclass, `ensure_columns`, the `update_restaurant`
  whitelist, `get_restaurant`). `update_restaurant` stamps a target (or the
  hourly rate) it is handed without a source as `set` only when the value
  CHANGED — the admin settings form passes the source itself for a field
  the admin touched, so an explicit save of the default counts — and records a
  `profile_changed` activity event with the old and new values of every
  profile field that changed.
- `intel_benchmarks` gains `orgs`, `max_org_share` and `members_json`
  (`[[value, org hash], …]`, server-side only; the hash an HMAC under
  `app_secrets` `intel_org_hash:vN`; the list NULLed once the band is past
  `benchmarks.MAX_BAND_AGE_WEEKS`, and a deleted restaurant alone in its
  organisation removed from every list — memory re-audit 9/29/26); the cohort is a peer
  partition key (`sm:…`, see the ladder below) or `platform` (behaviour metrics only); a week's
  row is written once and frozen (a row from before `members_json` is
  replaced, and is never shown). Invariant: no stored band rests on fewer
  than `privacy.MIN_ORGS` organisations.
- `intel_peer_assignments` (restaurant × week × family: rung, partition key,
  peer-set hash — never ids — n, orgs, profile source and confirmation date,
  drift; UNIQUE per restaurant, week and family). Fix round #44: `rung` is
  what the restaurant would be SHOWN (`peers` only when a band on its
  ladder passes `benchmarks.publish_row` for a metric it measures), and
  n / orgs / hash are that band's; `partition_key` stays the confirmed
  partition. `drift` is read for CONSECUTIVE ISO weeks and only ever becomes
  a question to the owner — it never moves the partition (#38).
- `intel_benchmarks.cohort` may also be a ladder rung (fix round #34/#36):
  the partition narrowed by `|t<ticket band>`, `|v<volume band>`,
  `|u<urbanity band>`, or the service model alone; `…|mixed` and `…|other`
  keys are never written or shown (#22).
- `intel_cohort_series` (cohort × metric × week: n, the balanced-panel
  median, n_joined, n_left; UNIQUE per cohort, metric and week). Fix round
  #41: a point is a Harrell–Davis median over ≥ 8 eligible restaurants from
  ≥ 5 organisations.

Benchmarking audit (9/24/26, workstream D), all DDL in `init_db`:
- `intel_rec_events` gains `metric`, `effect_pct`, `effect_z` (signed so
  positive means better), `baseline_kind`, `after_end`, `tags_json` — filled
  by `feedback.sync` from `recommendation_outcomes` and the episode's
  `rec_instances.tags`, only on a `measured` row whose result the learning
  counts (cleared otherwise); index on `event_at` (the confidence log reads
  365 days).
- `intel_dna` (restaurant_id, week, `dims_json` `{dim: {raw, z, n, basis,
  norm}}`, `coverage`, `version`, computed_at; UNIQUE(restaurant_id, week)) —
  Restaurant DNA; ratios, rates, shares and bands only, never dollars; a
  dimension below its minimum data has raw NULL and says what it needs.
  `raw` is the truth: the stored `z`/`norm` record the night, and every
  distance re-normalises both rows from `raw` under one norm set. Similarity,
  platform norms and prediction read only rows at the current `version`
  (`dna.DNA_VERSION` = 2 from 9/24/26: S6 is the confirmed service model, S7
  the concept, labor % blank on the assumed wage).
- `intel_benchmark_facts` (restaurant_id, metric, week, `payload_json`,
  `available`, computed_at; UNIQUE(restaurant_id, metric, week)) — the
  engine's comparisons materialised nightly; the viewer-dependent `location`
  kind is never stored; rows older than 8 weeks are pruned by the pass. Each
  payload carries `version` and `inputs_key` (a hash of the settings it was
  computed from); `engine.payload_for` serves a row only while both match
  and it is under 36 hours old.
- `intel_effects` (restaurant_id, rec_kind, metric, week, `available`,
  `payload_json`, computed_at; UNIQUE(restaurant_id, rec_kind, metric,
  week)) — the weekly neighbour prediction fact; unavailable below its
  floors.
